Langlands duality on the Beilinson-Drinfeld Grassmannian
Justin Campbell and Sam Raskin

Abstract
. We calculate various categories of equivariant sheaves on the Beilinson-Drinfeld Grassmannian in Langlands dual terms. For one, we obtain the factorizable derived geometric Satake theorem. More generally, we calculate the categorical analogue of unramified vectors in the Jacquet module of sheaves on the Grassmannian. In all cases, our spectral categories involve factorization modules for factorization algebras related to the Langlands dual group.



1.
Introduction

1.1.
What is this paper about?

Our main objectives in this paper are as follows:

•

Realize a strategy of Gaitsgory-Lurie giving
a natural construction of the derived geometric Satake equivalence

[
BF08
]
(cf. the first footnote in

loc. cit
.).

•

Extend derived geometric Satake from the usual affine Grassmannian
to the Beilinson-Drinfeld (or factorizable) affine Grassmannian.

•

Extend the Arkhipov-Bezrkuvaknikov-Ginzburg equivalence

[
ABG04
]
to the Beilinson-Drinfeld affine Grassmannian.

In fact, we unify the latter two subjects with a mutual generalization to parabolic
subgroups
P
of
G
; for
P=G
we obtain factorizable derived Satake,
while for
P=B
we obtain factorizable Arkhipov-Bezrukavnikov-Ginzburg.

These results have been widely anticipated. In particular, Gaitsgory
has spoken and written about our main results for some time,
cf.
[
Gai15
]
§4.7 and §6.6.3,

[
Gai11
]
§10.3.3,

[
ABG+14
]
Talk V.2 Conjecture 1,

[
ABC+18
]
Program §4.1.1, and
[
AG15
]

Remark 12.6.7 to name a few.

However, the questions we consider have previously resisted
precise formulations. In the above sources, one finds
no definitions of the spectral (or
Langlands dual
) sides
of the equivalences under consideration. So although our results
have been anticipated, we can find no precisely formulated
conjecture in the literature that
we can point to and say “We proved
this
conjecture!”
In large part, our task in this paper is to precisely formulate
the problem and to do so in a way that is amenable to its solution.

Accordingly, below we will give an extended discussion
of our point of view on the spectral side.

1.2.
Applications

Derived Satake at a point is closely related to subtle issues in the
global geometric Langlands program; see the above references as
well as
[
FR23
]
and
[
FR22
]
.
Beraldo
[
Ber19
]
has given a lovely interpretation of the
meaning of full factorizable Satake, for which we refer to
loc. cit
.

For a long time (cf.
[
Gai11
]
and
[
Gai15
]
),
it has been anticipated that our results in the parabolic case are required
to understand global geometric Langlands near reducible local systems.
Forthcoming work
[
CGR23
]
of Chen-Gaitsgory-R. uses our present work
in completely settling exactly this problem.

1.3.
The Gaitsgory-Lurie heuristic (spherical case)

We now recall the idea of Gaitsgory and Lurie, in part because it was
never written down (as far as we are aware).
For purposes of an introduction, we give ourselves the liberty
to be lax with our homological algebra and notation around
factorization structures.

We let
\Gr_{G}
denote the affine Grassmannian, which is the quotient

\mathfrak{L}G/\mathfrak{L}^{+}G
of the algebraic loop group of
G
by the
group of jets into
G
. Beilinson-Drinfeld viewed
\Gr_{G}
as a factorization space
fibered over the Ran space of finite subsets of the curve
X
. The geometric side of derived Satake is the
category
\mathsf{Sph}_{G}:=\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G}
of D-modules on the
affine Grassmannian
1
1

1

In the main body of this text, the symbol
\mathsf{Sph}_{G}
is used for a slightly modified version of
\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G}
, which we will not need in this introduction.
.

Gaitsgory-Lurie observed that
\mathsf{Sph}_{G}
is a
factorization monoidal category

because of the Beilinson-Drinfeld factorization structure on the affine Grassmannian, and as such,
it acts on the factorization category
\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}

of Whittaker D-modules on the affine Grassmannian.

By a version of the geometric Casselman-Shalika formula of

[
FGV01
]
, it is known that

(1.3.1)

\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}\simeq\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})

as factorization categories
(see Theorem
6.5.1
below).

We use the following standard heuristics:
factorization categories are analogous to
\mathbb{E}_{2}
-categories
(also known as braided monoidal categories), while factorization monoidal
categories are analogous to
\mathbb{E}_{3}
-categories. These heuristics
are made somewhat precise in the literature, see

[
Lur17a
]
§5.5 and notes from the
talk by Nick Rozenblyum in
[
ABC+18
]
. See also

[
Noc23
]
, where the
\mathbb{E}_{3}
-structure on
\mathsf{Sph}_{G}
is made
completely rigorous.

Now recall that for an
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-category
\mathcal{C}
, there is
a certain category
Z_{\mathbb{E}_{n}}(\mathcal{C})
called its

\mathbb{E}_{n}
-center
, which is the universal

\mathbb{E}_{n+1}
-category acting on
\mathcal{C}
as an
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-category
in a suitable sense – we refer to
[
Lur17a
]
§5.3 for definitions
and details. Explicitly, as a monoidal category,
one can calculate
Z_{\mathbb{E}_{n}}(\mathcal{C})
as

\mathsf{End}_{\mathcal{C}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{\mathbb{E}_{n}}}(\mathcal{C})
, the category of endofunctors
of
\mathcal{C}
as an
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-module category over itself.

Following this heuristic, we imagine that
\mathsf{Sph}_{G}
is an
\mathbb{E}_{3}
-category (which we know it literally is, cf.
[
Noc23
]
)
acting on the
\mathbb{E}_{2}
-category
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})
via the equivalence (
1.3.1
).
Therefore, heuristically, we obtain a canonical functor of
\mathbb{E}_{3}
-categories

(1.3.2)

\mathsf{Sph}_{G}\longrightarrow Z_{\mathbb{E}_{2}}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})).

On the other hand, it is well-known that:

Z_{\mathbb{E}_{2}}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G}))=Z_{\mathbb{E}_{2}}(\mathsf{QCoh}(\mathbb{B}\check{G}))=\mathsf{QCoh}((\mathbb{B}\check{G})^{\mathbb{S}^{2}})

(cf.
[
BZFN10
]
Corollary 5.12). Here the last term

(\mathbb{B}\check{G})^{\mathbb{S}^{2}}
is the derived stack parametrizing maps
from the homotopy type of the
2
-sphere to the stack
\mathbb{B}\check{G}
.
Writing
\mathbb{S}^{2}
as the suspension of
\mathbb{S}^{1}
, this stack may be calculated
more explicitly as

(\mathbb{B}\check{G})^{\mathbb{S}^{2}}=\mathbb{B}\check{G}\underset{(\mathbb{B}\check{G})^{\mathbb{S}^{1}}}{\times}\mathbb{B}\check{G}=\mathbb{B}\check{G}\underset{(\check{G}\overset{\operatorname{ad}}{/}\check{G})}{\times}\mathbb{B}\check{G}=(\Omega_{1}\check{G})/\check{G}

where

\Omega_{1}\check{G}:=\Spec(k)\underset{\check{G}}{\times}\Spec(k)

is the DG group of automorphisms of the identity in
\check{G}
.
Using logarithms, one finds that

\Omega_{1}\check{G}=\Omega_{0}\check{\mathfrak{g}}=\Spec(\Sym(\check{\mathfrak{g}}[1])),

so we can interpret the functor
(
1.3.2
) as a functor

\mathsf{Sph}_{G}\longrightarrow\Sym(\check{\mathfrak{g}}[1]))\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})).

The derived Satake theorem then says that this functor is

almost
an equivalence. More precisely, the derived Satake theorem of

[
BF08
]
says that this is true
up to
renormalization
– we should use the
renormalized Satake category

and the category
\mathsf{IndCoh}((\mathbb{B}\check{G})^{\mathbb{S}^{2}})
.

To summarize, their heuristic says to follow the recipe:

(1)

Use the factorization action of
\mathsf{Sph}_{G}
on
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})

to obtain a functor to a suitable analogue of the
\mathbb{E}_{2}
-center
of
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})
.

(2)

Settle the homological algebra issues to obtain a functor that
is plausibly an equivalence.

(3)

Perform an explicit calculation to prove that the functor so
obtained is an equivalence.

Remark 1.3.1
.

In
[
BF08
]
, one finds that many of the difficulties
have to do with constructing the functor. The Gaitsgory-Lurie idea is
of a markedly different nature than what Bezrukavnikov-Finkelberg considered.

1.4.
Centers and
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-modules

We will reinterpret centers (and more generally: centralizers)
in terms of modules over rings (or near enough).
This material is not necessary, but we find it plays
an important motivating role for our main results.

Suppose that
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
are symmetric monoidal categories.

Given an
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-monoidal functor
F:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
, its
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-centralizer

Z_{\mathbb{E}_{n}}(F)

is the category

\mathsf{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{\mathbb{E}_{n}}}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D}),

i.e., the

\mathbb{E}_{n}
-Hochschild homology of
\mathcal{D}
as an
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-module
category for
\mathcal{C}
. Its key property is that an
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-map

\mathcal{E}\to Z_{\mathbb{E}_{n}}(F)
is equivalent to an
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-map

\mathcal{C}\otimes\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{D}
whose composition

\mathcal{C}\xrightarrow{\id_{\mathcal{C}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}}}\mathcal{C}\otimes\mathcal{E}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}

is
F
as an
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-functor.
(We recover the
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-center of
\mathcal{C}

by taking the centralizer of
\id_{\mathcal{C}}
.)

We now give an alternative to

\mathbb{E}_{n}
-centralizers (that coincides in some cases).
First, one
may form
\mathsf{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})
, the category of continuous
DG functors from
\mathcal{C}
to
\mathcal{D}
.
Using Day convolution, this category is naturally symmetric monoidal as well.

We remind the reader that
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-algebras in

\mathsf{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})
are the same as (left) lax
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-monoidal
(continuous DG) functors from
\mathcal{C}
to
\mathcal{D}
.
A variant: given
F:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
a lax
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-monoidal
functor, thought of as an
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-algebra in

\mathsf{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})
, an
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-module for

F
in
\mathsf{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})
is a functor

G:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
of lax
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-module categories
for
\mathcal{C}
. (In fact, this variant can be put on equal footing
with its predecessor using general colored operads.)

Now suppose that
\mathcal{C}
is
rigid
(symmetric) monoidal.
Then recall that a
lax
linear morphism of

\mathcal{C}
-module categories is automatically linear –
this is standard for left module categories, but holds
just the same for
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-module categories. Therefore,
in the above discussion, we have

Z_{\mathbb{E}_{n}}(F):=\mathsf{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{\mathbb{E}_{n}}}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})=\mathsf{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{\mathbb{E}_{n}}^{\operatorname{lax}}}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})

where
\mathcal{C}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{\mathbb{E}_{n}}^{\operatorname{lax}}
is
the category of
lax

\mathbb{E}_{n}
-module categories
for
\mathcal{C}
. In particular, we see that when
\mathcal{C}
is
rigid, we can extend the definition of

\mathbb{E}_{n}
-centralizer to allow
F
to be
merely lax
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-monoidal. Moreover, using the preceding
paragraph, we can think of
Z_{\mathbb{E}_{n}}(F)
as

\mathbb{E}_{n}
-modules for
F
in the symmetric monoidal category

\mathsf{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})
.

We now make one additional manipulation, continuing to
assume
\mathcal{C}
is rigid. Then recall that
\mathcal{C}
is canonically
self-dual as a DG category. In particular, we obtain

\mathsf{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})\simeq\mathcal{C}^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{D}\simeq\mathcal{C}\otimes\mathcal{D}

where one easily finds
2
2

2

For completeness,
we supply the argument. Giving a lax symmetric monoidal functor

\mathcal{E}\to\mathsf{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})
is the same as giving a lax
symmetric monoidal functor
\mathcal{C}\otimes\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{D}
. For

\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{C}\otimes\mathcal{D}
, we have the lax symmetric monoidal
functor

\mathcal{C}\otimes\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{C}\otimes\mathcal{C}\otimes\mathcal{D}\xrightarrow{m\otimes\operatorname{id}}\mathcal{C}\otimes\mathcal{D}\xrightarrow{\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{C}},-)\otimes\operatorname{id}}\mathcal{D}

(the key point being that
\mathds{1}
is compact and

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathds{1},-):\mathcal{C}\to\Vect

is lax symmetric monoidal). This gives rise to the lax symmetric
monoidal functor
\mathcal{C}\otimes\mathcal{D}\to\mathsf{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})
.
By definition, this is the composite equivalence from before.
One can check that this lax symmetric monoidal equivalence is
actually symmetric monoidal.

that the composite
equivalence is symmetric monoidal.

To summarize, we obtain the following under the
rigidity assumption on
\mathcal{C}
. Below, for
F:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}

(possibly lax)
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-monoidal, we let
\mathcal{K}_{F}\in\mathcal{C}\otimes\mathcal{D}

be the corresponding object.

(1)

The
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-center of
\mathcal{C}
is the category of
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-modules
for
\mathcal{K}_{\id_{\mathcal{C}}}\in\mathbb{E}_{n}\text{\textendash}{\mathsf{alg}}(\mathcal{C}\otimes\mathcal{D})
.

(2)

More generally, for
F:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
lax
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-linear,
the
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-centralizer of
F
is the category of
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-modules
for
\mathcal{K}_{F}\in\mathbb{E}_{n}\text{\textendash}{\mathsf{alg}}(\mathcal{C}\otimes\mathcal{D})
.

In particular, we have expressed
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-centers and centralizers
in terms of
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-modules.

Example 1.4.1
.

For
\mathcal{C}=\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})
, we find that
Z_{\mathbb{E}_{2}}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G}))

is the category of
\mathbb{E}_{2}
-modules for the regular representation

\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}}\in\mathsf{ComAlg}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G}\times\check{G}))
.

In §
1.6
below, we will extend this discussion
to the setting where there is a parabolic subgroup; we remark
that there, we actually do consider
\mathbb{E}_{2}
-modules
for lax monoidal functors and use centralizers rather than centers.

Remark 1.4.2
.

A technical remark: in
[
Ras21a
]
§6, it was observed that
factorization categories attached to
rigid
symmetric monoidal
categories have much more favorable properties than general
factorization categories. We consider the use of rigidity
in the above discussion to be related to this observation, but
here working purely in the
\mathbb{E}_{2}
-setting.

1.5.
Chiral setting

Following standard analogies, we consider chiral (or factorization)
algebras as de Rham analogues of
\mathbb{E}_{2}
-algebras (which are Betti
objects), and similarly for
\mathbb{E}_{2}
-modules.

Recall from
[
Ras21a
]
that any symmetric monoidal category naturally gives rise to a factorization
category (analogous to thinking of an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-category as an
\mathbb{E}_{2}
-category), and
any commutative algebra in a symmetric monoidal category gives rise to a factorization algebra
in the associated factorization category (analogous to thinking of an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra as an
\mathbb{E}_{2}
-algebra).

We can then form the category of factorization modules

\mathsf{Sph}^{\spec,\naive}:=\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G}\times\check{G})).

In §
2
and §
5
, we
explain how to perform
homological algebra corrections to
\mathsf{Sph}^{\spec,\naive}

(analogous to replacing
\mathsf{QCoh}
by
\mathsf{IndCoh}
) to
obtain a factorization category
\mathsf{Sph}_{\check{G}}^{\spec}
.

Following Gaitsgory, we refer in this paper to this
process of correcting homological defects as
renormalization
.
The specific technical issues we face here are novel
and significant. Their resolution
occupies a large portion of this work.

In §
6
, we explain how the Gaitsgory-Lurie paradigm
plays out in the chiral setting. Roughly speaking, their strategy
for constructing the derived Satake functor
goes through without hiccups in our
setting. The factorizable derived Satake equivalence itself is stated as
Theorem
6.6.1
.

Our proof that the functor is an equivalence takes up §
9
.
We do this by reducing to the case that
G=T
is a torus; in §
7
we verify this case directly.
Our process of reduction to the torus uses the study of Jacquet functors from
[
Ras21a
]
-
[
Ras16
]
.

1.6.
The Gaitsgory-Lurie heuristic (parabolic case)

As far as we are aware, Gaitsgory and Lurie did not observe
that a similar strategy also applies in the presence of a parabolic

P
of
G
.

Let
N_{P}
denote the unipotent radical of
P
and let
M=P/N_{P}
. On
the geometric side, we consider the
factorization category

\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M}

where on the right hand side, we have formed the
coinvariant
category
, cf.
[
Ras16
]
. By
[
Che20
]
, we note
that this category can also be thought of as

\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})^{\mathfrak{L}N_{P^{-}}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M}

i.e.,
invariants
for
the
opposite
parabolic.

On the spectral side, there are
two
natural candidates
to consider, attached to two lax symmetric monoidal
functors

\Chev_{\Omega},\Chev_{\Upsilon}:\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M}).

Both functors come by first restricting a
representation from
\check{G}
to
\check{P}
and then applying
a functor
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{P})\to\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M})
.

For
\Chev_{\Omega}
, the relevant
functor
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{P})\to\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M})

is (derived) invariants for
\check{N}_{\check{P}}
.

The functor
\Chev_{\Upsilon}
is more complicated to define.
Consider
\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{\check{P}}
as a Lie algebra
in
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M})
. We consider

Z_{\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M}),\mathbb{E}_{1}}(\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{\check{P}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M})),

i.e., the
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-center (alias: Drinfeld
center)
in the sense of
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M})
-linear monoidal categories.
Because
\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{\check{P}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M}))

is itself symmetric monoidal, there is a natural functor

\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{\check{P}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M}))\longrightarrow Z_{\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M}),\mathbb{E}_{1}}(\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{\check{P}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M})).

We remark that one may identify

Z_{\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M}),\mathbb{E}_{1}}(\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{\check{P}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M}))
more explicitly with

(\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{\check{P}}\otimes\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(\mathbb{S}^{1}))\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M}))=U_{\mathbb{E}_{2}}(\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{\check{P}})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{\mathbb{E}_{2}}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M})).

(We highlight these later expressions are closer to those used in

[
Ras21a
]
-
[
Ras16
]
and in the body of this paper, although they are
perhaps less conceptual.)
Then
\Chev_{\Upsilon}
comes by composing the forgetful functor

\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\to\mathsf{Rep}(\check{P})
with the composite functor

\mathsf{Rep}(\check{P})\subset\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{\check{P}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M}))\longrightarrow Z_{\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M}),\mathbb{E}_{1}}(\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{\check{P}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M}))\xrightarrow{\oblv}\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{\check{P}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M}))\xrightarrow{\oblv}\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M}).

Up to issues of completion, we expect the
\mathbb{E}_{2}
-centralizers

Z_{\mathbb{E}_{2}}(\Chev_{\Omega})
and
Z_{\mathbb{E}_{2}}(\Chev_{\Upsilon})

of these functors to be “approximately” (i.e., up to issues
of completion) equivalent
by a Koszul duality procedure.

Although
\Chev_{\Omega}
is easier to define and arguably
is the better object, we use (the de Rham analogue of)

\Chev_{\Upsilon}
. There are several reasons for this, which
we take a moment to record:

•

One should think that
\Chev_{\Upsilon}
is adapted to

\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M
-coinvariants and
\Chev_{\Omega}
is adapted to invariants.
One may use either invariants or coinvariants due to
[
Che20
]
,
so we are similarly free to use either
\Chev_{\Upsilon}
or

\Chev_{\Omega}
.

•

The paper
[
Ras16
]
uses
\Chev_{\Upsilon}
, which
makes our references somewhat easier.

However, we remark that in the
quantum

setting, more recent papers in the subject, such as

[
CF21a
]
,
[
Yan21
]
, and
[
GL19
]

prefer
\Chev_{\Omega}
.

•

When working factorizably (as opposed to at a single point),
it appears to be much more technically convenient to use

\Chev_{\Upsilon}
rather than
\Chev_{\Omega}
. This was
implicit in
[
Ras16
]
, and holds again in our present work,
particularly as concerns renormalization, ULA properties,
and the use of t-structures.

With those remarks out of the way, we note that there is
a pairing

\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M}\otimes\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}\longrightarrow(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}

by convolution. One can further
!
-restrict to:

(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}(P\times N_{M}^{-})_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}=\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{M})^{\mathfrak{L}N_{M}^{-},\psi}

for
N_{M}^{-}:=N^{-}\cap M
. Applying the equivalence (
1.3.1
) for

G
and
M
, we can rewrite the resulting functor as a pairing

\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M}\otimes\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M}).

By
[
Ras16
]
Theorem 4.15.1, the induced functor

\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M})

obtained by pairing with the unit object
in
\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M}
is the functor

\Chev_{\Upsilon}
(or rather, its de Rham counterpart).
If we imagined we were in the
\mathbb{E}_{2}
-setting, this would
yield a functor

\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M}\longrightarrow Z(\Chev_{\Upsilon})

that ought to be an equivalence up to issues of
renormalization.

Using the essentially the same discussion as in
§
1.5
, we formulate and prove a purely
de Rham version of
exactly this assertion, Theorem
6.12.3
. As we discuss in the paper,
on underlying categories (i.e., forgetting factorization structures),
we recover (by different means) the main theorem of
[
ABG04
]
.

Remark 1.6.1
.

In truth, the references to
[
Ras16
]
above are for

P=B
. This is ultimately due to a choice in
[
BG08
]

to work only with the Borel. Work in progress by
Færgeman-Hayash is extending
[
BG08
]
to the
case of general parabolics. As such, our results for general parabolics
(besides
B
and
G
) are conditional on their work.

1.7.
Notation and conventions

We work over a field
k
of characteristic zero. We also assume that
k
is algebraically closed, which is not strictly necessary but simplifies some notations.

Denote by
X
a fixed smooth and connected (but not necessarily proper) curve over
k
.

We fix a reductive group
G
over
k
and a Borel subgroup
B\subset G
. Let
N\subset B
denote the unipotent radical of
B
and
T=B/N
the Cartan. Choose a splitting
T\to B
, so that we can consider the opposite Borel
B^{-}
, with unipotent radical
N^{-}
. We will denote by
P
a standard parabolic, with unipotent radical
N_{P}
, Levi subgroup
M
, opposite parabolic
P^{-}
, etc. The coweight lattice will be denoted by
\Lambda=\Hom(\mathbb{G}_{m},T)
.

The Langlands dual group of
G
(over
k
) will be denoted by
\check{G}
, with corresponding standard parabolics
\check{P}
, etc.

We freely use the language of higher category theory as developed in
[
Lur17b
]
and
[
Lur17a
]
, and usefully summarized in
[
GR19
]
Chapter 1. We will use the term
category
to mean
(\infty,1)
-category
, and
DG category
to mean
presentable stable

k
-linear category
. DG categories assemble into a category
\mathsf{DGCat}
, which is symmetric monoidal with respect to the Lurie tensor product. The unit for this tensor product is the DG category
\Vect
of (complexes of)
k
-vector spaces.

We work in the setting of derived algebraic geometry, i.e. our test objects are understood to be affine DG schemes over
k
. For any prestack
Y
over
k
, we denote by
\mathsf{QCoh}(Y)
the DG category of quasicoherent sheaves on
Y
. If
Y
is locally almost of finite type, we can also consider the DG category
\mathsf{IndCoh}(Y)
of ind-coherent sheaves, whose theory is developed in
[
Gai13
]
and
[
GR19
]
. We will denote the DG category of D-modules on such a prestack by
\mathsf{D}(Y)
.

We will make use of the theory of D-modules on certain infinite-dimensional spaces such as loop groups, which has been developed in
[
Ras15b
]
and
[
Ber17
]
. We use the
\mathsf{D}^{*}
version of this theory by default.

Finally, for a (possibly lax) prestack
Y
, we will denote by
\mathsf{ShvCat}(Y)
the category of sheaves of categories on
Y
. We refer the reader to
[
Gai14
]
for more details on this notion, or
[
Ras15a
]
for the lax setting.

1.8.
Acknowledgements

As the above introduction should make clear, this paper develops
an old idea of Dennis Gaitsgory and Jacob Lurie.

We warmly thank Dennis Gaitsgory for the boundless generosity with which he
shares his ideas.
We learned of his work with Lurie through conversations with him, but also
learned the major technical tools used in this paper from him.

In addition, we thank Dima Arinkin, David Ben-Zvi,
Dario Beraldo, Lin Chen, Gurbir Dhillon, Kevin Lin, Sergey Lysenko, and Nick Rozenblyum
for enlightening conversations related to this material.

Sloan Research Fellowship.

2.
Renormalizing crystals of categories

Below
S
will denote a smooth quasicompact scheme over
k
unless otherwise specified. This section lays the technical foundations for renormalization in the presence of a crystal structure over
S
, i.e. a
\mathsf{D}(S)
-module structure. Proposition
2.7.1
gives sufficient conditions under which the canonical renormalization of a
\mathsf{D}(S)
-module category with compatible t-structure admits a natural crystal structure. Definition
2.8.1
formulates what it means for a crystal of categories with compatible t-structure to be “almost ULA generated,” and Proposition
2.8.2
says that the canonical renormalization of such a category is ULA generated.

2.1.

The forgetful functor

\oblv_{S}:\mathsf{D}(S)\longrightarrow\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)

is symmetric monoidal, and hence can be viewed as a morphism of
\mathsf{D}(S)
-modules. Its left adjoint

\ind_{S}:\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(S)

therefore admits an oplax
\mathsf{D}(S)
-linear structure, which is easily seen to be strict. Given a
\mathsf{D}(S)
-module category
\mathcal{C}
, we therefore obtain adjoint functors

(2.1.1)

\ind_{\mathcal{C}}:\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\underset{\mathsf{D}(S)}{\otimes}\mathcal{C}\rightleftarrows\mathcal{C}:\oblv_{\mathcal{C}}.

Moreover, for any
\mathsf{D}(S)
-linear functor
F:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
we have commutative squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes_{\mathsf{D}(S)}\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes_{\mathsf{D}(S)}\mathcal{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id\otimes F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oblv_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oblv_{\mathcal{D}}}

and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes_{\mathsf{D}(S)}\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes_{\mathsf{D}(S)}\mathcal{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id\otimes F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ind_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ind_{\mathcal{D}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}

Proposition 2.1.1
.

The adjunction (
2.1.1
) is both monadic and comonadic.

Proof.

For the monadicity it suffices to show that
\oblv_{\mathcal{C}}
is conservative, which is Lemma B.2.1 in
[
Ras21a
]
.

We claim that the monad
\oblv_{\mathcal{C}}\circ\ind_{\mathcal{C}}
is
effective
in the sense of
[
Ras15c
]
Definition 6.9.1, which will imply comonadicity of the adjunction by Remark 6.9.2 of
loc. cit.
The convolution action of the infinitesimal groupoid

\widehat{(S^{2})}_{\Delta}\cong S\underset{S_{\dR}}{\times}S

on
S
gives rise to an action of
\mathsf{IndCoh}(\widehat{(S^{2})}_{\Delta})
on
\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)
, which in turn induces an equivalence of monoidal categories

\mathsf{IndCoh}(\widehat{(S^{2})}_{\Delta})\tilde{\longrightarrow}\End_{\mathsf{D}(S)}(\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)).

The natural t-structure on
\mathsf{IndCoh}(\widehat{(S^{2})}_{\Delta})
induces a t-structure on
\End_{\mathsf{D}(S)}(\mathsf{IndCoh}(S))
, which we shift by
\dim S
so that the coconnective objects are exactly the left t-exact functors (i.e. we normalize so that the unit
\id_{\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)}
belongs to the heart).

The monad
\oblv_{S}\circ\ind_{S}
lifts to an associative algebra in
\End_{\mathsf{D}(S)}(\mathsf{IndCoh}(S))
, which corresponds to the algebra of differential operators in
\mathsf{IndCoh}(\widehat{(S^{2})}_{\Delta})
. According to Lemma 6.17.1 of
loc. cit.
, to prove that the monad is effective, it suffices to show that the following conditions are satisfied:

(i)

\oblv_{S}\circ\ind_{S}
is coconnective in
\End_{\mathsf{D}(S)}(\mathsf{IndCoh}(S))
;

(ii)

\oblv_{S}\circ\ind_{S}
is right flat, i.e. the functor

\displaystyle\End_{\mathsf{D}(S)}(\mathsf{IndCoh}(S))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\End_{\mathsf{D}(S)}(\mathsf{IndCoh}(S))

\displaystyle F

\displaystyle\mapsto\oblv_{S}\circ\ind_{S}\circ F

is left t-exact;

(iii)

the object

\cofib(\id_{\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)}\longrightarrow\oblv_{S}\circ\ind_{S})

in
\End_{\mathsf{D}(S)}(\mathsf{IndCoh}(S))
is right flat and eventually connective.

All three of these conditions follow from the existence of the standard filtration on differential operators. Namely, the corresponding filtration on
\oblv_{S}\circ\ind_{S}
in
\End_{\mathsf{D}(S)}(\mathsf{IndCoh}(S))
has associated graded given by tensoring with the locally free sheaf
\Sym_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}\TT(S)
(using the action of
\mathsf{QCoh}(S)
on
\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)
).

∎

2.2.

Recall that for any DG categories
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
equipped with t-structures, their tensor product
\mathcal{C}\otimes\mathcal{D}
admits a canonical t-structure, whose connective subcategory
(\mathcal{C}\otimes\mathcal{D})^{\leq 0}
is generated under colimits by objects
c\boxtimes d
with
c
and
d
connective. If
\mathcal{C}=\mathsf{D}(S_{1})
and
\mathcal{D}=\mathsf{D}(S_{2})
, then this t-structure makes the natural equivalence

\mathsf{D}(S_{1})\otimes\mathsf{D}(S_{2})\tilde{\longrightarrow}\mathsf{D}(S_{1}\times S_{2})

t-exact.

We will use the following technical lemma frequently. Recall that the t-structure on
\mathcal{C}
is said to be
compactly generated
if the category
\mathcal{C}^{\leq 0}
is compactly generated.

Lemma 2.2.1
.

Let
\mathcal{C}_{1}
,
\mathcal{C}_{2}
, and
\mathcal{D}
be DG categories equipped with t-structures, and suppose we are given a functor
F:\mathcal{C}_{1}\to\mathcal{C}_{2}
.

(i)

If
F
is right t-exact, then so is

F\otimes\id_{\mathcal{D}}:\mathcal{C}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{D}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{2}\otimes\mathcal{D}.

(ii)

Suppose that
F
is left t-exact, and that either

(a)

the t-structures on
\mathcal{C}_{1}
and
\mathcal{C}_{2}
are compactly generated, or

(b)

the t-structure on
\mathcal{D}
is compactly generated.

Then
F\otimes\id_{\mathcal{D}}
is left t-exact.

Proof.

This is a combination of Proposition A.1, Lemma A.6, and Lemma A.9 in
[
YR22
]
.

∎

2.3.
Compatible t-structures

The Verdier self-duality equivalence

\mathsf{D}(S)^{\vee}\cong\mathsf{D}(S)

interchanges the symmetric monoidal structure whose multiplication is given by
\otimes^{!}
with the cocommutative coalgebra structure on
\mathsf{D}(S)
whose comultiplication is given by

\Delta_{\dR,*}:\mathsf{D}(S)\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(S\times S)\cong\mathsf{D}(S)\otimes\mathsf{D}(S).

In particular, a
(\mathsf{D}(S),\otimes^{!})
-module structure on a DG category
\mathcal{C}
is the same datum as a
(\mathsf{D}(S),\Delta_{\dR,*})
-comodule structure. In what follows we pass freely between the two.

Similarly to the case of D-modules, the Serre self-duality equivalence

\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)^{\vee}\cong\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)

interchanges the symmetric monoidal structure whose multiplication is given by
\otimes^{!}
with the cocommutative coalgebra structure on
\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)
whose comultiplication is given by
\Delta_{*}
, so that an
(\mathsf{IndCoh}(S),\otimes^{!})
-module structure is the same datum as an
(\mathsf{IndCoh}(S),\Delta_{*})
-comodule structure.

Definition 2.3.1
.

Given a
\mathsf{D}(S)
-module category
\mathcal{C}
, we say that a t-structure on
\mathcal{C}
is
compatible with the

\mathsf{D}(S)
-module structure
if the corresponding coaction map

\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(S)\otimes\mathcal{C}

is t-exact. Similarly, if
\mathcal{D}
is an
\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)
-module category, we say that a t-structure on
\mathcal{D}
is
compatible with the

\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)
-module structure
if the coaction map

\mathcal{D}\to\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes\mathcal{D}

is t-exact.

Proposition 2.3.2
.

If
\mathcal{C}
is a
\mathsf{D}(S)
-module category equipped with a compatible t-structure, then the category
\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes_{\mathsf{D}(S)}\mathcal{C}
admits a unique t-structure such that
\ind_{\mathcal{C}}
is t-exact. Moreover, this t-structure makes
\oblv_{\mathcal{C}}
t-exact and is compatible with the
\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)
-action.

Proof.

We claim that the comonad
\ind_{\mathcal{C}}\circ\oblv_{\mathcal{C}}
is t-exact. In view of the previous proposition, this will imply that there is a unique t-structure on
\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes_{\mathsf{D}(S)}\mathcal{C}
which makes
\ind_{\mathcal{C}}
t-exact (because the comonad is left t-exact), and moreover that
\oblv_{\mathcal{C}}
is t-exact with respect to this t-structure (because the comonad is t-exact).

The cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S\times S_{\dR}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\dR}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\dR}\times S_{\dR}.}

gives rise to a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes\mathsf{D}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}(S)\otimes\mathsf{D}(S),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ind_{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ind_{S}\otimes\id_{\mathsf{D}(S)}}

where the functors are given by
\mathsf{IndCoh}
direct image, and base change implies that the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}(S)\otimes\mathsf{D}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes\mathsf{D}(S)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oblv_{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oblv_{S}\otimes\id_{\mathsf{D}(S)}}

also commutes. Both of these squares take place in the category of
\mathsf{D}(S)
-modules, where
\mathsf{D}(S)
acts on the right factor of the tensor product categories. Notice also that the horizontal functors are fully faithful and admit
\mathsf{D}(S)
-linear right adjoints. Thus, tensoring over
\mathsf{D}(S)
with
\mathcal{C}
, we obtain a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}(S)\otimes\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes_{\mathsf{D}(S)}\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}(S)\otimes\mathcal{C}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oblv_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oblv_{S}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ind_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ind_{S}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}}}

where the horizontal functors are fully faithful. In order to prove that
\ind_{\mathcal{C}}\circ\oblv_{\mathcal{C}}
is t-exact, it therefore suffices to prove that the bottom-left circuit of the outer square is t-exact, since the bottom horizontal functor is the coaction functor and hence t-exact by hypothesis.

Since the upper horizontal functor is t-exact, it is enough to prove that both right vertical functors are t-exact. The functors
\oblv_{S}
and
\ind_{S}
are both t-exact and the t-structures on
\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)
and
\mathsf{D}(S)
are compactly generated, so this follows from Lemma
2.2.1
.

To see that the t-structure on
\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes_{\mathsf{D}(S)}\mathcal{C}
is compatible with the
\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)
-action, consider the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S\times S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\dR}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\dR}\times S_{\dR}.}

Passing to ind-coherent sheaves under direct image and tensoring over
\mathsf{D}(S)
with
\mathcal{C}
, we see that the coaction functors fit into a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes_{\mathsf{D}(S)}\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes_{\mathsf{D}(S)}\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}(S)\otimes\mathcal{C}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ind_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ind_{S}\otimes\ind_{\mathcal{C}}}

Since the lower-left circuit of this square is t-exact, it suffices to show that
\ind_{S}\otimes\ind_{\mathcal{C}}
is t-exact and conservative. We have seen above that

\ind_{S}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}}:\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(S)\otimes\mathcal{C}

is t-exact. Since
\ind_{\mathcal{C}}
is t-exact and the t-structure on
\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)
is compactly generated, the functor

\id_{\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)}\otimes\ind_{\mathcal{C}}:\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\underset{\mathsf{D}(S)}{\otimes}\mathcal{C}

is t-exact by Lemma
2.2.1
. Note that the proof of Proposition
2.1.1
shows that
\ind_{S}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}}
and
\id_{\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)}\otimes\ind_{\mathcal{C}}
are both comonadic and in particular conservative. Thus their composition
\ind_{S}\otimes\ind_{\mathcal{C}}
is t-exact and conservative as claimed.

∎

2.4.
Almost compact generation

This section borrows heavily from the appendix to
[
BZNP17
]
. We warn the reader that our terminology does not agree with
loc. cit.
in some places. For example, we use the term “coherent t-structure” for a weaker notion than theirs.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a DG category equipped with a t-structure. Recall that an object
c
in
\mathcal{C}
is called
almost compact
if its truncation
\tau^{\geq n}c
is compact in
\mathcal{C}^{\geq n}
for any
n\in\mathbb{Z}
. If
c
is eventually coconnective and almost compact, then
c
is called
coherent
. Denote by
\mathcal{C}^{\coh}\subset\mathcal{C}
the (non-cocomplete) full subcategory consisting of coherent objects.

If the t-structure on
\mathcal{C}
is right complete, then any almost compact object is eventually connective, and hence any coherent object is cohomologically bounded.

We will use the folllowing technical lemma repeatedly.

Lemma 2.4.1
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
be DG categories equipped with t-structures which are compatible with filtered colimits and right complete. Suppose that
F:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
is t-exact and admits a continuous right adjoint, and moreover that
F
is conservative on
\mathcal{C}^{+}
. If
c
belongs to
\mathcal{C}^{+}
and
F(c)
is almost compact, then
c
is almost compact (equivalently, coherent).

Proof.

See Step 3 in the proof of Lemma 6.11.2 in
[
Ras19
]
.

∎

Definition 2.4.2
.

We say that
\mathcal{C}
is
coherent
with respect to its t-structure if the inclusion

\mathcal{C}^{\geq 0}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\geq-1}

preserves compact objects.

For example, if
A
is a classical commutative algebra, then
A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}
with its usual t-structure is coherent if and only if
A
is coherent, i.e. every finitely generated ideal is finitely presented.

Proposition 2.4.3
.

Suppose that the t-structure on
\mathcal{C}
is compatible with filtered colimits. Then
\mathcal{C}
is coherent if and only if almost compact objects in
\mathcal{C}
are stable under truncation.

In particular, if
\mathcal{C}
is coherent then the subcategory
\mathcal{C}^{\coh}
is stable under truncation functors, and hence inherits a t-structure from
\mathcal{C}
. This t-structure extends uniquely to a t-structure on the
canonical renormalization

\mathcal{C}^{\ren}:=\mathsf{Ind}(\mathcal{C}^{\coh})

which is compatible with filtered colimits. The unique continuous extension

\mathcal{C}^{\ren}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}

of the inclusion
\mathcal{C}^{\coh}\subset\mathcal{C}
is t-exact.

Definition 2.4.4
.

We call
\mathcal{C}

almost compactly generated
with respect to its t-structure if it satisfies the following conditions:

(i)

the t-structure is compatible with filtered colimits and right complete;

(ii)

\mathcal{C}
is coherent;

(iii)

\mathcal{C}^{\geq 0}
is compactly generated.

Remark 2.4.5
.

Conditions (i) and (iii) are usually rather easy to verify in practice, while coherence is more subtle. One particularly bothersome example of this is the fact that the tensor product of coherent categories need not be coherent; for instance, it is well-known that a ring of polynomials with coefficients in a coherent ring need not be coherent.

The canonical renormalization behaves well for almost compactly generated categories.

Proposition 2.4.6
.

If
\mathcal{C}
is almost compactly generated, then the t-exact functor
\mathcal{C}^{\ren}\to\mathcal{C}
restricts to an equivalence

\mathcal{C}^{\ren,+}\tilde{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{C}^{+}.

The next lemma supplies a convenient criterion for almost compact generation.

Lemma 2.4.7
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{C}
is equipped with a t-structure such that

(i)

the t-structure is compatible with filtered colimits and right complete;

(ii)

the category
\mathcal{C}^{\heartsuit}
is compactly generated;

(iii)

any compact object in
\mathcal{C}^{\heartsuit}
is almost compact in
\mathcal{C}
.

Then
\mathcal{C}
is almost compactly generated.

2.5.

Suppose that
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
are DG categories equipped with t-structures. We will write

\Fun_{\fha}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})\subset\Fun(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})

for the full subcategory consisting of exact continuous functors of finite homological amplitude, or equivalently those exact continuous functors which send
\mathcal{C}^{+}
into
\mathcal{D}^{+}
. Denote by

\Fun_{+\text{-}\!\cts}(\mathcal{C}^{+},\mathcal{D}^{+})\subset\Fun(\mathcal{C}^{+},\mathcal{D}^{+})

the full subcategory consisting of exact functors which commute with filtered colimits bounded uniformly from below. There is a natural restriction functor

(2.5.1)

\Fun_{\fha}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})\longrightarrow\Fun_{+\text{-}\!\cts}(\mathcal{C}^{+},\mathcal{D}^{+}).

Lemma 2.5.1
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{C}
is almost compactly generated, and that
\mathcal{C}^{\ren}\tilde{\to}\mathcal{C}
. Then the functor (
2.5.1
) is an equivalence.

Proof.

The inverse functor is given by the composite

\Fun_{+\text{-}\!\cts}(\mathcal{C}^{+},\mathcal{D}^{+})\longrightarrow\Fun(\mathcal{C}^{\coh},\mathcal{D})\longrightarrow\Fun(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D}),

where the first functor is restriction and the second is left Kan extension. Namely, as observed in Example 4.4.4 of
[
Ras19
]
, the fact that
\mathcal{C}^{\coh}
is stable under truncation functors implies that any functor in
\Fun_{+\text{-}\!\cts}(\mathcal{C}^{+},\mathcal{D}^{+})
is left Kan extended from
\mathcal{C}^{\coh}
.

∎

2.6.
Functorial renormalization

Let us write
\mathsf{DGCat}_{\tstr}
for the category of DG categories equipped with t-structures, with
1
-morphisms given by all continuous functors (we impose no t-exactness conditions on functors here). As described above, the Lurie tensor product of two objects in
\mathsf{DGCat}_{\tstr}
admits a canonical t-structure, and hence the Lurie tensor product lifts to a symmetric monoidal structure on
\mathsf{DGCat}_{\tstr}
. We denote by
(\mathsf{DGCat}_{\tstr})^{\otimes}
the associated pseudo-tensor category.
3
3

3

The term “pseudo-tensor category,” which we have borrowed from Beilinson-Drinfeld, is formally identical to “colored operad” but plays a different conceptual role. A morphism from a colored operad to a pseudo-tensor category should be thought of as an algebra over the colored operad in the pseudo-tensor category. In particular, for us colored operads are “small,” while pseudo-tensor categories tend to be “large.”

We define a
1
-full pseudo-tensor subcategory

(\mathsf{DGCat}_{\tstr})^{\otimes}_{\fha}\subset(\mathsf{DGCat}_{\tstr})^{\otimes}

as follows. An object in
\mathsf{DGCat}_{\tstr}
belongs to
(\mathsf{DGCat}_{\tstr})^{\otimes}_{\fha}
provided that it is almost compactly generated. An
n
-ary morphism

\mathcal{C}_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathcal{C}_{n}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}

belongs to
(\mathsf{DGCat}_{\tstr})^{\otimes}_{\fha}
provided that the image of the composition

\mathcal{C}_{1}^{+}\times\cdots\times\mathcal{C}_{n}^{+}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathcal{C}_{n}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}

is contained in
\mathcal{D}^{+}
.

Next, consider the symmetric monoidal category
\mathsf{DGCat}_{\tstr}^{\Delta^{1}}
of arrows in
\mathsf{DGCat}_{\tstr}
. Define a
1
-full pseudo-tensor subcategory

(\mathsf{DGCat}_{\tstr}^{\Delta^{1}})^{\otimes}_{\ren}\subset(\mathsf{DGCat}_{\tstr}^{\Delta^{1}})^{\otimes}

as follows. An object
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{C}
in
\mathsf{DGCat}_{\tstr}^{\Delta^{1}}
belongs to
(\mathsf{DGCat}_{\tstr}^{\Delta^{1}})^{\otimes}_{\ren}
provided that the following conditions are satisfied:

(i)

\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{C}
is a t-exact functor;

(ii)

\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
are almost compactly generated;

(iii)

the category
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
is compactly generated by
(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})^{\coh}
;

(iv)

\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{C}
induces an equivalence
(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})^{+}\tilde{\to}\mathcal{C}^{+}
.

(Note that these conditions imply that
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{C}
factors uniqely through a continuous t-exact equivalence
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\tilde{\to}\mathcal{C}^{\ren}
.) Given objects

\mathcal{C}_{1}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{C}_{1},\cdots\!,\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{C}_{n},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{D}

in
(\mathsf{DGCat}_{\tstr}^{\Delta^{1}})^{\otimes}_{\ren}
, an
n
-ary morphism

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{1}^{\prime}\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathcal{C}_{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D},}

in
(\mathsf{DGCat}_{\tstr}^{\Delta^{1}})^{\otimes}
is an
n
-ary morphism in
(\mathsf{DGCat}_{\tstr}^{\Delta^{1}})^{\otimes}_{\ren}
provided that both horizontal functors are
n
-ary morphisms in
(\mathsf{DGCat}_{\tstr})^{\otimes}_{\fha}
(in fact, it suffices to impose this condition on the lower horizontal functor).

Proposition 2.6.1
.

The second projection

\displaystyle\mathsf{DGCat}_{\tstr}^{\Delta^{1}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathsf{DGCat}_{\tstr}

\displaystyle(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{C})

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathcal{C}

induces an equivalence of pseudo-tensor categories

(\mathsf{DGCat}_{\tstr}^{\Delta^{1}})^{\otimes}_{\ren}\tilde{\longrightarrow}(\mathsf{DGCat}_{\tstr})^{\otimes}_{\fha}.

Proof.

This functor is essentially surjective on the underlying categories because for any almost compactly generated category
\mathcal{C}
, the canonical renormalization
\mathcal{C}^{\ren}\to\mathcal{C}
is an object of
(\mathsf{DGCat}_{\tstr}^{\Delta^{1}})^{\otimes}_{\ren}
which projects to
\mathcal{C}
.

To see that this functor is fully faithful on
n
-ary morphisms, suppose that we are given objects

\mathcal{C}_{1}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{C}_{1},\cdots\!,\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{C}_{n},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{D}

in
(\mathsf{DGCat}_{\tstr}^{\Delta^{1}})^{\otimes}_{\ren}
. We claim that any
n
-ary morphism

\mathcal{C}_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathcal{C}_{n}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}

in
(\mathsf{DGCat}_{\tstr})^{\otimes}_{\fha}
lifts to an
n
-ary morphism in
(\mathsf{DGCat}_{\tstr}^{\Delta^{1}})^{\otimes}_{\ren}
which is unique up to contractible choice. Namely, the image of the composition

(\mathcal{C}_{1}^{\prime})^{\coh}\times\cdots\times(\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\prime})^{\coh}\tilde{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{C}_{1}^{\coh}\times\cdots\times\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\coh}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}

is contained in
\mathcal{D}^{+}
. Since this functor is exact in each variable separately, it follows that there is a unique continuous extension to

\mathcal{C}_{1}^{\prime}\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\prime}=\mathsf{Ind}((\mathcal{C}_{1}^{\prime})^{\coh})\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathsf{Ind}((\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\prime})^{\coh})\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}.

Note that this extension defines an
n
-ary morphism in
(\mathsf{DGCat}_{\tstr}^{\Delta^{1}})^{\otimes}_{\ren}
, since any object in
(\mathcal{C}_{i}^{\prime})^{+}
is a filtered colimit of objects in
(\mathcal{C}_{i}^{\prime})^{\coh}
bounded uniformly from below. This completes the proof.

∎

We will make frequent use of the endomorphism of pseudo-tensor categories

(2.6.1)

\displaystyle(\mathsf{DGCat}_{\tstr})^{\otimes}_{\fha}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow(\mathsf{DGCat}_{\tstr})^{\otimes}_{\fha}

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathcal{C}^{\ren}

obtained as the composition

(\mathsf{DGCat}_{\tstr})^{\otimes}_{\fha}\tilde{\longrightarrow}(\mathsf{DGCat}_{\tstr}^{\Delta^{1}})^{\otimes}_{\ren}\longrightarrow(\mathsf{DGCat}_{\tstr})^{\otimes}_{\fha},

where the first functor is inverse to the equivalence of Proposition
2.6.1
and the second functor is the first projection
(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{C})\mapsto\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
.

2.7.
Renormalization for crystals of categories

We continue to let
S
denote a smooth quasicompact scheme.

Consider the pseudo-tensor category
(\mathsf{D}(S)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{\tstr})^{\otimes}
whose objects are
\mathsf{D}(S)
-module categories equipped with a compatible t-structure, with
n
-ary morphisms
(\mathcal{C}_{1},\cdots\!,\mathcal{C}_{n})\to\mathcal{D}
given by arbitrary
\mathsf{D}(S)
-linear functors

\mathcal{C}_{1}\underset{\mathsf{D}(S)}{\otimes}\cdots\underset{\mathsf{D}(S)}{\otimes}\mathcal{C}_{n}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}.

We define a pseudo-tensor subcategory

(\mathsf{D}(S)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{\tstr})^{\otimes}_{\fha}\subset(\mathsf{D}(S)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{\tstr})^{\otimes}

a follows. An object
\mathcal{C}
of
(\mathsf{D}(S)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{\tstr})^{\otimes}
belongs to
(\mathsf{D}(S)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{\tstr})^{\otimes}_{\fha}
if
\mathcal{C}
is almost compactly generated. An
n
-ary morphism

\mathcal{C}_{1}\underset{\mathsf{D}(S)}{\otimes}\cdots\underset{\mathsf{D}(S)}{\otimes}\mathcal{C}_{n}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}

belongs to
(\mathsf{D}(S)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{\tstr})^{\otimes}_{\fha}
provided that the image of the composition

\mathcal{C}_{1}^{+}\times\cdots\times\mathcal{C}_{n}^{+}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{1}\underset{\mathsf{D}(S)}{\otimes}\cdots\underset{\mathsf{D}(S)}{\otimes}\mathcal{C}_{n}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}

is contained in
\mathcal{D}^{+}
.

Proposition 2.7.1
.

The morphism (
2.6.1
) lifts canonically to a morphism of pseudo-tensor categories

\displaystyle(\mathsf{D}(S)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{\tstr})^{\otimes}_{\fha}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow(\mathsf{D}(S)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{\tstr})^{\otimes}_{\fha}

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathcal{C}^{\ren}.

Proof.

Observe that
\mathsf{D}(S)
is a commutative algebra object in
(\mathsf{DGCat}_{\tstr})^{\otimes}_{\fha}
satisfying
\mathsf{D}(S)^{\ren}\tilde{\to}\mathsf{D}(S)
. Moreover, for any
\mathsf{D}(S)
-module category
\mathcal{C}
, the coaction functor

\coact_{\mathcal{C}}:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(S)\otimes\mathcal{C}

is left adjoint to the action functor

\act_{\mathcal{C}}:\mathsf{D}(S)\otimes\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}.

It follows that if
\mathcal{C}
is equipped with a compatible t-structure, then
\act_{\mathcal{C}}
is left t-exact, and hence the
\mathsf{D}(S)
-action on
\mathcal{C}
takes place in
(\mathsf{DGCat}_{\tstr})^{\otimes}_{\fha}
provided that
\mathcal{C}
is almost compactly generated. All of this implies that (
2.6.1
) is functorial on
\mathsf{D}(S)
-modules; we need only check that if the
\mathsf{D}(S)
-action on
\mathcal{C}
is compatible with the t-structure, then so is the induced action on
\mathcal{C}^{\ren}
.

First, we claim that the functor
\coact_{\mathcal{C}^{\ren}}
has finite homological amplitude. Since
(\mathcal{C}^{\ren})^{+}
is generated by compact objects of
\mathcal{C}^{\ren}
under filtered colimits bounded uniformly from below, it suffices to show that
\coact_{\mathcal{C}^{\ren}}
sends compact objects to eventually coconnective objects. Observe that
\coact_{\mathcal{C}^{\ren}}
is left adjoint to the continuous functor
\act_{\mathcal{C}^{\ren}}
and hence preserves compact objects, so the claim will follow if we prove that compact objects in
\mathsf{D}(S)\otimes\mathcal{C}^{\ren}
are eventually coconnective. The category
\mathsf{D}(S)\otimes\mathcal{C}^{\ren}
is compactly generated by objects of the form
\mathcal{M}\boxtimes c
where
\mathcal{M}
and
c
are compact in
\mathsf{D}(S)
in
\mathcal{C}^{\ren}
respectively, so we need only prove that objects of this form are eventually coconnective, since they generate all compact objects in
\mathsf{D}(S)\otimes\mathcal{C}^{\ren}
under finite colimits and retracts. But compact objects in
\mathsf{D}(S)
and
\mathcal{C}^{\ren}
are eventually coconnective, which implies that objects
\mathcal{M}\boxtimes c
as above are eventually coconnective, by Lemma
2.2.1
.

To see that
\coact_{\mathcal{C}^{\ren}}
is t-exact, consider the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\ren}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}(S)\otimes\mathcal{C}^{\ren}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}(S)\otimes\mathcal{C}.}

The left vertical and lower horizontal functors are t-exact, and the right vertical functor is t-exact by Lemma
2.2.1
. We have already shown that the upper horizontal functor has finite homological amplitude, and hence we obtain a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{C}^{\ren})^{+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathsf{D}(S)\otimes\mathcal{C}^{\ren})^{+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathsf{D}(S)\otimes\mathcal{C})^{+}.}

Since
(\mathcal{C}^{\ren})^{+}\to\mathcal{C}^{+}
is an equivalence and in particular conservative, Lemma 4.6.2(3) in
[
Ras19
]
implies that the right vertical functor is conservative. It follows immediately that
\coact_{\mathcal{C}^{\ren}}
is left t-exact. As for right t-exactness, since the t-structure on
\mathcal{C}^{\ren}
is compactly generated, it suffices to show that
\coact_{\mathcal{C}^{\ren}}
sends compact objects in
(\mathcal{C}^{\ren})^{\leq 0}
into
(\mathsf{D}(S)\otimes\mathcal{C}^{\ren})^{\leq 0}
. Since compact objects in
\mathcal{C}^{\ren}
are eventually coconnective, this follows from the corresponding property of
\coact_{\mathcal{C}}
using the same commutative square.

∎

2.8.
Almost ULA generation

Suppose that we are given a
\mathsf{D}(S)
-module category
\mathcal{C}
with compatible t-structure.

Definition 2.8.1
.

An object
c
in
\mathcal{C}
will be called
almost ULA
over
S
if
\oblv_{\mathcal{C}}(c)
is almost compact in
\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes_{\mathsf{D}(S)}\mathcal{C}
. We will say that
\mathcal{C}
is
almost ULA generated
if
\mathcal{C}
is almost compactly generated, and moreover
(\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes_{\mathsf{D}(S)}\mathcal{C})^{\geq 0}
is compactly generated by objects of the form

\tau^{\geq 0}(\mathcal{F}\otimes\oblv_{\mathcal{C}}(c))

where
\mathcal{F}
is a perfect complex on
S
and
c
is almost ULA in
\mathcal{C}
.

As the name suggests, the renormalization of an almost ULA generated category is ULA generated.

Proposition 2.8.2
.

Suppose that a
\mathsf{D}(S)
-module category
\mathcal{C}
is almost ULA generated. Then the
\mathsf{D}(S)
-module category
\mathcal{C}^{\ren}
is ULA generated by the objects
\tau^{\geq n}c
in

\mathcal{C}^{+}\cong(\mathcal{C}^{\ren})^{+}\subset\mathcal{C}^{\ren},

where
n\in\mathbb{Z}
and
c
is almost ULA in
\mathcal{C}
.

This proposition is immediate from the following lemma.

Lemma 2.8.3
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a
\mathsf{D}(S)
-module category equipped with compatible t-structure, and suppose that
\mathcal{C}
is almost compactly generated. Then
\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes_{\mathsf{D}(S)}\mathcal{C}
is coherent.

If we assume in addition that
(\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes_{\mathsf{D}(S)}\mathcal{C})^{\geq 0}
is compactly generated (e.g. if
\mathcal{C}
is almost ULA generated), it follows that
\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes_{\mathsf{D}(S)}\mathcal{C}
is almost compactly generated. In that case, the functor

\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\underset{\mathsf{D}(S)}{\otimes}\mathcal{C}^{\ren}\longrightarrow\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\underset{\mathsf{D}(S)}{\otimes}\mathcal{C}

induced by
\mathcal{C}^{\ren}\to\mathcal{C}
factors uniquely through a t-exact equivalence

\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\underset{\mathsf{D}(S)}{\otimes}\mathcal{C}^{\ren}\tilde{\longrightarrow}(\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\underset{\mathsf{D}(S)}{\otimes}\mathcal{C})^{\ren}.

Proof.

For the first claim, it suffices to show that a compact object
c
in
(\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes_{\mathsf{D}(S)}\mathcal{C})^{\geq 0}
is almost compact (equivalently, coherent). Since
\ind_{\mathcal{C}}
is t-exact with continuous and t-exact right adjoint
\oblv_{\mathcal{C}}
, the object
\ind_{\mathcal{C}}(c)
is compact in
\mathcal{C}^{\geq 0}
and hence almost compact in
\mathcal{C}
by coherence of the t-structure on the latter category. Now Lemma
2.4.1
implies that
c
is almost compact in
\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes_{\mathsf{D}(S)}\mathcal{C}
as desired.

Next, consider the functor

\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\underset{\mathsf{D}(S)}{\otimes}\mathcal{C}^{\ren}\longrightarrow\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\underset{\mathsf{D}(S)}{\otimes}\mathcal{C}

induced by
\mathcal{C}^{\ren}\to\mathcal{C}
. This fits into the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes_{\mathsf{D}(S)}\mathcal{C}^{\ren}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes_{\mathsf{D}(S)}\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\ren}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ind_{\mathcal{C}^{\ren}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ind_{\mathcal{C}}}

and is therefore t-exact, since the vertical functors are t-exact and conservative. For the same reason, both

(\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\underset{\mathsf{D}(S)}{\otimes}\mathcal{C}^{\ren})^{+}\xrightarrow{\ind_{\mathcal{C}^{\ren}}}(\mathcal{C}^{\ren})^{+}

and

(\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\underset{\mathsf{D}(S)}{\otimes}\mathcal{C})^{+}\xrightarrow{\ind_{\mathcal{C}}}\mathcal{C}^{+}

are comonadic (cf. the Proof of Lemma 3.7.1 in
[
Ras19
]
). Since
(\mathcal{C}^{\ren})^{+}\tilde{\to}\mathcal{C}^{+}
, we deduce that the functor under consideration induces an equivalence

(\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\underset{\mathsf{D}(S)}{\otimes}\mathcal{C}^{\ren})^{+}\tilde{\longrightarrow}(\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\underset{\mathsf{D}(S)}{\otimes}\mathcal{C})^{+}.

It folows that the functor

(\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\underset{\mathsf{D}(S)}{\otimes}\mathcal{C})^{\ren}\longrightarrow\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\underset{\mathsf{D}(S)}{\otimes}\mathcal{C}

factors uniquely through a t-exact continuous functor

(\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\underset{\mathsf{D}(S)}{\otimes}\mathcal{C})^{\ren}\longrightarrow\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\underset{\mathsf{D}(S)}{\otimes}\mathcal{C}^{\ren},

which we claim is an equivalence. Namely, observe that the triangles

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes_{\mathsf{D}(S)}\mathcal{C})^{\ren}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes_{\mathsf{D}(S)}\mathcal{C}^{\ren}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\ren}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ind_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ren}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ind_{\mathcal{C}^{\ren}}}

and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes_{\mathsf{D}(S)}\mathcal{C})^{\ren}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes_{\mathsf{D}(S)}\mathcal{C}^{\ren}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\ren}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oblv_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ren}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oblv_{\mathcal{C}^{\ren}}}

commute, since they commute after restricting to eventually coconnective objects, and the categories
(\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes_{\mathsf{D}(S)}\mathcal{C})^{\ren}
and
\mathcal{C}^{\ren}
are compactly generated by coherent objects. Since the functor
\ind_{\mathcal{C}^{\ren}}
is comonadic by Proposition
2.1.1
, it suffices to show that
\ind_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ren}
is comonadic.

For this, observe that
\End_{\mathsf{D}(S)}(\mathsf{IndCoh}(S))
is almost compactly generated with respect to the t-structure defined in the proof of Proposition
2.1.1
, and that

\End_{\mathsf{D}(S)}(\mathsf{IndCoh}(S))^{\ren}\tilde{\longrightarrow}\End_{\mathsf{D}(S)}(\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)).

Namely, we have an equivalence

\End_{\mathsf{D}(S)}(\mathsf{IndCoh}(S))\tilde{\longrightarrow}\mathsf{IndCoh}((\widehat{S^{2}})_{\Delta})

which is t-exact up to shift, and the t-structure on the right side has these properties. The compatibility of the
D(S)
-action with the t-structure on
\mathcal{C}
implies that the action of
\End_{\mathsf{D}(S)}(\mathsf{IndCoh}(S))
on
\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes_{\mathsf{D}(S)}\mathcal{C}
takes place in
(\mathsf{DGCat}_{\tstr})^{\otimes}_{\fha}
, and hence (
2.6.1
) induces an action on the renormalization
(\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes_{\mathsf{D}(S)}\mathcal{C})^{\ren}
. The monad induced by the adjunction

\ind_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ren}:(\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\underset{\mathsf{D}(S)}{\otimes}\mathcal{C})^{\ren}\rightleftarrows\mathcal{C}^{\ren}:\oblv_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ren}

is given by the action of the associative algebra
\oblv_{S}\circ\ind_{S}
in
\End_{\mathsf{D}(S)}(\mathsf{IndCoh}(S))
, since this is the case on eventually coconnective objects and
(\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes_{\mathsf{D}(S)}\mathcal{C})^{\ren}
is compactly generated by coherent objects. As in Proposition
2.1.1
, it follows that this monad is effective and hence that
\ind_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ren}
is comonadic.

∎

2.9.
Change of base: open embedding

Suppose that
j:U\to S
is an open embedding and
\mathcal{C}
is a
\mathsf{D}(S)
-module category with compatible t-structure. Put

\mathcal{C}_{U}:=\mathsf{D}(U)\underset{\mathsf{D}(S)}{\otimes}\mathcal{C}.

Then we have an adjunction

j^{*}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}}:\mathcal{C}=\mathsf{D}(S)\underset{\mathsf{D}(S)}{\otimes}\mathcal{C}\rightleftarrows\mathcal{C}_{U}:j_{*}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}}

where the right adjoint is fully faithful, and in particular
\mathcal{C}_{U}
admits a unique t-structure such that
j_{*}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}}
is left t-exact, or equivalently such that
j^{*}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}}
is right t-exact. Here we are abusing notation slightly by writing
j_{*}
rather than
j_{\dR,*}
, but since
j
is an open embedding, this should not cause any confusion.

Lemma 2.9.1
.

The functor
j^{*}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}}:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}_{U}
is t-exact. If
j
is affine, then the right adjoint
j_{*}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}}:\mathcal{C}_{U}\to\mathcal{C}
is t-exact.

Proof.

To see that
j^{*}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}}
is t-exact, it suffices to show it is left t-exact, which is equivalent to left t-exactness of the endofunctor

(j_{*}j^{*})\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}}:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{U}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}.

Similarly, if
(j_{*}j^{*})\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}}
is t-exact, then
j_{*}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}}
is t-exact. We have a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}(S)\otimes\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}(S)\otimes\mathcal{C}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coact_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(j_{*}j^{*})\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(j_{*}j^{*})\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coact_{\mathcal{C}}}

where the horizontal functors are fully faithful and t-exact. By Lemma
2.2.1
, the right vertical functor is left t-exact, respectively right t-exact if the functor
j_{*}j^{*}
is such. The lemma follows.

∎

Proposition 2.9.2
.

If
\mathcal{C}
is almost ULA generated over
S
, then
\mathcal{C}_{U}
is almost ULA generated over
U
, and the functor

(\mathcal{C}^{\ren})_{U}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{U}

induced by
\mathcal{C}^{\ren}\to\mathcal{C}
factors uniquely through a t-exact equivalence

(\mathcal{C}^{\ren})_{U}\tilde{\longrightarrow}(\mathcal{C}_{U})^{\ren}.

Proof.

Suppose that
\mathcal{C}
is almost compactly generated. Since
j_{*}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}}:\mathcal{C}_{U}\to\mathcal{C}
is left t-exact and conservative, the t-structure on
\mathcal{C}_{U}
is compatible with filtered colimits. For the same reason, the t-structure on
\mathcal{C}
is right separated and hence right complete. To see that
(\mathcal{C}_{U})^{\geq 0}
is compactly generated, consider the adjunction

j^{*}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}}:\mathcal{C}^{\geq 0}\rightleftarrows(\mathcal{C}_{U})^{\geq 0}:j_{*}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}}.

Since
j_{*}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}}
is continuous and fully faithful, hence conservative, it follows that
(\mathcal{C}_{U})^{\geq 0}
is compactly generated.

To see that
\mathcal{C}_{U}
is almost compactly generated, it remains to show that the t-structure is coherent. We claim that any compact object
c
in
(\mathcal{C}_{U})^{\geq 0}
is a direct summand of an object
(j^{*}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}})(c_{0})
where
c_{0}
is a compact object in
\mathcal{C}^{\geq 0}
. Since
\mathcal{C}
is almost compactly generated and
j^{*}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}}
preserves almost compact objects, it will follow that
c
is almost compact in
\mathcal{C}_{U}
. To prove the claim, write

(j_{*}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}})(c)\cong\underset{\alpha}{\colim}\ c_{\alpha}

as a filtered colimit of compact objects
c_{\alpha}
in
\mathcal{C}^{\geq 0}
. Then we have

c\cong\underset{\alpha}{\colim}\ (j^{*}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}})(c_{\alpha})

in
(\mathcal{C}_{U})^{\geq 0}
. Compactness of
c
in
(\mathcal{C}_{U})^{\geq 0}
now implies that
c
is a direct summand of
(j^{*}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}})(c_{\alpha})
for some
\alpha
.

Renormalization of the above adjunction yields a
\mathsf{D}(S)
-linear adjunction

(j^{*}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}})^{\ren}:\mathcal{C}^{\ren}\rightleftarrows(\mathcal{C}_{U})^{\ren}:(j_{*}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}})^{\ren}.

The right adjoint is continuous and fully faithful, so in particular this adjunction is monadic. Note that
(j_{*}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}})^{\ren}
factors through

j_{*}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}^{\ren}}:(\mathcal{C}^{\ren})_{U}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\ren},

since this is the case after restricting to
(\mathcal{C}_{U})^{+}
. The resulting functor

(\mathcal{C}_{U})^{\ren}\longrightarrow(\mathcal{C}^{\ren})_{U}

corresponds to a morphism of (left t-exact) monads on
\mathcal{C}^{\ren}
, which is an isomorphism because it is such after restricting to
\mathcal{C}^{+}
.

The assertion that
\mathcal{C}_{U}
is almost ULA generated over
U
follows readily from the above.

∎

2.10.
Change of base: closed embedding

Suppose that we are given a smooth closed subvariety
i:T\to S
. Writing

\mathcal{C}_{T}:=\mathsf{D}(T)\underset{\mathsf{D}(S)}{\otimes}\mathcal{C}

for any
\mathsf{D}(S)
-module category
\mathcal{C}
, note that we have an adjunction

i_{\dR,*}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}}:\mathcal{C}_{T}\rightleftarrows\mathcal{C}:i^{!}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}},

with the left adjoint being fully faithful.

Lemma 2.10.1
.

The functor
i^{!}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}}:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}_{T}
sends objects ULA over
S
to objects ULA over
T
. If
\mathcal{C}
is ULA generated over
S
, the
\mathcal{C}_{T}
is ULA generated over
T
by objects of the form
(i^{!}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}})(c)
, where
c
is ULA in
\mathcal{C}
.

Proof.

Consider the commutative square of symmetric monoidal categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}(T)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{IndCoh}(T).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oblv_{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oblv_{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{!}}

This can also be viewed as a commutative square of
\mathsf{D}(S)
-modules, which we can tensor with
\mathcal{C}
to obtain

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes_{\mathsf{D}(S)}\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{IndCoh}(T)\otimes_{\mathsf{D}(T)}\mathcal{C}_{T}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{!}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oblv_{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oblv_{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{!}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}}}

Since
S
and
T
are smooth, the functor

i_{*}:\mathsf{IndCoh}(T)\longrightarrow\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)

admits a left adjoint
i^{\mathsf{IndCoh},*}
, which agrees with
i^{!}
up to tensoring with a cohomologically shifted line bundle. Thus
i^{!}
admits a
\mathsf{D}(S)
-linear right adjoint, which implies that the lower horizontal functor in the above square admits a continuous right adjoint and hence preserves compact objects. It follows immediately that the upper horizontal functor preserves ULA objects.

The second claim follows from the observation that the image of

i^{!}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}}:\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes_{\mathsf{D}(S)}\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathsf{IndCoh}(T)\otimes_{\mathsf{D}(T)}\mathcal{C}_{T}

generates the target under colimits, which is immediate from the corresponding property of

i^{!}:\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\longrightarrow\mathsf{IndCoh}(T)

(this being equivalent to the conservativity of its right adjoint, which is isomorphic to
i_{*}
tensored with a cohomologically shifted line bundle).

∎

Lemma 2.10.2
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{C}
is equipped with a t-structure compatible with the
\mathsf{D}(S)
-action. Then the image of the fully faithful functor

i_{\dR,*}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}}:\mathcal{C}_{T}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}

is stable under truncations, so
\mathcal{C}_{T}
admits a unique t-structure such that
i_{\dR,*}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}}
is t-exact. Moreover, the subcategory
(\mathcal{C}_{T})^{\heartsuit}\subset\mathcal{C}^{\heartsuit}
is stable under taking subobjects, and the t-structure on
\mathcal{C}_{T}
is compatible with the
\mathsf{D}(T)
-action.

Proof.

The stability of
\mathcal{C}_{T}
under truncations and of
(\mathcal{C}_{T})^{\heartsuit}
under taking subobjects both follow formally from Lemma
2.9.1
(specifically, the fact that
j^{*}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}}
is t-exact). To see that the
\mathsf{D}(T)
-action on
\mathcal{C}_{T}
is compatible with the t-structure, consider the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}(T)\otimes\mathcal{C}_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}(S)\otimes\mathcal{C}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\dR,*}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coact_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coact_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\dR,*}\otimes i_{\dR,*}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}}}

The upper horizontal and right vertical functors are t-exact, so it suffices to show that that the lower horizontal functor is conservative and t-exact. In fact, it is fully faithful, being the tensor product of two fully faithful functors which admit continuous right adjoints. To see the t-exactness, note that this functor can be decomposed as

\mathsf{D}(T)\otimes\mathcal{C}_{T}\xrightarrow{i_{\dR,*}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}_{T}}}\mathsf{D}(S)\otimes\mathcal{C}_{T}\xrightarrow{\id_{\mathsf{D}(S)}\otimes i_{\dR,*}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}}}\mathsf{D}(S)\otimes\mathcal{C}.

Since the t-structures on
\mathsf{D}(S)
and
\mathsf{D}(T)
are compactly generated, the t-exactness of these two functors follows from that of
i_{\dR,*}
and
i_{\dR,*}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}}:\mathcal{C}_{T}\to\mathcal{C}
by Lemma
2.2.1
.

∎

If
\mathcal{C}_{T}
and
\mathcal{C}
are almost ULA generated, then we obtain an adjunction

(i_{\dR,*}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}})^{\ren}:(\mathcal{C}_{T})^{\ren}\rightleftarrows\mathcal{C}^{\ren}:(i^{!}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}})^{\ren},

which takes place in
\mathsf{D}(S)
-modules by Proposition
2.7.1
.

Proposition 2.10.3
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{C}_{T}
are almost ULA generated (with respect to
S
and
T
respectively). Then the functor
(i_{\dR,*}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}})^{\ren}
factors through an equivalence

(\mathcal{C}_{T})^{\ren}\tilde{\longrightarrow}(\mathcal{C}^{\ren})_{T}.

Proof.

Note that
(\mathcal{C}^{\ren})_{T}
is the kernel of the monad
(j_{*}j^{*})\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}^{\ren}}
, which fits into the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\ren}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\ren}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(j_{*}j^{*})\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}^{\ren}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(j_{*}j^{*})\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}}}

Since
\mathcal{C}^{\ren,+}\tilde{\to}\mathcal{C}^{+}
, and
(\mathcal{C}_{T})^{\coh}\subset\mathcal{C}_{T}
is annihilated by
(j_{*}j^{*})\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}}
, it follows that
(i_{\dR,*}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}})^{\ren}
factors through

i_{\dR,*}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}^{\ren}}:(\mathcal{C}^{\ren})_{T}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\ren}.

To see that the resulting functor

(\mathcal{C}_{T})^{\ren}\longrightarrow(\mathcal{C}^{\ren})_{T}

is essentially surjective, observe that Proposition
2.8.2
and Lemma
2.10.1
together imply that
(\mathcal{C}^{\ren})_{T}
is ULA generated over
T
by objects of the form
(i^{!}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}^{\ren}})(c)
, where
c
is an object in
\mathcal{C}^{\ren}
which is ULA over
S
. Now we appeal to the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\ren}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\ren}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(i_{\dR,*}i^{!})\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}^{\ren}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(i_{\dR,*}i^{!})\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}}}

which shows that
((i_{\dR,*}i^{!})\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}^{\ren}})(c)
agrees with
((i_{\dR,*}i^{!})\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}})(c)
under the identification
\mathcal{C}^{\ren,+}\tilde{\to}\mathcal{C}^{+}
(in the latter formula, we view
c
as an object of
\mathcal{C}^{+}
). But the object
((i_{\dR,*}i^{!})\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}})(c)
belongs to
(\mathcal{C}_{T})^{\coh}\subset\mathcal{C}
, which shows that
(i^{!}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{C}^{\ren}})(c)
belongs to the essential image of
(\mathcal{C}_{T})^{\ren}
as needed.

∎

3.
Preliminaries on factorization

This section covers foundational technical material on factorization categories, algebras, and modules. The key result is Proposition
3.9.2
, which gives sufficient conditions under which it is possible to renormalize a factorization category.

3.1.

We refer the reader to §6 of
[
Ras15a
]
for careful definitions of the objects introduced in this section. An alternative but equivalent set of definitions can be found in §10 of
[
CF21b
]
(see Remark 10.5.11 there, which compares the two).

We will denote by
\mathsf{FactCat}
the category of unital factorization categories on
X
, with morphisms given by strictly unital factorizable functors. We can also consider the category
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}
consisting of lax factorization categories. Both of these categories admit variants in which functors are allowed to be lax unital, and together these assemble into a cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{FactCat}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}_{\laxuntl}.}

Here the horizontal functors are fully faithful, while the vertical functors are only
1
-fully faithful but act as the identity on objects.

By definition, we have a forgetful functor

\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}_{\laxuntl}\longrightarrow\mathsf{ShvCat}(\Ran^{\un}_{\dR}),

where
\Ran^{\un}
denotes the unital Ran space of
X
, i.e. the lax prestack parameterizing finite subsets of
X
and inclusions between them. The category
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}_{\laxuntl}
admits a natural symmetric monoidal structure which makes the forgetful functor symmetric monoidal. This restricts to a symmetric monoidal structure on each of the
1
-full subcategories of
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}_{\laxuntl}
considered above.

Given an object
\mathcal{C}
in
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}_{\laxuntl}
, we will write

\mathcal{C}_{Y}:=\Gamma(Y,\mathcal{C})

for any prestack
Y
mapping to
\Ran^{\un}_{\dR}
. For example, for any finite set
I
we have the
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
-module category
\mathcal{C}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
and the
\mathsf{IndCoh}(X^{I})
-module category

\mathcal{C}_{X^{I}}=\mathsf{IndCoh}(X^{I})\underset{\mathsf{D}(X^{I})}{\otimes}\mathcal{C}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}.

3.2.
A combinatorial presentation for factorization categories

We denote by
\mathsf{fSet}
the category of finite sets, and write
\mathsf{fSet}_{\surj}\subset\mathsf{fSet}
for the subcategory consisting of nonempty finite sets and surjections between them.

Recall from
[
Ras15a
]
that for a lax prestack
Y
, there is a certain
1
-full subcategory

\mathsf{ShvCat}(Y)^{\naive}\subset\mathsf{ShvCat}(Y)

with the same objects but fewer morphisms. In the case of unital Ran space, the subcategory

\mathsf{ShvCat}(\Ran_{\dR}^{\un})^{\naive}\subset\mathsf{ShvCat}(\Ran_{\dR}^{\un})

contains only those morphisms which are “strictly unital,” i.e.

\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}=\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}_{\laxuntl}\underset{\mathsf{ShvCat}(\Ran_{\dR}^{\un})}{\times}\mathsf{ShvCat}(\Ran_{\dR}^{\un})^{\naive}.

From the colimit presentation

\Ran=\underset{I\in\mathsf{fSet}^{\op}_{\surj}}{\colim}X^{I}

and the
1
-affineness of
X^{I}_{\dR}
, we obtain a limit presentation

\mathsf{ShvCat}(\Ran_{\dR})=\lim_{I\in\mathsf{fSet}_{\surj}}\mathsf{D}(X^{I})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}.

The following result supplies an analogous presentation of
\mathsf{ShvCat}(\Ran^{\un}_{\dR})^{\naive}
as a lax limit.

Proposition 3.2.1
.

There is a canonical fully faithful embedding of symmetric monoidal categories

\mathsf{ShvCat}(\Ran^{\un}_{\dR})^{\naive}\longrightarrow\underset{I\in\mathsf{fSet}}{\operatorname{lax}\,\lim}\ \mathsf{D}(X^{I})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}.

The image consists of those objects
\{\mathcal{C}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\}
such that, for any surjection
I\to J
, the functor

\mathsf{D}(X^{J})\underset{\mathsf{D}(X^{I})}{\otimes}\mathcal{C}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{X^{J}_{\dR}}

is an equivalence.

Proof.

This is Corollary 4.6.1 in
[
Ras15a
]
.

∎

Following
loc. cit.
, we write
\Part\subset\Part_{\un}
for the twisted arrows categories of
\mathsf{fSet}_{\surj}\subset\mathsf{fSet}
. For any
p:I\to J
in
\Part_{\un}
, there is a corresponding open subscheme
U(p)\subset X^{I}
, and given
\epsilon:p_{1}\to p_{2}
in
\Part_{\un}
, there is a unique morphism
U(\epsilon):U(p_{2})\to U(p_{1})
which makes the following square commute:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U(p_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U(p_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{I_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{I_{1}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U(\epsilon)}

Thus we obtain a functor
U
from
\Part_{\un}^{\op}
into schemes.

If we equip
\Part_{\un}
