with the symmetric monoidal structure given by
(p_{1},p_{2})\mapsto p_{1}\sqcup p_{2}
, then
U
is canonically oplax symmetric monoidal, since

U(p_{1}\sqcup p_{2})\subset U(p_{1})\times U(p_{2})

as subschemes of
X^{I_{1}\sqcup I_{2}}=X^{I_{1}}\times X^{I_{2}}
. This endows the resulting functor

\displaystyle\Part_{\un}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathsf{Cat}

\displaystyle p

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathsf{D}(U(p))\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}

with a lax symmetric monoidal structure, and hence its straightening (the corresponding cocartesian fibration)

(3.2.1)

\underset{p\in\Part_{\un}}{\operatorname{lax}\,\colim}\ \mathsf{D}(U(p))\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}\longrightarrow\Part_{\un}

is naturally symmetric monoidal (in particular, the lax colimit admits a symmetric monoidal structure).

Proposition 3.2.2
.

There is canonical equivalence from
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}
to the full subcategory of lax symmetric monoidal sections of (
3.2.1
) satisfying the following conditions:

(i)

morphisms in
\Part\subset\Part_{\un}
map to cocartesian morphisms;

(ii)

morphisms between objects of the form
\varnothing\to I
map to cocartesian morphisms.

Proof.

This is a slight variant of Proposition-Construction 8.4.1 (cf. also §8.12) in
[
Ras15a
]
.

∎

3.3.
Dualizable factorization categories

We will need the following technical result.

Proposition 3.3.1
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{C}
in
\mathsf{FactCat}
has the property that
\mathcal{C}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
is dualizable as a
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
-module for any finite set
I
. Assume also that for any injection
J\to I
, the unital structure functor

\mathsf{D}(X^{I})\underset{\mathsf{D}(X^{J})}{\otimes}\mathcal{C}_{X^{J}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

admits a
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
-linear right adjoint. Then
\mathcal{C}
is dualizable as an object of
\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl}
.

Proof.

First, observe that the hypothesis in the proposition implies that

\mathsf{D}(X^{I})\underset{\mathsf{D}(X^{J})}{\otimes}\mathcal{C}_{X^{J}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

admits a
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
-linear right adjoint for any (not necessarily injective) map
I\to J
in
\mathsf{fSet}
. Namely, any such map can written as the composition of a surjection and an injection, and the functor in question is an equivalence if
I\to J
is surjective. It follows that the sheaf of categories underlying
\mathcal{C}
is dualizable as an object of
\mathsf{ShvCat}(\Ran_{\dR}^{\un})
, with dual object given by dualizing termwise and then passing to left adjoints of the structure functors.

A lax factorization structure on
\mathcal{C}
induces an oplax factorization structure on
\mathcal{C}^{\vee}
, and if the factorization structure on
\mathcal{C}
is strict then so is the one on
\mathcal{C}^{\vee}
. It is not difficult to see that the evaluation

\mathcal{C}^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\Vect

and coevaluation

\Vect\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{C}

morphisms lift from
\mathsf{ShvCat}(\Ran_{\dR}^{\un})
to
\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl}
.

∎

Remark 3.3.2
.

We emphasize that it is necessary to take the dual of
\mathcal{C}
in
\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl}
rather than
\mathsf{FactCat}
. The point is that the coevaluation functor

\Vect\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{C}

is typically only lax unital.

3.4.
Commutative factorization categories

As shown in
[
Ras15a
]
§7.5, one can functorially attach an object of
\mathsf{FactCat}
to any symmetric monoidal DG category. Given a symmetric monoidal DG category, we denote the associated factorization category by the same symbol to avoid cluttering the notation. We will take care to avoid confusion by specifying which object we are referring to in each case.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a symmetric monoidal category. For any finite set
I
, we have a symmetric monoidal functor

\Loc_{X^{I}}:\mathcal{C}^{\otimes I}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{X^{I}_{\dR}},

defined in Remark 6.8.4 of
[
Ras21a
]
.

Proposition 3.4.1
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{C}
is a rigid symmetric monoidal category whose underlying DG category is compactly generated.

(i)

The functor
\Loc_{X^{I}}
sends compact objects to objects ULA over
X^{I}
. If
\{c_{\alpha}\}
is a set of compact generators for
\mathcal{C}^{\otimes I}
, then
\{\Loc_{X^{I}}(c_{\alpha})\}
is a set of ULA generators for
\mathcal{C}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
.

(ii)

The factorization category attached to
\mathcal{C}
is dualizable and self-dual as an object of
\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl}
.

Proof.

For (i), see Proposition 6.16.1 and the proof of Theorem 6.7.1 in
loc. cit.

By Corollary 6.18.2 of
[
Ras21a
]
, the
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
-module category
\mathcal{C}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
is dualizable and self-dual for any finite set
I
. According to Proposition
3.3.1
, to prove (ii) it remains to show that for any injection
J\to I
, the structure map

\mathsf{D}(X^{I})\underset{\mathsf{D}(X^{J})}{\otimes}\mathcal{C}_{X^{J}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

admits a
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
-linear right adjoint, or equivalently preserves ULA objects. This follows from the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\otimes J}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\otimes I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}(X^{I})\underset{\mathsf{D}(X^{J})}{\otimes}\mathcal{C}_{X^{J}_{\dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{X^{I}_{\dR}},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id\otimes\Loc_{X^{J}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Loc_{X^{I}}}

where the upper horizontal arrow acts as the identity on
\mathcal{C}^{\otimes J}
and inserts the unit in
\mathcal{C}^{\otimes(I\setminus J)}
.

∎

The following lemma will also be useful.

Lemma 3.4.2
.

Let
F:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
be a symmetric monoidal functor. Assume that
\mathcal{C}
is rigid and compactly generated, and that the unit object in
\mathcal{D}
is compact, so that in particular the right adjoint
F^{\RR}
of
F
is continuous. Then the morphism in
\mathsf{FactCat}
arising from
F
admits a right adjoint in
\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl}
, which in particular agrees with
F^{\RR}
fiberwise.

Proof.

It suffices to prove that for any finite set
I
, the
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
-linear functor

F:\mathcal{C}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

preserves ULA objects. Using the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\otimes I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\otimes I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{\otimes I}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Loc_{X^{I}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Loc_{X^{I}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}

and Proposition
3.4.1
, it is enough to prove that the composition

\mathcal{C}^{\otimes I}\xrightarrow{F^{\otimes I}}\mathcal{D}^{\otimes I}\xrightarrow{\Loc_{X^{I}}}\mathcal{D}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

sends compact objects to objects ULA over
X^{I}
. This functor is symmetric monoidal, and the proof of Lemma 6.16.2 in
[
Ras21a
]
shows that the unit object in
\mathcal{D}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
is ULA, so the claim follows from the rigidity of
\mathcal{C}^{\otimes I}
.

∎

3.5.
Functoriality of factorization modules

Consider the category
(\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl})^{\mathsf{FactAlg}^{\op}}
, whose objects are pairs
(\mathcal{C},A)
consisting of a factorization category
\mathcal{C}
and an (always unital unless otherwise specified) factorization algebra
A
in
\mathcal{C}
. A morphism
(\mathcal{C},A)\to(\mathcal{D},B)
is given by a lax unital factorization functor
F:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
and a morphism
B\to F(A)
of factorization algebras in
\mathcal{D}
.

For a given pair
(\mathcal{C},A)
as above we can consider the lax factorization category of (unital) factorization modules
A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fact}(\mathcal{C})
. This construction extends to a functor

(3.5.1)

(\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl})^{\mathsf{FactAlg}^{\op}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}_{\laxuntl}.

In particular, given a morphism
(\mathcal{C},A)\to(\mathcal{D},B)
consisting of
F:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
and
B\to F(A)
, we have the resulting functor

A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fact}(\mathcal{C})\longrightarrow F(A)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fact}(\mathcal{D})\longrightarrow B\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fact}(\mathcal{D}).

Here the first functor is induced by
F
, and the second is restriction of the factorization module structure along
B\to F(A)
(cf.
[
CF21b
]
§10.6).

We observe that
(\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl})^{\mathsf{FactAlg}^{\op}}
admits a natural symmetric monoidal structure, given on objects by

(\mathcal{C},A)\otimes(\mathcal{D},B)=(\mathcal{C}\otimes\mathcal{D},A\boxtimes B).

Moreover, the functor (
3.5.1
) is naturally lax symmetric monoidal: in particular, for
(\mathcal{C},A)
and
(\mathcal{D},B)
in
(\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl})^{\mathsf{FactAlg}^{\op}}
we have a canonical morphism

A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fact}(\mathcal{C})\otimes B\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fact}(\mathcal{D})\longrightarrow(A\boxtimes B)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fact}(\mathcal{C}\otimes\mathcal{D})

in
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}
, which intertwines the forgetful functors to
\mathcal{C}\otimes\mathcal{D}
.

3.6.

We will also need the following lemma. Let
\mathcal{C}
be a factorization category and
A\to B
a morphism of factorization algebras in
\mathcal{C}
. The resulting morphism

B\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathcal{C})\longrightarrow A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathcal{C})

in
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}_{\laxuntl}
sends the vacuum module
B
to a factorization algebra in
A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathcal{C})
which we denote by the same symbol
B
.

Lemma 3.6.1
.

Applying (
3.5.1
) to the forgetful functor

A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathcal{C})\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}

and the factorization module
B
induces an isomorphism

B\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathcal{C}))\longrightarrow B\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathcal{C})

in
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}
.

Proof.

The inverse is given by applying (
3.5.1
) to the restriction functor

B\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathcal{C})\longrightarrow A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathcal{C})

and the factorization module
B
.

∎

3.7.

Observe that the projection
(\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl})^{\mathsf{FactAlg}^{\op}}\to\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl}
is symmetric monoidal, and in particular induces a functor

\mathsf{AssocAlg}((\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl})^{\mathsf{FactAlg}^{\op}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{AssocAlg}(\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl}).

This functor admits a canonical section

(3.7.1)

\mathsf{AssocAlg}(\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl})\longrightarrow\mathsf{AssocAlg}((\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl})^{\mathsf{FactAlg}^{\op}})

given on objects by
\mathcal{A}\mapsto(\mathcal{A},\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}})
, where
\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}}
denotes the monoidal unit of
\mathcal{A}
viewed as a factorization algebra.

The lax symmetric monoidal structure on (
3.5.1
) gives rise to a functor

\mathsf{AssocAlg}((\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl})^{\mathsf{FactAlg}^{\op}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{AssocAlg}(\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}_{\laxuntl}).

Note that the composition of this functor with (
3.7.1
) factors through the
1
-full subcategory

\mathsf{AssocAlg}(\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact})\longrightarrow\mathsf{AssocAlg}(\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}_{\laxuntl}),

and hence can be viewed as a functor

(3.7.2)

\displaystyle\mathsf{AssocAlg}(\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathsf{AssocAlg}(\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact})

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathcal{A}).

Namely, the factorization unit and the monoidal unit in
\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathcal{A})
coincide, both being the vacuum module.

3.8.
Unital vs. non-unital factorization modules

Fix a factorization category
\mathcal{C}
. We denote by
\mathsf{FactAlg}(\mathcal{C})
and
\mathsf{FactAlg}^{\nonuntl}(\mathcal{C})
the categories of unital and non-unital factorization algebras, respectively.

Proposition 3.8.1
.

The forgetful functor

\OblvUnit:\mathsf{FactAlg}(\mathcal{C})\longrightarrow\mathsf{FactAlg}^{\nonuntl}(\mathcal{C})

admits a left adjoint
\AddUnit
. The comonad

\AddUnit\circ\OblvUnit:\mathsf{FactAlg}(\mathcal{C})\longrightarrow\mathsf{FactAlg}(\mathcal{C})

is given by
A\mapsto A\times\unit_{\mathcal{C}}
.

As in the setting of commutative algebras, a unital
\AddUnit(A)
-module structure is the same datum as a non-unital
A
-module structure.

Proposition 3.8.2
.

Given
A
in
\mathsf{FactAlg}^{\nonuntl}(\mathcal{C})
, the composition

\AddUnit(A)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathcal{C})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\AddUnit(A)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fact,\nonuntl}(\mathcal{C})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fact,\nonuntl}(\mathcal{C})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

is an equivalence for any nonempty finite set
I
.

Modules over cartesian products of factorization algebras also behave as expected.

Proposition 3.8.3
.

For any
A
,
B
in
\mathsf{FactAlg}(\mathcal{C})
, the functor

\res^{A\times B}_{A}\oplus\res^{A\times B}_{B}:A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathcal{C})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\times B\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathcal{C})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow(A\times B)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathcal{C})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

is an equivalence for any finite set
I
.

Combining the above, we obtain the following useful result.

Proposition 3.8.4
.

For any
A
in
\mathsf{FactAlg}(\mathcal{C})
, the forgetful functor

A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathcal{C})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fact,\nonuntl}(\mathcal{C})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

factors canonically as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathcal{C})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathcal{C})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\times\mathcal{C}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fact,\nonuntl}(\mathcal{C})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\id,0)}

where the vertical functor is an equivalence. In particular, this forgetful functor is fully faithful.

3.9.
Renormalization

Finally, we formulate conditions under which it is possible to renormalize a factorization category.

Proposition 3.9.1
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a sheaf of categories on
\Ran_{\dR}^{\un}
, and suppose we are given a t-structure on
\mathcal{C}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
for each finite set
I
, compatible with the
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
-action. Assume also that the following conditions are satisfied:

(i)

\mathcal{C}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
is almost ULA generated for any
I
in
\mathsf{fSet}
;

(ii)

for any surjection
I\to J
, the functor
\mathcal{C}_{X^{J}_{\dR}}\to\mathcal{C}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
left adjoint to the structure functor is t-exact;

(iii)

for any injection
I\to J
, the structure functor
\mathcal{C}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\to\mathcal{C}_{X^{J}_{\dR}}
has finite homological amplitude.

Then there exists a sheaf of categories
\mathcal{C}^{\ren}
on
\Ran_{\dR}^{\un}
equipped with a morphism
\mathcal{C}^{\ren}\to\mathcal{C}
, such that for any finite set
I
the functor
(\mathcal{C}^{\ren})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\to\mathcal{C}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
realizes
(\mathcal{C}^{\ren})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
as the renormalization of
\mathcal{C}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
constructed in Proposition
2.7.1
.

Proof.

The almost ULA generation condition implies in particular that each
\mathcal{C}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
is almost compactly generated, and conditions (ii-iii) imply that the structure functor
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})\to\mathsf{D}(X^{J})
has finite homological amplitude for any map of finite sets
I\to J
. Applying the description of
\mathsf{ShvCat}(\Ran^{\un}_{\dR})^{\naive}
from Proposition
3.2.1
and the construction of Proposition
2.7.1
, we obtain an object of

\underset{I\in\mathsf{fSet}}{\operatorname{lax}\,\lim}\ \mathsf{D}(X^{I})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod},

which lies in the image of
\mathsf{ShvCat}(\Ran^{\un}_{\dR})^{\naive}
by Propositions
2.8.2
and
2.10.3
.

∎

Proposition 3.9.2
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be an object of
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}
. Suppose that we are given a t-structure on
\mathcal{C}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
for any finite set
I
satisfying the conditions of Proposition
3.9.1
, and that for any finite sets
I_{1},I_{2}
, the image of the composite functor

\mathcal{C}_{X^{I_{1}}_{\dR}}^{+}\times\mathcal{C}_{X^{I_{2}}_{\dR}}^{+}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{X^{I_{1}}_{\dR}}\otimes\mathcal{C}_{X^{I_{2}}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}((X^{I_{1}}\times X^{I_{2}})_{\disj})\underset{\mathsf{D}(X^{I_{1}\sqcup I_{2}})}{\otimes}\mathcal{C}_{X^{I_{1}\sqcup I_{2}}_{\dR}}

is contained in

(\mathsf{D}((X^{I_{1}}\times X^{I_{2}})_{\disj})\underset{\mathsf{D}(X^{I_{1}\sqcup I_{2}})}{\otimes}\mathcal{C}_{X^{I_{1}\sqcup I_{2}}_{\dR}})^{+}.

Then the sheaf of categories
\mathcal{C}^{\ren}
on
\Ran_{\dR}^{\un}
from Proposition
3.9.1
naturally lifts to an object of
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}
.

Proof.

Apply Proposition
3.2.2
to view
\mathcal{C}
as a lax symmetric monoidal section of (
3.2.1
). By Proposition
2.9.2
, for any object
p:I\to J
in
\Part_{\un}
, the category

\mathcal{C}_{U(p)}=\mathsf{D}(U(p))\underset{\mathsf{D}(X^{I})}{\otimes}\mathcal{C}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

is almost ULA generated over
U(p)
with respect to the unique t-structure that makes
\mathcal{C}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\to\mathcal{C}_{U(p)}
t-exact. Given objects
p_{1}:I_{1}\to J_{1}
,
p_{2}:I_{2}\to J_{2}
and a morphism
\epsilon:p_{1}\to p_{2}
in
\Part_{\un}
, we claim that the corresponding structure functor
\mathcal{C}_{U(p_{1})}\to\mathcal{C}_{U(p_{2})}
has finite homological amplitude. This follows from hypotheses (ii-iii) of Proposition
3.9.1
and the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{X^{I_{1}}_{\dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{X^{I_{2}}_{\dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{U(p_{1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{U(p_{2})},}

since the vertical functors are t-exact with fully faithful and left t-exact right adjoints. Similarly, the structure functor
\mathcal{C}_{U(p_{1})}\otimes\mathcal{C}_{U(p_{2})}\to\mathcal{C}_{U(p_{1}\sqcup p_{2})}
coming from the lax symmetric monoidal structure sends
\mathcal{C}_{U(p_{1})}^{+}\times\mathcal{C}_{U(p_{2})}^{+}
into
\mathcal{C}_{U(p_{1}\sqcup p_{2})}^{+}
, which we can see from the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{X^{I_{1}}_{\dR}}\otimes\mathcal{C}_{X^{I_{1}}_{\dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}((X^{I_{1}}\times X^{I_{2}})_{\disj})\underset{\mathsf{D}(X^{I_{1}\sqcup I_{2}})}{\otimes}\mathcal{C}_{X^{I_{1}\sqcup I_{2}}_{\dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{U(p_{1})}\otimes\mathcal{C}_{U(p_{2})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{U(p_{1}\sqcup p_{2})}.}

Combining the above observations, it follows that applying (
2.6.1
) termwise to the categories
\mathcal{C}_{U(p)}
yields another lax symmetric monoidal section of (
3.2.1
). This section satisfies condition (i) of Proposition
3.2.2
by Proposition
2.10.3
, and condition (ii) is trivial to verify.

∎

Let
(\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact})^{\otimes}_{\tstr}
be the pseudo-tensor category whose objects consist of
\mathcal{C}
in
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}
together with a t-structure on
\mathcal{C}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
for every finite set
I
, and whose
n
-ary morphisms are the same as those in the symmetric monoidal category
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}
, i.e. we do not impose any t-exactness conditions on functors.

Denote by

(\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact})^{\otimes}_{\aULA}\subset(\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact})^{\otimes}_{\tstr}

the pseudo-tensor subcategory whose objects
\mathcal{C}
satisfy the conditions of Propositions
3.9.1
and
3.9.2
, and whose
n
-ary morphisms
(\mathcal{C}_{1},\cdots\!,\mathcal{C}_{n})\to\mathcal{D}
satisfy the condition that the image of

(\mathcal{C}_{1})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{+}\times\cdots\times(\mathcal{C}_{n})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{+}\longrightarrow(\mathcal{C}_{1})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\underset{\mathsf{D}(X^{I})}{\otimes}\cdots\underset{\mathsf{D}(X^{I})}{\otimes}(\mathcal{C}_{n})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

is contained in
\mathcal{D}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{+}
for any finite set
I
.

Proposition 3.9.3
.

The assignment
\mathcal{C}\mapsto\mathcal{C}^{\ren}
of Proposition
3.9.2
naturally lifts to a morphism of pseudo-tensor categories

(\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact})^{\otimes}_{\aULA}\longrightarrow(\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact})^{\otimes}_{\aULA}.

Proof.

This is immediate, since the construction of Proposition
3.9.2
is induced by the morphism of pseudo-tensor categories (
2.6.1
).

∎

4.
A local acyclity theorem

The main goal of this section is to prove Theorem
4.6.1
, which says in particular that the vacuum factorization module for a commutative algebra almost of finite type is almost ULA. From this we deduce Corollary
4.6.1.1
, which says that the category of factorization modules over a commutative algebra of finite type satisfies the conditions of Proposition
3.9.2
and hence can be renormalized, provided that the same is true for the category of commutative modules over that algebra.

4.1.
Representations as a factorization category

Below we collect some facts about the factorization category
\mathsf{Rep}(H)
, where
H
is an algebraic group.

The forgetful functor
\oblv_{H}:\mathsf{Rep}(H)\to\Vect
is symmetric monoidal, and hence gives rise to a morphism in
\mathsf{FactCat}
.

Proposition 4.1.1
.

For any finite set
I
, the functor

\oblv_{H}:\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(X^{I})

is conservative, and there is a unique t-structure on
\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
which makes this functor t-exact. The morphism
\oblv_{H}
admits a right adjoint
\coind_{H}
in
\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl}
, and for any finite set
I
, the functor

\coind_{H}:\mathsf{D}(X^{I})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

is t-exact.

Proof.

See Propositions 6.22.1 and 6.24.1 of
[
Ras21a
]
.

∎

Proposition 4.1.2
.

The factorization category
\mathsf{Rep}(H)
, with the t-structure on
\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
defined as above for each finite set
I
, satisfies the conditions of Proposition
3.9.2
, and

\mathsf{Rep}(H)^{\ren}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(H)

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

The t-structure on
\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
inherits compatibility with filtered colimits and right completeness from
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
via the conservative and t-exact functor
\oblv_{H}
. To see that this t-structure is almost compactly generated, it remains to prove that if an object
\mathcal{M}
is compact in
\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{\geq 0}
, then it is almost compact in
\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
. By the previous proposition, we have the
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
-linear adjunction

\oblv_{H}:\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\rightleftarrows\mathsf{D}(X^{I}):\coind_{H}

with both functors t-exact. Thus
\oblv_{H}(\mathcal{M})
is compact in
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})^{\geq 0}
, hence almost compact because
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
is almost compactly generated. Now Lemma
2.4.1
implies that
\mathcal{M}
is almost compact.

If
\{V_{\alpha}\}
is a set of compact generators for
\mathsf{Rep}(H)^{\otimes I}
, then
\{\Loc_{X^{I}}(V_{\alpha})\}
is a set of ULA generators for
\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
. Note that
\Loc_{X^{I}}
is t-exact up to shift, and in particular each of these objects is eventually coconnective. It follows that
\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
is almost ULA generated and that

(\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}})^{\ren}\tilde{\longrightarrow}\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}.

It is not difficult to see that the other conditions in Propositions
3.9.1
and
3.9.2
are are inherited from
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
via
\oblv_{H}
.

∎

4.2.
The t-structure on factorization modules

Let
A
be a commutative algebra in
\mathsf{Rep}(H)^{\leq 0}
.

Proposition 4.2.1
.

For any finite set
I
, there is a unique t-structure on
A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
which makes the forgetful functor

\oblv^{\fact}:A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

t-exact.

We will need the following lemma.

Lemma 4.2.2
.

If
Y
and
Z
are classical local complete intersection schemes and
f:Y\to Z
is finite flat, then
f^{!}:\mathsf{D}(Z)\to\mathsf{D}(Y)
is t-exact.

Proof.

First, we claim that the forgetful functor

\oblv_{Y}:\mathsf{D}(Y)\longrightarrow\mathsf{IndCoh}(Y)

is t-exact. Recall that for any scheme
Y
the functor
\oblv_{Y}
is left t-exact, so it suffices to prove right t-exactness. Since the claim is Zariski local on
Y
, we can assume that there exists a regular closed embedding
i:Y\to W
where
W
is smooth. The composite functor

\mathsf{D}(Y)\xrightarrow{i_{\dR,*}}\mathsf{D}(W)\xrightarrow{\oblv_{W}}\mathsf{IndCoh}(W)

is t-exact, and admits a nonnegative filtration with associated graded

\Sym_{\mathcal{O}_{W}}(\NN(Y/W))\otimes i_{*}\oblv_{Y}(-)

(here
\NN(Y/W)
denotes the normal bundle, and the tensor product denotes the action of
\mathsf{QCoh}(W)
on
\mathsf{IndCoh}(W)
). Since this associated graded contains
i_{*}\oblv_{Y}(-)
as a direct summand and
i_{*}
is t-exact and conservative, it follows that
\oblv_{Y}
is right t-exact.

Using the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}(Z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}(Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{IndCoh}(Z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{IndCoh}(Y),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oblv_{Z}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oblv_{Y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{!}}

it is therefore enough to prove that
f^{!}:\mathsf{IndCoh}(Z)\longrightarrow\mathsf{IndCoh}(Y)
is t-exact. This functor is left t-exact because
f
is finite. On the other hand, since
Y
is Gorenstein, so is the morphism
f
, which implies right t-exactness.

∎

Now we can prove the proposition.

Proof of Proposition
4.2.1
.

First, we claim that if such a t-structure exists on

A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fact,\nonuntl}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}},

then the proposition will follow. Namely, by Proposition
3.8.4
we have

A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fact,\nonuntl}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\cong A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\times\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}.

It therefore suffices to observe that the direct factor
0\times\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
is stable under truncation functors.

Thus we are reduced to proving the proposition for non-unital factorization modules. In that case, by
[
FG12
]
Corollary 7.3.5 we have the Koszul duality equivalence

A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fact,\nonuntl}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\tilde{\longrightarrow}(A\otimes\omega_{X}[-1])\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\ch}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}},

where
(A\otimes\omega_{X}[-1])
is the commutative chiral algebra attached to
A
. This equivalence intertwines the forgetful functors to
\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
. If
\mathcal{M}
is an object of

(A\otimes\omega_{X}[-1])\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\ch}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}},

we claim that the truncation
\tau^{\leq 0}\mathcal{M}
taken in
\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
admits a chiral
(A\otimes\omega_{X}[-1])
-module structure compatible with the morphism
\tau^{\leq 0}\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{M}
. It will then follow that

\Hom_{(A\otimes\omega_{X}[-1])\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\ch}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}}(\mathcal{N},\tau^{\leq 0}\mathcal{M})\tilde{\longrightarrow}\Hom_{(A\otimes\omega_{X}[-1])\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\ch}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}}(\mathcal{N},\mathcal{M})

for any
\mathcal{N}
which is connective in
\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
, since

\Hom_{\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}}(\mathcal{N},\tau^{>0}\mathcal{M})=0

and
\oblv_{A\otimes\omega_{X}[-1]}
is conservative.

Let
i:Z_{I}\to X\times X^{I}
denote the inclusion of the incidence divisor and
j:(X\times X^{I})_{\disj}\to X\times X^{I}
its complement, and write
p:Z_{I}\to X^{I}
for the projection. Note that by Lemma
4.2.2
, the functor

i_{\dR,*}p^{!}:\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X_{\dR}}\otimes\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

is t-exact. The structure map of
\mathcal{M}
is a morphism

j_{*}j^{*}((A\otimes\omega_{X}[-1])\boxtimes\mathcal{M})\longrightarrow i_{\dR,*}p^{!}\mathcal{M}

in
\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X_{\dR}}\otimes\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
. The object

j_{*}j^{*}((A\otimes\omega_{X}[-1])\boxtimes\tau^{\leq 0}\mathcal{M})

is connective, and hence the composition

j_{*}j^{*}((A\otimes\omega_{X}[-1])\boxtimes\tau^{\leq 0}\mathcal{M})\longrightarrow j_{*}j^{*}((A\otimes\omega_{X}[-1])\boxtimes\mathcal{M})\longrightarrow i_{\dR,*}p^{!}\mathcal{M}

factors uniquely through

\tau^{\leq 0}i_{\dR,*}p^{!}\mathcal{M}=i_{\dR,*}p^{!}\tau^{\leq 0}\mathcal{M}.

The same reasoning applies to
n
-ary chiral operations for
n>1
, which yields the desired chiral module structure on
\tau^{\leq 0}\mathcal{M}
.

∎

4.3.
Commutative factorization modules

We continue to assume that
A
is connective. Recall that
A
determines a commutative factorization algebra in the commutative factorization category
\mathsf{Rep}(H)
, so that in particular we obtain an object
A_{X^{I}}
in
\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
for any finite set
I
.

Lemma 4.3.1
.

For any finite set
I
, we have
H^{i}(A_{X^{I}})=0
for all
i>-\#I
. If
A
is eventually coconnective, then so is
A_{X^{I}}
.

Proof.

It suffices to prove both claims after applying the t-exact and conservative functor

\oblv_{H}:\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(X^{I}),

so we can assume that
H
is trivial.

The first assertion can be proved by the same argument as Lemma 6.24.3 of
[
Ras21a
]
.

The second assertion is clear from the Cousin filtration, whose subquotients have the form
A^{\otimes J}\otimes j_{*}j^{!}\omega_{Z(p)}
where
p:I\to J
is surjective and
j:Z(p)\to X^{I}
is the inclusion of the corresponding stratum.

∎

The symmetric monoidal functor

\ind_{A}:\mathsf{Rep}(H)\longrightarrow A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))

gives rise to a morphism of factorization categories. Since these symmetric monoidal categories are rigid, Lemma
3.4.2
says that
\ind_{A}
admits a right adjoint

\oblv_{A}:A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(H)

in
\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl}
.

Proposition 4.3.2
.

For any finite set
I
, the category
A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
admits a unique t-structure such that

\oblv_{A}:A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

is t-exact.

Proof.

The functor
\oblv_{A}
admits the
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
-linear left adjoint
\ind_{A}
, and the monad
\oblv_{A}\circ\ind_{A}
on
\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
is given by tensoring with the commutative algebra
A_{X^{I}}
. Since the tensor product in
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
is has homological amplitude
\#I
, the same holds for
\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
, so Lemma
4.3.1
implies that this monad is right t-exact. The proposition follows.

∎

The forgetful functor from commutative to factorization
A
-modules is a morphism

\oblv^{\operatorname{com}\to\operatorname{fact}}:A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))\longrightarrow A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))

in
\mathsf{FactCat}
. It is compatible with the forgetful functors to
\mathsf{Rep}(H)
, and in particular the functor

\oblv^{\operatorname{com}\to\operatorname{fact}}:A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

is t-exact for any finite set
I
.

By definition, the
vacuum factorization module

\Vac_{A}
in
A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))
is the unit object for the factorization structure. The unitality of
\oblv^{\operatorname{com}\to\operatorname{fact}}
implies that

\Vac_{A}=\oblv^{\operatorname{com}\to\operatorname{fact}}A.

4.4.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a symmetric monoidal DG category,
\mathcal{C}
an
\mathcal{A}
-module category, and
\mathcal{C}_{0}\subset\mathcal{C}
a (not necessarily stable) full subcategory. For any associative algebra
A
in
\mathcal{A}
, we can consider

A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathcal{C}_{0}):=A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathcal{C})\underset{\mathcal{C}}{\times}\mathcal{C}_{0}.

We will use the following formal lemma.

Lemma 4.4.1
.

Suppose that
A\to B
is a morphism of associative algebras in
\mathcal{A}
with the property that

\Hom_{\mathcal{C}}(B^{\otimes n}\otimes M,N)\longrightarrow\Hom_{\mathcal{C}}(A^{\otimes n}\otimes M,N)

is an isomorphism for all
n\geq 1
and all
M,N
in
\mathcal{C}_{0}
. Then the restriction of scalars functor

B\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathcal{C}_{0})\longrightarrow A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathcal{C}_{0})

is an equivalence.

Lemma 4.4.2
.

Fix
m\geq 0
. Let
A\to B
be a morphism of connective commutative algebras in
\mathsf{Rep}(H)
such that
H^{i}(A)\tilde{\to}H^{i}(B)
for all
i\geq-m
. Then for any finite set
I
, we have

H^{i}(A_{X^{I}})\tilde{\longrightarrow}H^{i}(B_{X^{I}})

for all
i\geq-\#I-m
.

Proof.

As in the proof of Lemma
4.3.1
, we can assume that
H
is trivial.

Let us treat the case
\#I=2
for simplicity, the general case being similar. Fix
i\geq-2-m
. Our hypothesis implies that

H^{i+1}(A\otimes\Delta_{\dR,*}\omega_{X})=H^{i+2}(A)\otimes\Delta_{\dR,*}\omega_{X}[-1]\longrightarrow H^{i+2}(B)\otimes\Delta_{\dR,*}\omega_{X}[-1]=H^{i+1}(B\otimes\Delta_{\dR,*}\omega_{X})

is an isomorphism. Since
A
and
B
are connective, we have

H^{i+2}(A\otimes A)\tilde{\longrightarrow}H^{i+2}(B\otimes B),

from which we deduce that

H^{i}(A\otimes A\otimes j_{*}j^{*}\omega_{X^{2}})\tilde{\longrightarrow}H^{i}(B\otimes B\otimes j_{*}j^{*}\omega_{X^{2}}).

On the other hand, the surjectivity of
H^{i+2}(A\otimes A)\to H^{i+2}(A)
implies that the connecting homomorphism

H^{i}(A\otimes A\otimes j_{*}j^{*}\omega_{X^{2}})\longrightarrow H^{i+1}(A\otimes\Delta_{\dR,*}\omega_{X})

for the Cousin triangle is surjective, and likewise for
B
, which implies the desired isomorphism.

∎

Lemma 4.4.3
.

In the situation of the previous lemma, the restriction of scalars functors

B\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{\leq 0,\geq-m}\longrightarrow A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{\leq 0,\geq-m}

and

B\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{\leq 0,\geq-m}\longrightarrow A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{\leq 0,\geq-m}

are equivalences.

Proof.

For the commutative case, we saw in the proof of Proposition
4.3.2
that

A_{X^{I}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}})\tilde{\longrightarrow}A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}.

For any
\mathcal{M}
in
\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{\leq 0,\geq-m}
and any
n\geq 1
, Lemmas
4.3.1
and
4.4.2
imply that

H^{i}(A_{X^{I}}^{\otimes n}\overset{!}{\otimes}\mathcal{M})\tilde{\longrightarrow}H^{i}(B_{X^{I}}^{\otimes n}\overset{!}{\otimes}\mathcal{M})

for all
i\geq-m
, since the tensor product in
\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
has cohomological amplitude
\#I
. Now apply Lemma
4.4.1
.

For the second equivalence, note that it suffices to prove the corresponding claim for non-unital factorization modules. Namely, by Proposition
3.8.4
and the definition of the t-structure on unital modules, we have

(A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{X^{I}_{\dR}})^{\leq 0,\geq-m}=A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\cap(A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fact,\nonuntl}_{X^{I}_{\dR}})^{\leq 0,\geq-m}.

As for the non-unital case, we use the equivalence

A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fact,\nonuntl}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\tilde{\longrightarrow}(A\otimes\omega_{X}[-1])\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\ch}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}},

which commutes with the forgetful functors to
\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
and in particular is t-exact. The hypotheses imply that
A\otimes\omega_{X}[-1]
and
B\otimes\omega_{X}[-1]
are connective in
\mathsf{D}(X)
, and that

H^{i}(A\otimes\omega_{X}[-1])\tilde{\longrightarrow}H^{i}(B\otimes\omega_{X}[-1])

for
i\geq-m
. Letting
\Ran_{I}
denote the space of finite subsets of
X
equipped with an
I
-tuple of marked points, the map

(\Ran\times\Ran_{I})_{\disj}\longrightarrow\Ran_{I}

given by disjoint union is étale. It follows that for any
\mathcal{M}
in
\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{\leq 0}
, any
n\geq 1
, and any nonempty finite set
I^{\prime}
, we have

H^{i}(((A\otimes\omega_{X}[-1])^{\otimes n}\overset{\ch}{\otimes}\mathcal{M})|^{!}_{X^{I^{\prime}\sqcup I}})\tilde{\longrightarrow}H^{i}(((B\otimes\omega_{X}[-1])^{\otimes n}\overset{\ch}{\otimes}\mathcal{M})|^{!}_{X^{I^{\prime}\sqcup I}})

for all
i\geq-m
. Here we are restricting along the natural map
X^{I^{\prime}\sqcup I}\to\Ran_{I}
, and
\overset{\ch}{\otimes}
denotes the chiral action of
\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{\Ran_{\dR}}
on
\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{\Ran_{I,\dR}}
(cf.
[
FG12
]
§7.2.4). Now the claim follows from Lemma
4.4.1
.

∎

Proposition 4.4.4
.

For any commutative algebra
A
in
\mathsf{Rep}(H)^{\leq 0}
, the t-exact functor
\oblv^{\operatorname{com}\to\operatorname{fact}}
restricts to an equivalence

(A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}})^{\heartsuit}\tilde{\longrightarrow}(A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}})^{\heartsuit}.

Proof.

Consider the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{0}(A)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{\heartsuit}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{0}(A)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{\heartsuit}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{\heartsuit}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{\heartsuit}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oblv^{\operatorname{com}\to\operatorname{fact}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oblv^{\operatorname{com}\to\operatorname{fact}}}

where the vertical functors, given by restriction of scalars along
A\to H^{0}(A)
, are equivalences by Lemma
4.4.3
. Thus we can assume that
A
is classical, in which case it is immediate from the definitions that
\oblv^{\operatorname{com}\to\operatorname{fact}}
is fully faithful at the level of abelian categories. It remains to prove essential surjectivity.

Recall the t-exact equivalence

A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fact,\nonuntl}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\tilde{\longrightarrow}(A\otimes\omega_{X}[-1])\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\ch}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}.

Writing
i:Z_{I}\to X\times X^{I}
and
p:Z_{I}\to X^{I}
for the inclusion of the incidence divisor and the projection as before, the structure map of a chiral module
\mathcal{M}
has the form

j_{*}j^{*}((A\otimes\omega_{X}[-1])\boxtimes\mathcal{M})\longrightarrow i_{\dR,*}p^{!}\mathcal{M}.

Objects in the essential image of

\oblv^{\com\to\fact}:A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\nonuntl}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{\heartsuit}\longrightarrow A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fact,\nonuntl}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{\heartsuit}

correspond under the above equivalence to those
\mathcal{M}
such that the composite map

(4.4.1)

(A\otimes\omega_{X}[-1])\boxtimes\mathcal{M}\longrightarrow j_{*}j^{*}((A\otimes\omega_{X}[-1])\boxtimes\mathcal{M})\longrightarrow i_{\dR,*}p^{!}\mathcal{M}

vanishes. But notice that the functor
i^{*}_{\dR}
is well-defined on
(A\otimes\omega_{X}[-1])\boxtimes\mathcal{M}
, and moreover

i^{*}_{\dR}((A\otimes\omega_{X}[-1])\boxtimes\mathcal{M})=A\otimes\mathcal{M}[1].

Since
A
is classical and the D-module underlying
\mathcal{M}
belongs to
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})^{\heartsuit}
, the map

A\otimes\mathcal{M}[1]\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}

corresponding to (
4.4.1
) vanishes. Thus

A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\nonuntl}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{\heartsuit}=A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fact,\nonuntl}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{\heartsuit}.

The assertion for unital modules follows immediately, since

A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{\heartsuit}=A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\nonuntl}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{\heartsuit}\cap A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

as subcategories of

A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\nonuntl}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}},

and likewise for factorization modules.

∎

4.5.
Local acyclity of the vacuum module

We continue to let
A
denote a commutative algebra in
\mathsf{Rep}(H)^{\leq 0}
. For any finite set
I
, the functor

\oblv^{\operatorname{com}\to\operatorname{fact}}:A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

is t-exact, and hence its (not necessarily continuous) right adjoint
\coind^{\fact\to\com}
is left t-exact.

Proposition 4.5.1
.

For any finite set
I
, the following conditions are equivalent:

(i)

the
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
-linear functor

\oblv^{\operatorname{com}\to\operatorname{fact}}:A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

preserves almost ULA objects;

(ii)

the functor

\coind^{\fact\to\com}:A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{+}\longrightarrow A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{+}

preserves filtered colimits bounded uniformly from below and is
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})^{+}
-linear;

(iii)

the vacuum module
\Vac_{A,X^{I}}
is almost ULA in

A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}.

Moreover, these equivalent conditions hold for the pair
(A,H)
if and only if they hold for
(A,1)
, i.e.
A
viewed as a commutative algebra in
\Vect^{\leq 0}
.

Proof.

Since
A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
is ULA generated, Lemma
4.5.2
below implies that (i) is equivalent to (ii).

Condition (i) implies (iii) because the unit object in
A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
is ULA.

Consider the commutative square of
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
-modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oblv^{\operatorname{com}\to\operatorname{fact}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oblv_{H}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oblv^{\operatorname{com}\to\operatorname{fact}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oblv_{H}}

The horizontal functors are t-exact and conservative, and both admit
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
-linear and t-exact right adjoints, induced by the right adjoint

\coind_{H}:\mathsf{D}(X^{I})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

of
\oblv_{H}
. In particular, the horizontal functors preserve almost ULA objects. Applying Lemma
2.4.1
, it follows immediately that (ii) implies (i), and that (iii) is equivalent to (iv).

As shown in the proof of Proposition
4.3.2
, the object
A_{X^{I}}
is ULA, hence almost ULA, in
A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
. Thus (i) implies (iii), and for the same reason (ii) implies (iv).

To complete the proof, it suffices to show that (iv) implies (ii). We will prove that the
\mathsf{IndCoh}(X^{I})
-linear functor

\oblv^{\operatorname{com}\to\operatorname{fact}}:A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{X^{I}}\longrightarrow A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{X^{I}}

preserves almost compact objects, which will follow if we can show that

\oblv^{\operatorname{com}\to\operatorname{fact}}:A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{X^{I}}^{\geq m}\longrightarrow(A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{X^{I}})^{\geq m}

preserves compact objects for any
m\in\mathbb{Z}
. As the claim is local on
X
, we can assume for simplicity that
X
is affine. Then the DG category
A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{X^{I}}
is compactly generated by the object
A_{X^{I}}
, and hence the (non-stable) category
A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{X^{I}}^{\geq m}
is compactly generated by the objects
\tau^{\geq m}(A_{X^{I}}[-i])
for
i\geq 0
. Since
\oblv^{\operatorname{com}\to\operatorname{fact}}
is t-exact, we have

\oblv^{\operatorname{com}\to\operatorname{fact}}\tau^{\geq m}(A_{X^{I}}[-i])=\tau^{\geq m}(\Vac_{A,X^{I}}),

and the right side is compact in
(A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{X^{I}})^{\geq m}
by hypothesis.

∎

Lemma 4.5.2
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
are
\mathsf{D}(S)
-modules with compatible t-structures, and that
F:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
is
\mathsf{D}(S)
-linear and t-exact. In particular, the (not necessarily continuous) right adjoint
F^{\RR}:\mathcal{D}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}
is left t-exact. If

F^{\RR}:\mathcal{D}^{+}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}^{+}

preserves filtered colimits bounded uniformly from below and is
\mathsf{D}(S)^{+}
-linear, then
F
preserves almost ULA objects. If we assume in addition that
\mathcal{C}
is ULA generated, then the converse holds.

Proof.

Cf. Proposition B.7.1 in
[
Ras21a
]
for the corresponding statement about ULA objects. Assume that
F^{\RR}
preserves filtered colimits bounded uniformly from below, which implies that
F
preserves almost compact objects. We have the commutative square of t-exact functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes_{\mathsf{D}(S)}\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes_{\mathsf{D}(S)}\mathcal{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id\otimes F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ind_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ind_{\mathcal{D}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}

where the vertical functors are conservative and have the continuous right adjoints
\oblv_{\mathcal{C}}
and
\oblv_{\mathcal{D}}
. Thus Lemma
2.4.1
implies that the upper horizontal functor preserves almost compact objects, from which it follows that
F
preserves almost ULA objects.

Now assume that
\mathcal{C}
is ULA generated and that
F
preserves almost ULA objects. The latter assumption implies that for any ULA object
c
in
\mathcal{C}
, the object

F(\oblv_{\mathcal{C}}(c))=\oblv_{\mathcal{D}}(F(c))

is almost compact. Since
\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes_{\mathsf{D}(S)}\mathcal{C}
is generated as an
\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)
-module by objects of the form
\oblv_{\mathcal{C}}(c)
, where
c
is ULA in
\mathcal{C}
, it follows that

\id\otimes F:\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\underset{\mathsf{D}(S)}{\otimes}\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\underset{\mathsf{D}(S)}{\otimes}\mathcal{D}

preserves almost compact objects, and hence
F
does as well by the above commutative square. It follows immediately that

F^{\RR}:\mathcal{D}^{+}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}^{+}

preserves filtered colimits bounded uniformly from below. This functor is automatically lax
\mathsf{D}(S)^{+}
-linear, so it remains to prove that this structure is strict. Passing to right adjoints in the square above, we obtain another commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes_{\mathsf{D}(S)}\mathcal{D})^{+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)\otimes_{\mathsf{D}(S)}\mathcal{C})^{+},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{\RR}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oblv_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oblv_{\mathcal{D}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\id\otimes F)^{\RR}}

where the vertical functors are conservative and
\mathsf{D}(S)^{+}
-linear. Thus it suffices to show that the lower horizontal functor is strictly
\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)^{+}
-linear. The claim is local on
S
, which we can therefore assume is affine. But then
\mathsf{IndCoh}(S)^{+}
is generated by the unit object under finite colimits and filtered colimits bounded uniformly from below.

∎

4.6.

The following theorem plays a key technical role in this paper.

Theorem 4.6.1
.

If
A
is almost of finite type, then the equivalent conditions of Proposition
4.5.1
hold.

We also record the following important consequence of the theorem here.

Corollary 4.6.1.1
.

If
A
is of finite type and
A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
is coherent for every finite set
I
, then
A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))
satisfies the hypotheses of Theorem
3.9.2
. In particular, under these hypotheses
A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
is almost ULA generated for every finite set
I
.

Proof.

First, observe that the hypothesis that
A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
is coherent implies that it is almost ULA generated. This follows from Proposition
4.1.2
and the
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
-linear adjunction

\ind_{A}:\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\rightleftarrows A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}:\oblv_{A}.

Next, we claim that
A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
is coherent. By Lemma
2.4.7
, it suffices to show that if
M
is compact in
A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{\heartsuit}
, then
M
almost compact. By Proposition
4.4.4
, we have
M=\oblv^{\com\to\fact}N
for some
N
in
A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{\heartsuit}
, which is therefore almost compact since
A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
was assumed coherent. Now the theorem implies that
M
is almost compact.

Proposition
4.4.4
implies that the essential image of

\oblv^{\com\to\fact}:A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{\geq 0}\longrightarrow A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{\geq 0}

generates the target under colimits, which implies that
A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
is almost ULA generated.

Condition (ii) of Proposition
3.9.1
is satisfied by
A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))
because it holds for
\mathsf{Rep}(H)
, and the morphism

\oblv_{A}:A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(H)

in
\mathsf{ShvCat}(\Ran^{\un}_{\dR})
is conservative over
X^{I}_{\dR}
for every finite set
I
. As for condition (iii), note that Lemma
4.3.1
implies that it holds for
A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))
because
A
was assumed to be of finite type, and in particular eventually coconnective. Since

\oblv^{\com\to\fact}:A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))\longrightarrow A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))

is a morphism in
\mathsf{ShvCat}(\Ran^{\un}_{\dR})
, the condition for
A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))
follows from Proposition
4.4.4
. The remaining condition in Proposition
3.9.2
follows from the corresponding property of
A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))
in a similar fashion.

∎

We remark that the hypothesis in the corollary holds if
\Spec A
is a homogeneous space for
H
. Indeed, in that case we have

A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))\cong\mathsf{Rep}(H_{y})

where
y\in(\Spec A)(k)
is any geometric point and
H_{y}
is its stabilizer. On the other hand, if
H
is the trivial group, then
A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
is usually not coherent.

4.7.
The case of a symmetric algebra

For the remainder of this section, we assume that
H
is trivial unless otherwise specified. As shown in Proposition
4.5.1
, there no loss of generality when proving Theorem
4.6.1
under this assumption.

Recall that the forgetful functor from chiral algebras to Lie-
*
algebras on
X
admits a left adjoint
\UU^{*\to\ch}
, called the
chiral enveloping algebra
. We emphasize that in this paper, all chiral algebras are non-unital, and hence our
\UU^{*\to\ch}

is the augmentation ideal of the unital version of the chiral enveloping algebra.

Below we prove the key special case of Theorem
4.6.1
where
A
is a symmetric algebra with finitely many generators. An important point is that in this case, the chiral algebra corresponding to
A
is also the chiral enveloping algebra of a Lie-
*
algebra.

Lemma 4.7.1
.

Let
A:=\Sym V
where
V
is a complex of vector spaces. Write
A^{+}:=\Sym^{>0}V
for the augmentation ideal of
A
, viewed as a non-unital commutative algebra. Then we have a canonical identification of chiral algebras

\UU^{*\to\ch}(V\otimes\omega_{X}[-1])\tilde{\longrightarrow}A^{+}\otimes\omega_{X}[-1],

where
V\otimes\omega_{X}[-1]
has the abelian Lie-
*
algebra structure and
A^{+}\otimes\omega_{X}[-1]
has the commutative chiral algebra structure induced by the non-unital commutative algebra structure on
A^{+}
.

Proof.

Since
A^{+}\otimes\omega_{X}[-1]
is commutative as a chiral algebra, the underlying Lie-
*
algebra

\oblv^{\ch\to*}(A^{+}\otimes\omega_{X}[-1])

is abelian, and we have a morphism of Lie-
*
algebras

V\otimes\omega_{X}[-1]\longrightarrow\oblv^{\ch\to*}(A^{+}\otimes\omega_{X}[-1]).

By adjunction, we obtain a morphism of chiral algebras

\UU^{*\to\ch}(V\otimes\omega_{X}[-1])\longrightarrow A^{+}\otimes\omega_{X}[-1],

which we claim is an isomorphism. This can be seen by passing to the associated graded of the PBW filtration on the chiral enveloping algebra, cf.
[
FG12
]
Corollary 6.5.2.

∎

Recall that for a given Lie-
*
algebra
L
, Lie-
*
modules for
L
assemble into an object
L\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{*}
in
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}
. We remark that for any finite set
I
, the unit object in
L\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{*}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
is the trivial module
\triv_{L}(\omega_{X^{I}})
.

Similarly, given a chiral algebra
B
, chiral modules for
B
form an object
B\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\ch}
in
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}
. One way to see this is to use the Koszul duality equivalence

B\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\ch}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\tilde{\longrightarrow}\CC^{\ch}(B)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fact}_{X^{I}_{\dR}},

where
\CC^{\ch}(B):=\AddUnit(\CC^{\ch}_{+}(B))
is obtained by adjoining a unit to the non-unital factorization algebra
\CC^{\ch}_{+}(B)
Koszul dual to
B
(here we also used Proposition
3.8.2
). This equivalence is
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
-linear and commutes with the forgetful functors to
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
. We emphasize that the unit object for the resulting factorization structure on
B\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\ch}
corresponds to
\Vac_{\CC^{\ch}(B)}
, and in particular the same object of
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
underlies both.

For any Lie-
*
algebra
L
on
X
, we have an adjunction

\ind^{*\to\ch}:L\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{*}\rightleftarrows\UU^{*\to\ch}(L)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\ch}:\oblv^{\ch\to*}

in
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}_{\laxuntl}
, with
\ind^{*\to\ch}
being strictly unital.

Lemma 4.7.2
.

If
A=\Sym V
where
V
is a connective complex of vector spaces with finite-dimensional total cohomology, then
\Vac_{A,X^{I}}
is almost ULA in
A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
.

Proof.

Let
A^{+}:=\Sym^{>0}V
be as in Lemma
4.7.1
, so by combining the previous lemma and Koszul duality, we obtain a
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
-linear equivalence

A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\tilde{\longrightarrow}(A^{+}\otimes\omega_{X}[-1])\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\ch}_{X^{I}_{\dR}},

which is compatible with the forgetful functors to
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
and in particular is t-exact. Now consider the
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
-linear adjunction

\ind^{*\to\ch}:(V\otimes\omega_{X}[-1])\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{*}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\rightleftarrows(A^{+}\otimes\omega_{X}[-1])\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\ch}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}:\oblv^{\ch\to*}.

The functor
\oblv^{\ch\to*}
intertwines the forgetful functors to
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
and hence is t-exact. It follows formally that
\ind^{*\to\ch}
sends objects which are almost ULA and eventually coconnective to almost ULA objects. Since
\ind^{*\to\ch}
is unital, it therefore suffices to show that
\triv_{V\otimes\omega_{X}[-1]}(\omega_{X^{I}})
is almost ULA in
(V\otimes\omega_{X}[-1])\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{*}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
(of course it is eventually coconnective, being concentrated in cohomological degree
-\#I
).

For this, we recall that
\triv_{V\otimes\omega_{X}[-1]}(\omega_{X^{I}})
has the natural nonnegative filtration whose
n^{\text{th}}
associated graded piece is

M_{n}:=\ind_{V\otimes\omega_{X}[-1]}(\Sym^{*,n}(V\otimes\omega_{X})\overset{*}{\otimes}(\Delta_{I})_{\dR,*}\omega_{X^{I}}),

where
\Delta_{I}:X^{I}\to\Ran_{I}
is the main diagonal and

\ind_{V\otimes\omega_{X}[-1]}:\mathsf{D}(\Ran_{I})\longrightarrow(V\otimes\omega_{X}[-1])\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{*}(\mathsf{D}(\Ran_{I}))

is the induction functor.

The lemma will follow if we prove these two assertions:

(i)

For any
n\geq 0
, the object
M_{n}
is ULA over
X^{I}
.

(ii)

Given
m\in\mathbb{Z}
, for
n
sufficiently large we have

\Hom(M_{n},N)=0

for any
N
in
((V\otimes\omega_{X}[-1])\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{*}_{X^{I}_{\dR}})^{\geq m}
.

For claim (i), note that
\ind_{V\otimes\omega_{X}[-1]}
preserves ULA objects, being left adjoint to the
D(X^{I})
-linear functor
\oblv_{V\otimes\omega_{X}[-1]}
. The object

(V\otimes\omega_{X})^{\overset{*}{\otimes}n}\overset{*}{\otimes}(\Delta_{I})_{\dR,*}\omega_{X^{I}}

is the de Rham direct image of
V^{\otimes n}\otimes\omega_{X^{n}\times X^{I}}
along the proper map

X^{n}\times X^{I}\longrightarrow\Ran\times\Ran_{I}\longrightarrow\Ran_{I},

hence is ULA over
X^{I}
. It follows that the object

\Sym^{*,n}(V\otimes\omega_{X})\overset{*}{\otimes}(\Delta_{I})_{\dR,*}\omega_{X^{I}}

obtained by taking coinvariants for the symmetric group
\Sigma_{n}
is also ULA over
X^{I}
.

Finally, for (ii) it is enough to show that given
m\in\mathbb{Z}
, for
n
sufficiently large we have

\Hom_{\mathsf{D}(\Ran_{I})}((V\otimes\omega_{X})^{\overset{*}{\otimes}n}\overset{*}{\otimes}\Delta_{I,*}\omega_{X^{I}},(\Delta_{I})_{\dR,*}N)=0

for any
N\in\mathsf{D}(X^{I})^{\geq m}
(since the same will therefore hold for the
\Sigma_{n}
-coinvariants

\Sym^{*,n}(V\otimes\omega_{X})\overset{*}{\otimes}(\Delta_{I})_{\dR,*}\omega_{X^{I}}

of the first object). Given nonempty finite
I^{\prime}
, define
Z
to be the fiber product

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{n}\times X^{I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{I^{\prime}\sqcup I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Ran_{I}.}

By base change, the object

((V\otimes\omega_{X})^{\overset{*}{\otimes}n}\overset{*}{\otimes}(\Delta_{I})_{\dR,*}\omega_{X^{I}})|^{!}_{X^{I^{\prime}\sqcup I}}

is the de Rham direct image of
V^{\otimes n}\otimes\omega_{X^{n}\times X^{I}}
along the finite map
Z\to X^{I^{\prime}\sqcup I}
, hence is concentrated in cohomological degrees
\leq-n-\#I
. Considering the same cartesian square in the case
n=0
shows that
((\Delta_{I})_{\dR,*}N)|^{!}_{X^{I^{\prime}\sqcup I}}
is concentrated in cohomological degrees
\geq-m
. The claim follows.

∎

4.8.
Filtrations and the associated graded

Let
A
be a commutative algebra in
\Vect^{\leq 0}
equipped with a nonnegative filtration. The functor

\gr:A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fil}\to\gr(A)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\gr}

which takes the associated graded module is symmetric monoidal, as is the forgetful functor

\oblv^{\fil}:A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fil}\longrightarrow A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}.

In particular, both give rise to morphisms in
\mathsf{FactCat}
.

We will write

A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fil}_{\geq 0}\subset A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fil}

for the full symmetric monoidal subcategory consisting of nonnegatively filtered modules (not to be confused with a constraint on cohomological degree, which we indicate with a superscript), and likewise for graded modules.

We also observe that the category
(\Vect^{\gr})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
has a grading by “total degree fiberwise over
X^{I}
,” characterized by the condition that the natural conservative functor

(\Vect^{\gr})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\Vect^{\gr}\otimes D(X^{I})

respects this grading. We will write
M_{\td=i}
for the graded component of
M
of total degree
i
.

Lemma 4.8.1
.

Let
B
be a nonnegatively graded connective commutative algebra, and suppose we are given an inverse system

\cdots\to M_{3}\to M_{2}\to M_{1}

in
B\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fact}(\Vect^{\gr})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
such that for each
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, the inverse system

\cdots\to(\oblv_{B}M_{3})_{\td=i}\to(\oblv_{B}M_{2})_{\td=i}\to(\oblv_{B}M_{1})_{\td=i}

in
(\Vect^{\gr})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
stabilizes. Then the forgetful functor

\oblv_{B}:B\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fact}(\Vect^{\gr})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow(\Vect^{\gr})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

preserves the limit
\lim_{n}M_{n}
.

Proof.

By Proposition
3.8.4
, the inclusion

B\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fact}(\Vect^{\gr})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow B\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fact,\nonuntl}(\Vect^{\gr})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

preserves limits. Applying the equivalence

B\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fact,\nonuntl}(\Vect^{\gr})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\tilde{\longrightarrow}(B\otimes\omega_{X}[-1])\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\ch}(\Vect^{\gr})_{X^{I}_{\dR}},

it suffices to check the corresponding claim in the category of non-unital chiral modules. The forgetful functor

(B\otimes\omega_{X}[-1])\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\ch}((\Vect^{\gr})_{\Ran_{I,\dR}})\longrightarrow(\Vect^{\gr})_{\Ran_{I,\dR}}

preserves limits, so it is enough to prove that the limit
\lim_{n}\oblv_{B}M_{n}
taken in
(\Vect^{\gr})_{\Ran_{I,\dR}}
belongs to the full subcategory
(\Vect^{\gr})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
. But this is clear, since for each graded component of fixed total degree the limit stabilizes and hence belongs to
(\Vect^{\gr})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
.

∎

Corollary 4.8.1.1
.

With
B
as in the lemma, suppose we are given a sequence of objects
Q_{1},Q_{2},\cdots
in
B\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fact}(\Vect^{\gr})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
such that for each
i\geq 1
, the object
\oblv_{B}Q_{i}
in
(\Vect^{\gr})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
is concentrated in total degree
\geq i
. Then the map

\bigoplus_{i\geq 1}Q_{i}\longrightarrow\prod_{i\geq 1}Q_{i}

in
B\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fact}(\Vect^{\gr})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Applying the lemma to the inverse system with terms

\prod_{i=1}^{n}Q_{i},

we see that the product

\prod_{i\geq 1}Q_{i}

is preserved by
\oblv_{B}
. Now the corollary follows from the observation that the map

\bigoplus_{i\geq 1}\oblv_{B}Q_{i}\longrightarrow\prod_{i\geq 1}\oblv_{B}Q_{i}

in
\Vect^{\gr}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
is an isomorphism, since in each graded component of fixed total degree, all but finitely many factors in the product vanish.

∎

Lemma 4.8.2
.

Fix a nonnegatively filtered connective commutative algebra
A
. If the conditions of Proposition
4.5.1
hold for
\gr(A)
, then they hold for
\oblv^{\fil}A
.

Proof.

Step 1:
With
B
as in Lemma
4.8.1
, fix
m\in\mathbb{Z}
and suppose that
P
is a compact object in
B\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fact}(\Vect^{\gr})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{\geq m}
. We claim that there exists
r\geq 0
such that for any object
Q
of
B\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fact}(\Vect^{\gr})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{\geq m}
with
\oblv_{B}Q
concentrated in total degree
\geq r
, we have

\Hom_{B\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fact}(\Vect^{\gr})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}}(P,Q)=0.

Otherwise we could choose a sequence
Q_{1},Q_{2},\cdots
in
B\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fact}(\Vect^{\gr})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{\geq m}
such that
\oblv_{B}Q_{i}
is concentrated in total degree
\geq i
and

\Hom_{B\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fact}(\Vect^{\gr})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}}(P,Q_{i})\neq 0

for any
i\geq 1
. By Corollary
4.8.1.1
, this contradicts the compactness of
P
in
B\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fact}(\Vect^{\gr})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{\geq m}
.

Step 2:
By Lemma
3.4.2
, the functor

\gr:(\Vect^{\fil})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow(\Vect^{\gr})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

admits a right adjoint which is
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
-linear and compatible with factorization. In particular, this lifts to a
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
-linear adjunction

A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\Vect^{\fil})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\rightleftarrows\gr(A)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\Vect^{\gr})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}.

We define a functorial
descending
filtration on any
M
in
A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\Vect^{\fil})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
as follows. Let
F_{0}:=M
, and for any
n\geq 0
define

F_{n+1}:=\fib(F_{n}\to\gr F_{n})

(here we are suppressing from the notation the right adjoint of
\gr
, which commutes with the forgetful functors to
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
). We claim that if
M
is concentrated in total degree
\geq 0
then

(\oblv_{A}M)_{\td=0}\longrightarrow(\oblv_{A}\gr M)_{\td=0}

is an isomorphism. It will then follow inductively that
F_{n}
is concentrated in total degree
\geq n
for any
n\geq 0
, and hence that

M\tilde{\longrightarrow}\lim_{n}M/F_{n}

by Lemma
4.8.1
.

Recall that
X^{I}
is stratified with strata
Z(p)
indexed by surjections
p:I\to J
. Using the Cousin filtration, it is enough to check the claim over each
Z(p)
. There we are working in the multifiltered category
(\Vect^{\fil})^{\otimes J}\otimes\mathsf{D}(Z(p))
with its standard grading by total degree, and the claim is evident.

Step 3:
Let
V:=\ind_{X^{I}}(\Vac_{A,X^{I}})
, an object of
A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\Vect^{\fil})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
, so the lemma will follow if we prove that
\oblv^{\fil}V
is almost compact in
A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
. Fixing
m\in\mathbb{Z}
, we must show that

\Hom_{A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}}(\oblv^{\fil}V,N)

preserves filtered colimits when viewed as a functor of
N
in
(A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{X^{I}_{\dR}})^{\geq m}
. Note that the functor

\oblv^{\fil}:\Vect^{\fil}_{\geq 0}\longrightarrow\Vect

admits a symmetric monoidal right adjoint
\triv^{\fil}_{\geq 0}
, which equips a vector space with the trivial nonnegative filtration. In particular, this lifts to a
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
-linear adjunction

\oblv^{\fil}:A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\Vect^{\fil}_{\geq 0})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\rightleftarrows A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}:\triv^{\fil}_{\geq 0}.

Thus we have

\Hom_{A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}}(\oblv^{\fil}V,N)=\Hom_{A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\Vect^{\fil})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}}(V,\triv^{\fil}_{\geq 0}N).

Fix
r
as in Step 1 for
B:=\gr(A)
and
P:=\tau^{\geq m}\gr(V)
. Recalling the descending filtration on
M:=\triv^{\fil}_{\geq 0}N
constructed in Step 2, we claim that

\Hom_{A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\Vect^{\fil})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}}(V,F_{r})=0.

Since
F_{r}=\lim_{k}F_{r}/F_{r+k}
, it suffices to show that

\Hom_{A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\Vect^{\fil})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}}(V,F_{n}/F_{n+1})=0

for any
n\geq r
. By construction we have

\displaystyle\Hom_{A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\Vect^{\fil})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}}(V,F_{n}/F_{n+1})

\displaystyle=\Hom_{A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\Vect^{\fil})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}}(V,\gr F_{n})

\displaystyle=\Hom_{\gr(A)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\Vect^{\gr})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}}(\gr(V),\gr F_{n}).

Note that
M=\triv^{\fil}_{\geq 0}N
implies that
\gr F_{n}
is concentrated in cohomological degrees
\geq m
for any
n\geq 0
(this can be checked over each stratum
Z(p)
, where it becomes an obvious statement about the trivial nonnegative multifiltration). Now the claim follows from Step 1.

Step 4:
Continuing the notation of Step 3, we have

\Hom_{A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}}(\oblv^{\fil}V,N)=\Hom_{A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\Vect^{\fil})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}}(V,M/F_{r}).

Thus it suffices to show that for any
n\geq 0
,

\Hom_{A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\Vect^{\fil})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}}(V,F_{n}/F_{n+1})=\Hom_{\gr(A)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\Vect^{\gr})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}}(\gr(V),\gr F_{n})

preserves filtered colimits when viewed as a functor of
N
in
(A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{X^{I}_{\dR}})^{\geq m}
. Since
\gr F_{n}
belongs to
\gr(A)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\Vect^{\gr})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{\geq m}
by Step 3, we are done by the hypothesis on
\gr(A)
.

∎

4.9.
Finitely presented algebras

A connective commutative algebra
A
is called
0
-finitely presented
if it is isomorphic to a classical finitely generated polynomial algebra. For
n\geq 1
, we say that
A
is
n
-finitely presented
if

A\cong B\underset{\Sym(V[n-1])}{\otimes}k

where
B
is
(n-1)
-finitely presented and
V
is a finite-dimensional classical vector space equipped with a
k
-linear map
V[n-1]\to B
. If
A
is
n
-finitely presented for some
n\geq 0
, we say that
A
is
finitely presented
.

For example, an algebra
A
is
1
-finitely presented if and only if its spectrum is a derived global complete intersection.

Lemma 4.9.1
.

The conditions of Proposition
4.5.1
hold for
A
finitely presented.

Proof.

We prove by induction on
n
that the conditions hold for any
A
of the form
B\otimes\Sym V
, where
B
is
n
-finitely presented and
V
is a connective complex of vector spaces with finite-dimensional total cohomology (for varying
n
, this statement is equivalent to the assertion of the lemma). The case
n=0
is precisely Lemma
4.7.2
. In general, we have

B\cong C\underset{\Sym(W[n-1])}{\otimes}k

where
C
is
(n-1)
-finitely presented and
W
is finite-dimensional and classical. Thus
B
admits a nonnegative filtration with associated graded algebra

\gr B\cong C\otimes\Sym(W[n]).

Consequently
A
admits a nonnegative filtration with associated graded

\gr A\cong C\otimes\Sym(W[n])\otimes\Sym V\cong C\otimes\Sym(W[n]\oplus V).

Theorem
4.6.1
holds for
\gr A
by the inductive hypothesis, whence the theorem holds for
A
by Lemma
4.8.2
.

∎

4.10.
Algebras almost of finite type

We will use the following approximation lemma to prove Theorem
4.6.1
.

Lemma 4.10.1
.

If
A
is connective and almost of finite type, there exists a directed system

B_{0}\longrightarrow B_{1}\longrightarrow B_{2}\longrightarrow\cdots

of connective commutative algebras mapping to
A
such that

(1)

B_{n}
is
n
-finitely presented for every
n\geq 0
,

(2)

H^{-i}(B_{n})\tilde{\to}H^{-i}(A)
for
i<n
,

(3)

H^{-n}(B_{n})\to H^{-n}(A)
is surjective.

In particular
\colim_{n}B_{n}\tilde{\to}A
.

Finally, we can prove the theorem.

Proof of Theorem
4.6.1
.

Choose a filtration
\colim_{n}B_{n}\tilde{\to}A
as in Lemma
4.10.1
. Fix
m\geq 0
. Lemma
4.4.3
implies that for
n>m
, the restriction of scalars induces an equivalence

(A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{X^{I}_{\dR}})^{\leq 0,\geq-m}\tilde{\longrightarrow}(B_{n}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{X^{I}_{\dR}})^{\leq 0,\geq-m}.

Moreover, by Lemmas
4.3.1
and
4.4.2
, this equivalence sends

\tau^{\geq-m}(\Vac_{A,X^{I}}[-\#I])\mapsto\tau^{\geq-m}(\Vac_{B_{n},X^{I}}[-\#I]).

The inclusion

(A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{X^{I}})^{\leq 0,\geq-m}\longrightarrow(A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{X^{I}})^{\geq-m}

preserves compact objects because the t-structure on
A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{X^{I}}
is compatible with filtered colimits. Since
\Vac_{B_{n},X^{I}}
is almost ULA by Lemma
4.9.1
, it follows that

\tau^{\geq-m}(\oblv_{X^{I}}\Vac_{A,X^{I}}[-\#I])

is compact in
(A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{X^{I}})^{\geq-m}
. Thus
\Vac_{A,X^{I}}
is almost ULA as desired.

∎

5.
Spectral Hecke categories

In this section, we construct the factorization categories which appear on the spectral (a.k.a. Langlands dual) side. These arise as renormalizations of certain categories of factorization modules.

5.1.
Chiral induction

Recall that the Lie bracket on
\mathfrak{n}_{P}
induces the structure of Lie-
*
algebra on the constant sheaf
\mathfrak{n}_{P}\otimes k_{X}
. The category of Lie-
*
modules

(\mathfrak{n}_{P}\otimes k_{X})\text{-mod}^{*}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))

forms an object in
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}
. Lemma 7.7.1 in
[
Ras21a
]
says that
(\mathfrak{n}_{P}\otimes k_{X})\text{-mod}^{*}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))
is equivalent to the factorization category attached to the symmetric monoidal category
\mathfrak{n}_{P}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))
, and in particular belongs to
\mathsf{FactCat}
. In what follows we will pass freely between the two.

Following
[
Ras21a
]
, we write

\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P},-):\mathfrak{n}_{P}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(M)

for the morphism in
\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl}
defined as the composition

\mathfrak{n}_{P}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))\xrightarrow{\ind^{*\to\ch}}\UU^{*\to\ch}(\mathfrak{n}_{P}\otimes k_{X})\text{-mod}^{\ch}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))\xrightarrow{\oblv}\mathsf{Rep}(M)

(this is the chiral analogue of the
\mathbb{E}_{2}
-monoidal functor denoted by
\Chev_{\Upsilon}
in the introduction).

Denote by
\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P})
the factorization algebra in
\mathsf{Rep}(M)
obtained by applying
\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P},-)
to the unit object in
\mathfrak{n}_{P}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))
. In particular, applying (
3.5.1
) to
\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P},-)
yields a morphism

(5.1.1)

\mathfrak{n}_{P}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))\longrightarrow\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))

in
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}
.

Proposition 5.1.1
.

We have a canonical identification

\CC^{\ch}(\UU^{*\to\ch}(\mathfrak{n}_{P}\otimes k_{X}))\tilde{\longrightarrow}\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P})

of factorization algebras in
\mathsf{Rep}(M)
, i.e.
\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P})
is the factorization algebra corresponding to the chiral enveloping algebra of the Lie-
*
algebra
\mathfrak{n}_{P}\otimes k_{X}
. Moreover, we have a commutative triangle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{n}_{P}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\UU^{*\to\ch}(\mathfrak{n}_{P}\otimes k_{X})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\ch}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\ref{indups})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ind^{*\to\ch}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

in
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}
.

Proof.

Since the functor
\ind^{*\to\ch}
is unital, the factorization algebra
\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P})
is by definition the object of
\mathsf{Rep}(M)
underlying the unit in

\UU^{*\to\ch}(\mathfrak{n}_{P}\otimes k_{X})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\ch}(\mathsf{Rep}(M)).

As explained in §
4.7
, the latter is
\CC^{\ch}(\UU^{*\to\ch}(\mathfrak{n}_{P}\otimes k_{X}))
, whence the first assertion of the proposition.

As for the commutative triangle, we construct the horizontal equivalence as the composition

\displaystyle\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))

\displaystyle\tilde{\longrightarrow}\CC^{\ch}_{+}(\UU^{*\to\ch}(\mathfrak{n}_{P}\otimes k_{X}))\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fact,\nonuntl}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))

\displaystyle\tilde{\longrightarrow}\UU^{*\to\ch}(\mathfrak{n}_{P}\otimes k_{X})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\ch}(\mathsf{Rep}(M)).

The commutativity of the triangle is then immediate from the construction of (
5.1.1
.

∎

Invoking the identifications from Proposition
5.1.1
, we have an adjunction

(5.1.2)

\ind^{*\to\ch}:\mathfrak{n}_{P}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))\rightleftarrows\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(M)):\oblv^{\ch\to*}

in
\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl}^{\laxfact}
. Note that the right adjoint is compatible with the forgetful functors to
\mathsf{Rep}(M)
, and in particular is conservative. As in
[
Ras21a
]
Corollary 7.8.1, this implies that
\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))
belongs to
\mathsf{FactCat}
, i.e. factorizes strictly.

5.2.

Note that the symmetric monoidal functor

\res^{N_{P}}_{\mathfrak{n}_{P}}:\mathsf{Rep}(P)\longrightarrow\mathfrak{n}_{P}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))

is fully faithful because
\mathfrak{n}_{P}
is nilpotent.

Lemma 5.2.1
.

For any finite set
I
, the functor

\res^{N_{P}}_{\mathfrak{n}_{P}}:\mathsf{Rep}(P)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{n}_{P}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

is fully faithful.

Proof.

Since the symmetric monoidal category
\mathsf{Rep}(P)
is rigid and the unit object in
\mathfrak{n}_{P}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))
is compact, Lemma
3.4.2
says that corresponding morphism in
\mathsf{FactCat}
admits a right adjoint in
\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl}
. The unit of this adjunction is an isomorphism because it is compatible with factorization and is an isomorphism over
X
.

∎

In what follows, we will freely identify
\mathsf{Rep}(P)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
with its essential image in
\mathfrak{n}_{P}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
.

Proposition 5.2.2
.

The monad on
\mathfrak{n}_{P}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
arising from the adjunction (
5.1.2
) preserves the full subcategory
\mathsf{Rep}(P)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
for any finite set
I
.

We will apply a general lemma, which we now prepare to state. Let
\mathcal{C}
be a commutative lax factorization category, i.e. an object of
\mathsf{ComAlg}(\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact})
, and
L
a Lie-
*
algebra in
\mathcal{C}
. Then we have adjoint functors

\ind^{*\to\ch}:L\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{*}(\mathcal{C})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\rightleftarrows\UU^{*\to\ch}(L)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\ch}(\mathcal{C})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}:\oblv^{\ch\to*}

for any finite set
I
.

Recall that
L\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{*}(\mathcal{C})
has a canonical structure of commutative lax factorization category compatible with the forgetful functor

\oblv_{L}:L\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{*}(\mathcal{C})\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}.

In particular, for any finite set
I
the category

L\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{*}(\mathcal{C})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

is equipped with a symmetric monoidal structure lifting the one on
\mathcal{C}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
, which will also be denoted by
\otimes^{!}
.

Viewing
L
with the adjoint action as an object of the symmetric monoidal category
