L\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{*}(\mathcal{C})_{X_{\dR}}
, the symmetric algebra
\Sym^{!}(L[1])
is a commutative algebra in this category and hence gives rise to a commutative factorization algebra in
L\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{*}(\mathcal{C})
.

Lemma 5.2.3
.

The monad
\oblv^{\ch\to*}\ind^{*\to\ch}
on
L\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{*}(\mathcal{C})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
admits a canonical filtration with associated graded isomorphic to

\Sym^{!}(L[1])_{X^{I}}\overset{!}{\otimes}(-).

Proof.

First, we record the apparently weaker assertion that the functor

\oblv_{\UU^{*\to\ch}(L)}\ind^{*\to\ch}:L\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{*}(\mathcal{C})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

is filtered with associated graded

\oblv_{L}(\Sym^{!}(L[1])_{X^{I}}\overset{!}{\otimes}(-)).

This is a variant of Corollary 6.5.2 in
[
FG12
]
and is proved in the same way.

Now we will deduce the lemma. Observe that the adjoint action canonically upgrades
L
to a Lie-
*
algebra in the commutative lax factorization category
L\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{*}(\mathcal{C})
. It follows immediately that
\UU^{*\to\ch}(L)
upgrades to a chiral algebra in
L\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{*}(\mathcal{C})
. Moreover, the morphism

\oblv_{L}:L\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{*}(\mathcal{C})\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}

of commutative lax factorization categories induces isomorphisms

L\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{*}(L\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{*}(\mathcal{C}))\tilde{\longrightarrow}L\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{*}(\mathcal{C})

and

\UU^{*\to\ch}(L)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\ch}(L\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{*}(\mathcal{C}))\tilde{\longrightarrow}\UU^{*\to\ch}(L)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\ch}(\mathcal{C}).

These fit into a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{*}(L\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{*}(\mathcal{C}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\UU^{*\to\ch}(L)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\ch}(L\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{*}(\mathcal{C}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{*}(\mathcal{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\UU^{*\to\ch}(L)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\ch}(\mathcal{C})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ind^{*\to\ch}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ind^{*\to\ch}}

in
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}
, so the lemma follows from the weaker assertion above applied to
L
as a Lie-
*
algebra in
L\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{*}(\mathcal{C})
.

∎

Proof of Proposition
5.2.2
.

Since
\mathfrak{n}\otimes k_{X}
belongs to
\mathsf{Rep}(P)_{X_{\dR}}
, it follows that

\Sym^{!}(\mathfrak{n}\otimes k_{X}[1])_{X^{I}}

belongs to
\mathsf{Rep}(P)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
. Now apply Lemma
5.2.3
to deduce the proposition.

∎

5.3.

For any finite set
I
, define

\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{0}^{\fact}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\subset\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

to be the full subcategory generated by the image of
\mathsf{Rep}(P)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
under
\ind^{*\to\ch}
. In view of Proposition
5.2.2
, it follows that these assemble into an object of
\mathsf{FactCat}
, and we have a monadic adjunction

(5.3.1)

\ind^{*\to\ch}:\mathsf{Rep}(P)\rightleftarrows\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{0}^{\fact}(\mathsf{Rep}(M)):\oblv^{\ch\to*}

in
\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl}
with the left adjoint belonging to
\mathsf{FactCat}
.

We equip

\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{0}^{\fact}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

with the unique t-structure such that
\oblv^{\ch\to*}
is left t-exact. Equivalently, the connective objects are generated by the image of
\mathsf{Rep}(P)^{\leq 0}
under
\ind^{*\to\ch}
. Proposition 7.11.1(2) of
[
Ras21a
]
says that
\ind^{*\to\ch}
is actually t-exact.

Proposition 5.3.1
.

For any finite set
I
, the t-structure on

\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{0}^{\fact}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

is coherent.

We will apply the following general lemma.

Lemma 5.3.2
.

Let
F:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
be a t-exact functor whose restriction

F:\mathcal{C}^{\heartsuit}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\heartsuit}

is fully faithful. Assume also that the right adjoint
F^{\RR}
is continuous and conservative. If the t-structure on
\mathcal{C}
is coherent, then so is the t-structure on
\mathcal{D}
.

Proof.

Standard arguments show that any compact object in
\mathcal{D}^{\heartsuit}
admits a finite filtration with subquotients of the form
F(c)
, where
c
is compact in
\mathcal{C}^{\heartsuit}
. If the t-structure on
\mathcal{C}
is coherent, then any compact object in
\mathcal{C}^{\heartsuit}
is almost compact in
\mathcal{C}
. Since
F
preserves almost compact objects and any extension of almost compact objects is almost compact, the lemma follows.

∎

Proof of Proposition
5.3.1
.

Lemma 7.16.1 of
[
Ras21a
]
says that

\ind^{*\to\ch}:\mathsf{Rep}(P)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{0}^{\fact}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

is fully faithful on the heart, and the t-structure
\mathsf{Rep}(P)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
is coherent by Proposition
4.1.2
. Thus the hypotheses of Lemma
5.3.2
apply to the adjunction (
5.3.1
).

∎

5.4.
The spectral Chevalley functor as a factorization algebra

We will abuse notation slightly by also writing
\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P},-)
for the composition

\mathsf{Rep}(G)\xrightarrow{\res^{G}_{P}}\mathsf{Rep}(P)\xrightarrow{\res^{N_{P}}_{\mathfrak{n}_{P}}}\mathfrak{n}_{P}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))\xrightarrow{\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P},-)}\mathsf{Rep}(M).

This is a morphism in
\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl}
, and hence can be viewed as a factorization algebra in
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl}}(\mathsf{Rep}(G),\mathsf{Rep}(M))
. But
\mathsf{Rep}(G)
is canonically self-dual in
\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl}
by Proposition
3.4.1
, so we can identify

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl}}(\mathsf{Rep}(G),\mathsf{Rep}(M))\cong\mathsf{Rep}(G)\otimes\mathsf{Rep}(M)\cong\mathsf{Rep}(G\times M).

We will write
\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P},\mathcal{O}_{G})
for the factorization algebra in
\mathsf{Rep}(G\times M)
corresponding to
\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P},-)
. As the notation suggests, this factorization algebra can also be obtained by applying the morphism

\id_{\mathsf{Rep}(G)}\otimes\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P},-):\mathsf{Rep}(G)\otimes\mathsf{Rep}(G)\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(G)\otimes\mathsf{Rep}(M)\cong\mathsf{Rep}(G\times M)

in
\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl}
to
\mathcal{O}_{G}
, viewed as a commutative factorization algebra in
\mathsf{Rep}(G)\otimes\mathsf{Rep}(G)
.

By construction, there is a canonical morphism of factorization algebras

\unit_{\mathsf{Rep}(G)}\boxtimes\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P})\longrightarrow\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P},\mathcal{O}_{G})

in
\mathsf{Rep}(G\times M)
. By Lemma
3.6.1
, this induces an equivalence

(5.4.1)

\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P},\mathcal{O}_{G})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times M))\tilde{\longrightarrow}\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P},\mathcal{O}_{G})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(G)\otimes\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))).

By definition, the factorization algebra
\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P},\mathcal{O}_{G})
is the image of
\res^{G\times G}_{G\times P}
under the functor

\id_{\mathsf{Rep}(G)}\otimes(\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P},-)\circ\res^{N_{P}}_{\mathfrak{n}_{P}}):\mathsf{Rep}(G\times P)\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(G)\otimes\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))),

and in particular belongs to the full subcategory

\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P},\mathcal{O}_{G})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(G)\otimes\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{0}^{\fact}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))).

We define

\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P},\mathcal{O}_{G})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{0}^{\fact}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times M))\subset\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P},\mathcal{O}_{G})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times M))

to be the full subcategory corresponding to

\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P},\mathcal{O}_{G})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(G)\otimes\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{0}^{\fact}(\mathsf{Rep}(M)))

under the equivalence (
5.4.1
).

The adjunction (
5.3.1
) induces an adjunction

\mathsf{Rep}(G\times P)\rightleftarrows\mathsf{Rep}(G)\otimes\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{0}^{\fact}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))

in
\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl}
after tensoring with
\id_{\mathsf{Rep}(G)}
. Applying the construction (
3.5.1
) yields an adjunction

(5.4.2)

\mathcal{O}_{G}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times P))\rightleftarrows\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P},\mathcal{O}_{G})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{0}^{\fact}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times M))

in
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}_{\laxuntl}
, with the left adjoint belonging to
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}
. Note that by construction, the right adjoint makes the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P},\mathcal{O}_{G})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{0}^{\fact}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times M))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{G}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times P))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Rep}(G\times M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Rep}(G\times P)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oblv_{\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P},\mathcal{O}_{G})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oblv_{\mathcal{O}_{G}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id\otimes\res^{P}_{M}}

commute, and in particular is conservative over
X^{I}_{\dR}
for any finite set
I
.

5.5.

We now define a t-structure on the category

\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P},\mathcal{O}_{G})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{0}^{\fact}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times M))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

for any finite set
I
. Recall that by Proposition
4.2.1
, the category

\mathcal{O}_{G}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times P))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

admits a unique t-structure which makes the forgetful functor to
\mathsf{Rep}(G\times P)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
t-exact.

Lemma 5.5.1
.

For any finite set
I
, there is a unique t-structure on the category

\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P},\mathcal{O}_{G})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{0}^{\fact}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times M))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

which makes the left adjoint in (
5.4.2
) t-exact over
X^{I}_{\dR}
, and this functor moreover restricts to an equivalence on the hearts of the t-structures.

Proof.

Since the right adjoint in (
5.4.2
) is conservative, there is a unique t-structure which makes the left adjoint right t-exact. To see that it is left t-exact, it suffices to check that the corresponding monad on

\mathcal{O}_{G}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times P))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

is left t-exact. The forgetful functor

\mathcal{O}_{G}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times P))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(G\times P)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

is t-exact and intertwines the monad in question with the monad

\id_{\mathsf{Rep}(G)}\otimes(\oblv^{\ch\to*}\circ\ind^{*\to\ch}\circ\res^{N_{P}}_{\mathfrak{n}_{P}})

on the target. Thus it suffices to show that the latter monad is left t-exact, which follows from the PBW theorem for factorization modules as in the proof of Proposition 7.11.1 in
[
Ras16
]
.

The left adjoint in (
5.4.2
) is an equivalence on hearts by a similar calculation using the PBW theorem (see the proof of Lemma 7.16.1 in
loc. cit.
; cf. also Proposition
4.4.4
above).

∎

By Proposition
4.4.4
, the functor

\mathsf{Rep}(P)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\cong\mathcal{O}_{G}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times P))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\xrightarrow{\oblv^{\com\to\fact}}\mathcal{O}_{G}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times P))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

restricts to an equivalence on the hearts of the t-structures. Combining this with Lemma
5.5.1
, we obtain an equivalence

(\mathsf{Rep}(P)_{X^{I}_{\dR}})^{\heartsuit}\tilde{\longrightarrow}(\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P},\mathcal{O}_{G})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{0}^{\fact}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times M))_{X^{I}_{\dR}})^{\heartsuit}.

Proposition 5.5.2
.

For any finite set
I
, the t-structure on

\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P},\mathcal{O}_{G})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{0}^{\fact}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times M))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

satisfies the hypotheses of Proposition
3.9.2
. In particular, this category is almost ULA generated over
X^{I}
.

Proof.

First, we show that the t-structure is almost ULA generated. Coherence follows from Lemma
5.5.1
using the criterion of Lemma
2.4.7
, since the left adjoint in (
5.4.2
) preserves almost compact objects, the category

\mathsf{Rep}(P)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{\heartsuit}

is compactly generated, and

\mathcal{O}_{G}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times P))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

is almost compactly generated by Corollary
4.6.1.1
. Recall that

\mathcal{O}_{G}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times P))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

admits a set of almost ULA generators by Corollary
4.6.1.1
, and since the right adjoint in (
5.4.2
) is left t-exact, conservative, and
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
-linear, the claim follows.

Hypothesis (ii) of Proposition
3.9.1
follows from the corresponding property of

\mathcal{O}_{G}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times P))

via the right adjoint in (
5.4.2
), which is left t-exact and conservative over
X^{I}_{\dR}
for all
I
. As for hypothesis (iii), by right completeness it suffices to check that the structure functors in question are left t-exact on the heart of the t-structure. Since

\mathcal{O}_{G}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times P))

has this property by Corollary
4.6.1.1
, the claim follows from Lemma
5.5.1
.

Similarly, the hypothesis in Proposition
3.9.2
is satisfied because of Lemma
5.5.1
and the corresponding property of

\mathcal{O}_{G}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times P)).

∎

5.6.
Definition of the spectral Hecke categories

Applying Propositions
3.9.2
and
5.5.2
, we obtain an object

\mathsf{Sph}^{\spec}_{G,P}:=\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P},\mathcal{O}_{G})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{0}^{\fact}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times M))^{\ren}

in
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}
. Composing the morphism

\mathsf{Rep}(P)\cong\mathcal{O}_{G}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times P))\xrightarrow{\oblv^{\operatorname{com}\to\operatorname{fact}}}\mathcal{O}_{G}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times P))

in
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}
with the left adjoint in (
5.4.2
), we obtain a morphism

(5.6.1)

\mathsf{Rep}(P)\longrightarrow\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P},\mathcal{O}_{G})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{0}^{\fact}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times M)).

This functor is t-exact over
X^{I}_{\dR}
for every finite set
I
, and hence renormalizes to a morphism

(5.6.2)

\mathsf{Rep}(P)\longrightarrow\mathsf{Sph}^{\spec}_{G,P}

in
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}
, where we used Proposition
4.1.2
to identify
\mathsf{Rep}(P)^{\ren}\cong\mathsf{Rep}(P)
. By Lemma
5.5.1
, this induces an equivalence

\mathsf{Rep}(P)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{\heartsuit}\tilde{\longrightarrow}(\mathsf{Sph}^{\spec}_{G,P})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{\heartsuit}

for any finite set
I
.

Proposition 5.6.1
.

The morphism (
5.6.2
) admits a right adjoint in
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}_{\laxuntl}
, which is conservative over
X^{I}_{\dR}
for any finite set
I
. In particular, the object
\mathsf{Sph}^{\spec}_{G,P}
belongs to
\mathsf{FactCat}
.

Proof.

Theorem
4.6.1
, together with the existence of the adjunction (
5.4.2
), implies that

\mathsf{Rep}(P)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow(\mathsf{Sph}^{\spec}_{G,P})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

preserves ULA objects for any finite set
I
. Moreover, the essential image of this functor generates its target, since it induces an equivalence on the hearts of the t-structures. Since
\mathsf{Rep}(P)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
is ULA generated by Proposition
4.1.2
, we obtain the desired right adjoint. It follows that
\mathsf{Sph}^{\spec}_{G,P}
factorizes strictly, since
\mathsf{Rep}(P)
does and we have just shown that (
5.6.2
) has a monadic right adjoint.

∎

5.7.
Associative algebra and bimodule structures

In the case
G=P
, we will write

\mathsf{Sph}^{\spec}_{G}:=\mathsf{Sph}^{\spec}_{G,G}

to simplify the notation. Here the factorization algebra
\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P},\mathcal{O}_{G})=\mathcal{O}_{G}
is the monoidal unit in

\mathsf{Rep}(G\times G)\cong\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl}}(\mathsf{Rep}(G)).

The object

\mathcal{O}_{G}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times G))

is obtained by applying the construction (
3.7.2
) to
\mathsf{Rep}(G\times G)
, hence inherits an associative algebra structure in
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}
. Similarly, the object

\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P},\mathcal{O}_{G})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times M))

is naturally a bimodule for the pair

(\mathcal{O}_{G}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times G)),\mathcal{O}_{M}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(M\times M))).

Proposition 5.7.1
.

The object
\mathsf{Sph}^{\spec}_{G}
admits a unique structure of associative algebra in
\mathsf{FactCat}
compatible with the morphism

\mathsf{Sph}^{\spec}_{G}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{G}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times G)).

Similarly, the object
\mathsf{Sph}^{\spec}_{G,P}
admits a unique structure of
(\mathsf{Sph}^{\spec}_{G},\mathsf{Sph}^{\spec}_{M})
-bimodule in
\mathsf{FactCat}
compatible with the morphism

\mathsf{Sph}^{\spec}_{G,P}\longrightarrow\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P},\mathcal{O}_{G})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times M))

in
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}
.

Proof.

For the first claim, by Proposition
3.9.3
it suffices to show that the unit and multiplication morphisms for the associative algebra

\mathcal{O}_{G}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times G))

are unary and binary morphisms, respectively, in the pseudo-tensor category
(\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact})^{\otimes}_{\aULA}
. This means that for any finite set
I
, the monoidal unit object in

\mathcal{O}_{G}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times G))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

is eventually coconnective, and that eventually coconnective objects are stable under the monoidal operation. By Lemmas
4.3.1
and
4.4.2
, the unit object
\mathcal{O}_{G,X^{I}}
is concentrated in cohomological degree
-\#I
, and in particular is eventually coconnective. As for the multiplication, the t-exact monoidal functor

\oblv^{\com\to\fact}:\mathsf{Rep}(G)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{G}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times G))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

induces an equivalence on the hearts by Proposition
4.4.4
, and monoidal structure on
\mathsf{Rep}(G)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
is left t-exact in each variable separately. The claim now follows by right completeness of the t-structure on

\mathcal{O}_{G}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times G))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}.

Recall that the morphism (
5.6.1
) in
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}
is t-exact and induces an equivalence on the hearts over each
X^{I}_{\dR}
. By right completeness of the t-structures, it follows that the action of the pair

(\mathcal{O}_{G}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times G)),\mathcal{O}_{M}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(M\times M)))

preserves the full subcategory

\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P},\mathcal{O}_{G})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{0}^{\fact}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times M))\subset\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P},\mathcal{O}_{G})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times M)),

and that the action of the pair on this subcategory takes place in
(\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact})^{\otimes}_{\aULA}
. Then we are done by Proposition
3.9.3
.

∎

6.
Construction of the derived Satake transform

In this section, we construct the factorization categories that appear on the geometric (a.k.a. automorphic) side. We then construct functors to the spectral side, and formulate our main results as Theorems
6.6.1
and
6.12.3
.

6.1.
Canonical twists

Let
\rho
denote the half-sum of the simple coroots of
G
. Choose a square root
(\Omega^{1}_{X})^{\otimes\frac{1}{2}}
of the canonical line bundle
\Omega^{1}_{X}
on
X
, and put

\rho(\Omega^{1}_{X}):=2\rho((\Omega^{1}_{X})^{\otimes\frac{1}{2}}),

which is a well-defined
T
-bundle on
X
because
2\rho\in\Lambda
is an integral coweight.

In what follows, we replace
G
by its
canonical twist
by
\rho(\Omega^{1}_{X})
. By definition, this is the group scheme of automorphisms of the
G
-bundle on
X
defined by

G\overset{T}{\times}\rho(\Omega^{1}_{X}).

Similarly, we replace
P
and
P^{-}
by their canonical twists, defined in the same way (the corresponding twist of
T
itself is trivial because
T
is commutative). These are subgroup schemes of the canonical twist of
G
. We remark that by construction, all of these twists are pure inner forms (cf. §2.15 of
[
Ras16
]
of the corresponding constant group schemes
G
,
P
, and
P^{-}
. In particular, replacing each of these groups with its canonical twist does not affect the corresponding space of principal bundles.

The canonical twist of
N
is defined as

N\overset{T}{\times}\rho(\Omega^{1}_{X})

using the adjoint action of
T
on
N
, and similarly for
N^{-}
. These are normal subgroup schemes of the canonical twists of
B
and
B^{-}
respectively, with the quotient in either case being the constant group scheme
T
. The canonical twists of
N
and
N^{-}
are
not
pure inner forms of the constant group schemes, and their spaces of principal bundles generally differ. One can similarly define the canonical twist of any subgroup of
N
stable under the adjoint action of
T
, such as
N_{P}
or
N_{M}:=N\cap M
.

These twists will be suppressed below in order to simplify the notation. Their presence can be safely ignored in most situations, with the notable exception of defining the Whittaker condition.

6.2.
The spherical Hecke stack

Write
\mathcal{H}_{G}:=\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G/\mathfrak{L}^{+}G
for the spherical Hecke stack, a groupoid in corr-unital factorization spaces (i.e. factorization spaces with a unit correspondence rather than a single morphism, cf.
[
CF21b
]
§10). The groupoid structure on
\mathcal{H}_{G}
induces a monoidal structure on

\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G,X^{I}}):=\mathsf{D}((\mathfrak{L}G)_{X^{I}})^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\times\mathfrak{L}^{+}G}

for each finite set
I
, and moreover these assemble into an object
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})
of
\mathsf{AssocAlg}(\mathsf{FactCat})
satisfying

\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}=\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G,X^{I}}).

For any finite set
I
, the Beilinson-Drinfeld affine Grassmannian

\Gr_{G,X^{I}}=(\mathfrak{L}G/\mathfrak{L}^{+}G)_{X^{I}}

is an ind-scheme locally of finite type, and in particular
\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G,X^{I}})
is equipped with a natural t-structure. We give
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G,X^{I}})
the unique t-structure such that the forgetful functor

\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G,X^{I}})=\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G,X^{I}})^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G}\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G,X^{I}})

is t-exact.

6.3.
Construction of the spherical Hecke category

The naïve Satake transform, as constructed in §6 of
[
Ras21a
]
, is a morphism

\Sat^{\naive}_{G}:\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})

in
\mathsf{AssocAlg}(\mathsf{FactCat})
. We recall from
loc. cit.
that for any finite set
I
, the corresponding functor

\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G,X^{I}})

is t-exact and induces an equivalence

\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{\heartsuit}\tilde{\longrightarrow}\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G,X^{I}})^{\heartsuit}.

Lemma 6.3.1
.

For any finite set
I
, the functor

\Sat^{\naive}_{G}:\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G,X^{I}})

preserves almost ULA objects.

Proof.

It suffices to show that the
\mathsf{IndCoh}(X^{I})
-linear functor

\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})_{X^{I}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})_{X^{I}}

preserves almost compact objects. By Proposition
4.1.2
, almost compact objects in
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})_{X^{I}}
are compact. Moreover, the symmetric monoidal structure is rigid, which means that compactness in
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})_{X^{I}}
is equivalent to dualizability. Since
\Sat^{\naive}_{G}
is monoidal, it preserves dualizable objects, so it remains only to show that the unit object
\delta_{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G,X^{I}}
is almost compact in
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})_{X^{I}}
. For this, recall that the forgetful functor

\oblv_{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G}:\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})_{X^{I}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})_{X^{I}}

is t-exact, conservative, and admits an
\mathsf{IndCoh}(X^{I})
-linear right adjoint, namely
*
-averaging with respect to
\mathfrak{L}^{+}G
. The image of
\delta_{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G,X^{I}}
under this functor is the direct image of
\omega_{X^{I}}
along the unit section

X^{I}\longrightarrow X^{I}\times_{X^{I}_{\dR}}(\Gr_{G,X^{I}})_{\dR},

which is ind-proper. It follows that
\oblv_{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G}(\delta_{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G,X^{I}})
is compact in
\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})_{X^{I}}
, and hence
\delta_{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G,X^{I}}
is almost compact in
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})_{X^{I}}
by Lemma
2.4.1
.

∎

Proposition 6.3.2
.

The t-structure on
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G,X^{I}})
satisfies the hypotheses of Proposition
3.9.2
. Moreover, the object

\mathsf{Sph}_{G}:=\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})^{\ren}

belongs to
\mathsf{FactCat}
and admits a unique associative algebra structure compatible with

\mathsf{Sph}_{G}\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G}).

Proof.

We first show that that the t-structure on
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G,X^{I}})
is coherent, or equivalently that any object compact in
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G,X^{I}})^{\geq 0}
is almost compact in
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G,X^{I}})
. The t-structure on
\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G,X^{I}})
is coherent because
\Gr_{G,X^{I}}
is an ind-scheme locally of finite type. Thus the claim follows from Lemma
2.4.1
applied to the functor

\oblv_{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G}:\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G,X^{I}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G,X^{I}}),

which is t-exact, conservative, and admits a continuous right adjoint.

The functor

\Sat^{\naive}_{G}:\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G,X^{I}})

induces an equivalence on the hearts and preserves almost ULA objects by Lemma
6.3.1
, and
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
is ULA generated by Proposition
4.1.2
. It follows readily that
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G,X^{I}})
is almost ULA generated. Conditions (ii-iii) of Proposition
3.9.1
, as well as the hypothesis of Proposition
3.9.2
, can be deduced from the corresponding properties of
\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G,X^{I}})
.

By Proposition
4.1.2
, renormalizing
\Sat^{\naive}_{G}
yields a morphism

\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Sph}_{G}

in
\mathsf{AssocAlg}(\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact})
. Moreover, by Lemma
6.3.1
, this morphism admits a right adjoint in
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}_{\laxuntl}
. Since
\Sat^{\naive}_{G}
is an equivalence on the hearts, this right adjoint is conservative and hence monadic over each
X^{I}_{\dR}
. It follows that
\mathsf{Sph}_{G}
belongs to
\mathsf{FactCat}
, i.e. factorizes strictly, since
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})
has this property.

For the last claim, by Proposition
3.9.3
it suffices to show that
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})
is an associative algebra in the pseudo-tensor category
(\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact})^{\otimes}_{\aULA}
. This follows from right completeness and the corresponding property of
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})
, once more using the fact that the monoidal functor

\Sat^{\naive}_{G}:\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Sph}_{G,X^{I}_{\dR}}

is t-exact and induces an equivalence on the hearts for any finite set
I
.

∎

Remark 6.3.3
.

The above result may be compared with
[
Noc23
]
Corollary 1.2
in the Betti setting.

6.4.
Whittaker (co)invariants

We have an isomorphism

N^{-}/[N^{-},N^{-}]\cong\prod_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{G}}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\Omega^{1}_{X}}

of group schemes over
X
, where

\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\Omega^{1}_{X}}:=\mathbb{G}_{a}\overset{\mathbb{G}_{m}}{\times}\Omega^{1}_{X}

is the canonical twist of
\mathbb{G}_{a}
. Summing over
\mathcal{I}_{G}
, we obtain a homomorphism
N^{-}\to\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\Omega^{1}_{X}}
and hence a homomorphism of factorizable group ind-schemes

\mathfrak{L}N^{-}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{L}(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\Omega^{1}_{X}}).

Finally, we compose with the canonical residue homomorphism

\mathfrak{L}(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\Omega^{1}_{X}})\longrightarrow\mathbb{G}_{a}

to obtain

\psi:\mathfrak{L}N^{-}\longrightarrow\mathbb{G}_{a}.

In particular, we obtain a multiplicative D-module (a.k.a. character D-module of rank 1)
\psi^{!}\exp[1]
on
\mathfrak{L}N^{-}
. For any category
\mathcal{C}
acted on by
\mathsf{D}((\mathfrak{L}G)_{X^{I}})
, we can consider the associated Whittaker invariants

\mathcal{C}^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}:=\mathcal{C}^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi^{!}\exp[1]},

and similarly for coinvariants (we suppress
X^{I}
from the notation here for simplicity). Theorem 2.2.1 of
[
Ras21b
]
asserts that there is a canonical equivalence

\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}\tilde{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{C}^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}.

6.5.
The spherical Whittaker category

As shown in
[
Ras16
]
Corollary 2.31.2, the
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
-module categories

\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G,X^{I}})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}

assemble into an object

\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}

of
\mathsf{FactCat}
, with unit given by the so-called Whittaker vacuum object. Convolution on the right defines an action of
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})
on this category, and in particular, we obtain a morphism

\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}

in
\mathsf{FactCat}
by acting on the unit.

Theorem 6.5.1
.

The composite functor

\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\xrightarrow{\Sat^{\naive}_{G}}\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}

is an isomorphism in
\mathsf{FactCat}
.

Proof.

This is Theorem 6.36.1 of
[
Ras21a
]
.

∎

In particular, the equivalence in the theorem induces an action of
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})
on

\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\cong\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}

in
\mathsf{FactCat}
. By Proposition
3.4.1
, this action corresponds to a morphism

(6.5.1)

\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})\longrightarrow\underline{\End}_{\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl}}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G}))\cong\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G}\times\check{G})

in
\mathsf{AssocAlg}(\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl})
.

We will need the following observation.

Lemma 6.5.2
.

For any finite set
I
, the functor

\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\xrightarrow{(\ref{sphtobimod})}\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G}\times\check{G})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

is t-exact.

Proof.

First, we claim that the composite functor

\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\xrightarrow{\Sat^{\naive}_{G}}\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G}\times\check{G})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

is t-exact. By the construction of (
6.5.1
), this composite agrees with the induction functor
\coind_{\check{G}}^{\check{G}\times\check{G}}
, i.e. the right adjoint to restriction along the diagonal homomorphism
\check{G}\to\check{G}\times\check{G}
. The t-exactness of
\coind_{\check{G}}^{\check{G}\times\check{G}}
be proved in the same way as (or deduced from) that of
\coind_{1}^{\check{G}}
, which is Proposition 6.24.2 of
[
Ras21a
]
.

This immediately implies that the functor in the proposition is right t-exact, since
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{\leq 0}
is generated under colimits by

\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{\heartsuit}\cong\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{\heartsuit}.

Right completeness of the t-structure implies that
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{\geq 0}
is generated by
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{\heartsuit}
under filtered colimits, shifts, and extensions, whence the functor in question is left t-exact.

∎

6.6.
Derived Satake transform

Note that the functor (
6.5.1
) is not strictly unital with respect to the factorization structure. Namely, it preserves monoidal units, but the monoidal and factorization units in
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G}\times\check{G})
do not coincide.

Applying the functor (
3.7.2
), we obtain a morphism

(6.6.1)

\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G}\times\check{G}))

in
\mathsf{AssocAlg}(\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact})

Observe that the monoidal functor

\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\xrightarrow{(\ref{presatake})}\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G}\times\check{G}))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

is t-exact for any finite set
I
, since the forgetful functor

\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G}\times\check{G}))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G}\times\check{G})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

is t-exact and conservative, and its composition with (
6.6.1
) is t-exact by Proposition
6.5.2
.

By Propositions
3.9.3
and
6.3.2
, together with Corollary
4.6.1.1
, we can renormalize (
6.6.1
) to obtain a morphism

\Sat_{G}:\mathsf{Sph}_{G}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Sph}_{\check{G}}^{\spec}

in
\mathsf{AssocAlg}(\mathsf{FactCat})
. Now we can state our first main theorem precisely.

Theorem 6.6.1
.

The functor
\Sat_{G}
is an equivalence.

This theorem will be proved in §
9
.

6.7.
The semi-infinite Whittaker category

Recall from
[
Ras16
]
§2.34 that the categories

((\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

assemble into an object of
\mathsf{FactCat}
, denoted there by
\mathsf{Whit}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}
. Following
loc. cit.
in the case
P=B
, we will now construct a morphism

(6.7.1)

(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M})

in
\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl}
.

First, we restrict the Whittaker condition to the subgroup
N^{-}_{M}:=N^{-}\cap M
, i.e. apply the forgetful functor

(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}\longrightarrow(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})^{\mathfrak{L}N_{M}^{-},\psi}.

At the next stage, restrict along the inclusion
\mathfrak{L}P\to\mathfrak{L}G
to obtain

(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})^{\mathfrak{L}N_{M}^{-},\psi}\longrightarrow(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}P)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})^{\mathfrak{L}N_{M}^{-},\psi}\cong\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{M})^{\mathfrak{L}N_{M}^{-},\psi}.

Recall that for any finite set
I
there is a degree map

\deg_{M}:\Gr_{M,X^{I}}\longrightarrow\pi_{1}(M),

compatible with factorization, where
\pi_{1}(M)
is viewed as discrete abelian group. We can identify

\pi_{1}(M)=\Lambda/(\mathbb{Z}\cdot R_{M}),

the lattice of coweights modulo the coroot lattice of
M
. Note that the homomorphism

\displaystyle\ell_{P}:\pi_{1}(M)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}

\displaystyle\lambda

\displaystyle\mapsto\langle 2\check{\rho}_{G}-2\check{\rho}_{M},\lambda\rangle

is well-defined, and hence we obtain an automorphism

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{M})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{M})

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathcal{M}[-\ell_{P}(\deg_{M})]

in
\mathsf{FactCat}
given by a cohomological shift which depends on the connected component.

Since
\mathfrak{L}N^{-}_{M}
is connected, this automorpism of
\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{M})
preserves the Whittaker condition. Compose this automorphism with the previously constructed morphism

(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{M})^{\mathfrak{L}N_{M}^{-},\psi}

to obtain another such morphism. Finally, applying Theorem
6.5.1
to
M
, we have

\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{M})^{\mathfrak{L}N_{M}^{-},\psi}\tilde{\longrightarrow}\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M}).

This completes the construction of the morphism (
6.7.1
).

Theorem 6.7.1
.

The morphism (
6.7.1
) sends the unit object to the factorization algebra
\Upsilon(\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{\check{P}})
in
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M})
.

Proof.

In the case
P=G
, the morphism (
6.7.1
) is inverse to the equivalence of Theorem
6.5.1
, and
\Upsilon(\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{\check{P}})
is the unit object in
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M})=\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})
, so there is nothing to show.

The case
P=B
is Theorem 4.4.1 of
[
Ras16
]
.

The general case will be proved in forthcoming work by Faergeman and Hayash.

∎

In particular, applying the construction (
3.5.1
) to (
6.7.1
), we obtain a morphism

(6.7.2)

(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}\longrightarrow\Upsilon(\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{\check{P}})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M}))

in
\mathsf{FactCat}
.

6.8.
The accessible Whittaker category

The morphism

(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}\xrightarrow{\oblv_{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}}\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M}\xrightarrow{\Av^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G}_{*}}\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M}

in
\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl}
admits a left adjoint (see
[
Ras16
]
Proposition 5.4.1), which we denote by
\Av^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}_{!}
. Moreover, by the construction of unital structures in
loc. cit.
, the morphism
\Av^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}_{!}
is strictly unital.

Define the factorization subcategory

(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi,\acc}\subset(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}

to be generated under colimits by the essential image of

\Av^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}_{!}:\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M}\longrightarrow(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}.

As explained in
loc. cit.
, this subcategory admits a unique unital factorization structure which makes the inclusion a morphism in
\mathsf{FactCat}
.

Theorem 6.8.1
.

The morphism (
6.7.2
) restricts to an equivalence

(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi,\acc}\tilde{\longrightarrow}\Upsilon(\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{\check{P}})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{0}^{\fact}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M})).

Proof.

In the case
P=G
, the accessibility condition is vacuous, and this is inverse to the equivalence of Theorem
6.5.1
.

In the case
P=B
, Theorem 5.7.1 of
[
Ras16
]
says that this functor is fully faithful. To prove essential surjectivity, we claim that for arbitrary
P
, the image of the functor generates

\Upsilon(\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{\check{P}})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{0}^{\fact}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M}))

under colimits. The functor in question is
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M})
-linear, where
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M})
acts on

(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi,\acc}

via
\Sat_{M}^{\naive}
. We have a commutative triangle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi,\acc}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Upsilon(\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{\check{P}})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{0}^{\fact}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M}))}

in
\mathsf{FactCat}
, where both diagonal morphisms are given by acting on the unit for the factorization structure. Similarly, the triangle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Rep}(\check{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Upsilon(\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{\check{P}})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{0}^{\fact}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M}))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\triv_{\check{N}_{\check{P}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\ref{chiralind2})}

commutes because both circuits are
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M})
-linear morphisms in
\mathsf{FactCat}
. Since the images of
\triv_{\check{N}_{\check{P}}}
and (
5.3.1
) generate their targets under colimits, the claim follows.

For the general case, by the argument above it suffices to show fully faithfulness. This can be deduced from Theorem
6.7.1
by adapting the argument of
[
Ras16
]
to the parabolic case. One also needs a parabolic version of the Arkhipov-Bezrukavnikov equivalence
[
AB09
]
, which can be deduced from the case
P=B
, as explained in §6 of
[
BL18
]
(cf. also
[
ACR18
]
).

∎

6.9.
The t-structure on the semi-infinite spherical category

We now prepare to define the spherical Hecke category
\mathsf{Sph}_{G,P}
attached to
P
. Appealing again to
[
Ras16
]
§2, we recall that the categories

(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

assemble into an object of
\mathsf{FactCat}
. We will denote the unit object by
\Delta^{0}
.

Consider the correspondence of corr-unital factorization spaces

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{L}^{+}P\backslash\mathfrak{L}P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{L}^{+}M\backslash\mathfrak{L}M.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{q}}

The composite morphism

\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{M})\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{q}^{!}}\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}P\backslash\mathfrak{L}P/\mathfrak{L}^{+}M)\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}P\backslash\mathfrak{L}P)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M}

is an isomorphism in
\mathsf{FactCat}
. We will abuse notation and denote it simply by
\mathfrak{q}^{!}
, and its inverse by
\mathfrak{q}_{!}
.

The morphism

\mathfrak{p}^{!}:\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M}\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}P\backslash\mathfrak{L}P)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M}

admits a left adjoint
\mathfrak{p}_{!}
. Explicitly, the composite

\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{M})\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{q}^{!}}\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}P\backslash\mathfrak{L}P)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M}\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{p}_{!}}\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M}

is given by acting on the unit object.

Now consider the adjunction

(6.9.1)

\mathfrak{p}_{!}\mathfrak{q}^{!}[-\ell_{P}(\deg_{M})]:\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{M})\rightleftarrows\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M}:\mathfrak{q}_{!}\mathfrak{p}^{!}[\ell_{P}(\deg_{M})],

which takes place in
\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl}
, with the left adjoint belonging to
\mathsf{FactCat}
. We also remark that the right adjoint is conservative, which follows from the fact that
\mathfrak{p}
is an ind-locally closed stratification.

We equip
(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
with the t-structure characterized by the requirement that

\mathfrak{q}_{!}\mathfrak{p}^{!}[\ell_{P}(\deg_{M})]:(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{M})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

be left t-exact. Equivalently, the connective objects are generated by the image of
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{M,X^{I}})^{\leq 0}
under
\mathfrak{p}_{!}\mathfrak{q}^{!}[-\ell_{P}(\deg_{M})]
.

We remark that this does
not
agree with the t-structure on this category defined (in the case
P=B
) by Gaitsgory in
[
Gai22
]
. Namely, unlike the t-structure introduced in
loc. cit.
, our t-structure is local on the curve
X
(since the t-structure on
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{M,X^{I}})
has this feature). However, the two t-structures do agree when restricted to objects supported at a single point
x\in X(k)
.

Lemma 6.9.1
.

For any finite set
I
, the functor

\mathfrak{p}_{!}\mathfrak{q}^{!}[-\ell_{P}(\deg_{M})]:\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{M})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

is t-exact.

Proof.

This functor is right t-exact by definition, so it suffices to show left t-exactness. Since its right adjoint is conservative, it is enough to prove that the resulting monad on
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{M,X^{I}})
is left t-exact. This monad is
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{M,X^{I}})
-linear, so its value on an object
\mathcal{M}
in
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{M,X^{I}})
is

(\mathfrak{q}_{!}\mathfrak{p}^{!}\Delta^{0}_{X^{I}})[\ell_{P}(\deg_{M})]\star\mathcal{M}.

Using
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
-linearity, we can reduce to the case that
I
has one element. So we must show that if
\mathcal{M}
belongs to
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{M,X})^{\geq 0}
, then

(\mathfrak{q}_{!}\mathfrak{p}^{!}\Delta^{0}_{X})[\ell_{P}(\deg_{M})]\star\mathcal{M}

is coconnective. Since
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{M,X})
is right complete, we can even assume that
\mathcal{M}
belongs to

\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{M,X})^{\heartsuit}\cong\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M})_{X_{\dR}}^{\heartsuit}\cong(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M})\otimes\mathsf{D}(X))^{\heartsuit}.

Choose an étale coordinate on
X
, so that
\Delta^{0}_{X}\cong\Delta^{0}_{x}\boxtimes\omega_{X}
for a fixed point
x\in X(k)
. Thus it suffices to show that for any
V\in\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M})^{\heartsuit}
, the object

(\mathfrak{q}_{!}\mathfrak{p}^{!}\Delta^{0}_{x})[\ell_{P}(\deg_{M})]\star\Sat_{M,x}^{\naive}(V)

belongs to
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{M,x})^{\geq 0}
. Theorem 1.5.5 in
[
Gai19
]
says that

(\mathfrak{q}_{!}\mathfrak{p}^{!}\Delta^{0}_{x})[\ell_{P}(\deg_{M})]

is coconnective when
P=B
, and the general case can be proved similarly. Now we are done by t-exactness of the convolution product on
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{M,x})
.

∎

6.10.
The t-structure on the semi-infinite Whittaker category

Consider the morphism

(6.10.1)

\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M})\longrightarrow(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})_{\acc}

in
\mathsf{FactCat}
given by acting on the unit of the factorization structure via
\Sat_{M}^{\naive}
. For any finite set
I
, we equip the category

(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi,\acc}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

with the t-structure whose connective objects are generated under colimits by the image of
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M})^{\leq 0}
under (
6.10.1
).

Proposition 6.10.1
.

The equivalence of Theorem
6.8.1
is exact over
X^{I}_{\dR}
for every finite set
I
.

Proof.

By definition, the connective objects in

\Upsilon(\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{\check{P}})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{0}^{\fact}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M}))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

are generated by the image of
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{P})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{\leq 0}
under the left adjoint in (
5.3.1
). But the latter is generated by the image of
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{\leq 0}
under

\triv_{\check{N}}:\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(\check{P})_{X^{I}_{\dR}},

so it suffices to show that the triangle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})_{\acc}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Upsilon(\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{\check{P}})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{0}^{\fact}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M}))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\ref{whitgen})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ind^{*\to\ch}\circ\triv_{\check{N}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

in
\mathsf{FactCat}
commutes. This is immediate from the fact that all three functors are
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M})
-linear and unital.

∎

Proposition 6.10.2
.

The morphism (
6.10.1
) is t-exact over
X^{I}_{\dR}
for any finite set
I
and preserves ULA objects. The resulting right adjoint

(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi,\acc}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M})

in
\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl}
is conservative over
X^{I}_{\dR}
for any finite set
I
.

Proof.

These are consequences of Theorem
6.8.1
. Alternatively, one can prove the t-exactness more directly along the same lines as Lemma
6.9.1
, and the existence of a factorizable and conservative right adjoint follows from Theorem 5.10.1 in
[
Ras16
]
.

∎

6.11.
Definition of the spherical Hecke category for a parabolic

In order to renormalize the spherical semi-infinite category, we need a couple of technical lemmas.

Lemma 6.11.1
.

For any finite set
I
, the functor

(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\xrightarrow{\Av^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}_{!}}(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi,\acc}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

is t-exact. Moreover, this functor is conservative on eventually coconnective objects.

Proof.

To see that the functor is right t-exact, consider the commutative triangle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi,\acc}}

in
\mathsf{FactCat}
, where both diagonal morphisms are given by acting on the unit objects. Over
X^{I}_{\dR}
, the diagonal functors are t-exact and generate their targets under colimits, which implies that the horizontal functor is right t-exact.

For left t-exactness, the Cousin filtration and factorization immediately reduce the claim to the case where
I
has one element. After étale localization on
X
, we have a
\mathsf{D}(X)
-linear equivalence

(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})_{X_{\dR}}\cong(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})_{x}\otimes\mathsf{D}(X)

for a fixed
x\in X(k)
, and similarly for

(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi,\acc}_{X_{\dR}}.

Thus, by Lemma
2.2.1
we can even reduce to proving the claim at a single point
x
. Since the t-structure on
(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})_{x}
is right complete, it is enough to prove that the functor is left t-exact when restricted to the heart. By (parabolic variants of) Proposition 1.5.7 and Theorem 4.2.3 in
[
Gai19
]
, there is a functor

(6.11.1)

\mathsf{Rep}(\check{P})\longrightarrow(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})_{x}

which induces an equivalence

\mathsf{Rep}(\check{P})^{\heartsuit}\tilde{\longrightarrow}(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})_{x}^{\heartsuit}.

Since
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{P})^{\heartsuit}
is generated under extensions and direct sums by the image of

\triv_{\check{N}_{\check{P}}}:\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M})^{\heartsuit}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(\check{P})^{\heartsuit},

the claim follows from Proposition
6.10.2
.

Finally, we prove conservativity on eventually coconnective objects. Again, using factorization we can reduce to the case that
I
has a single element. The functor
\Av^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}_{!}
is left adjoint to
\Av^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G}_{*}
, and by the t-exactness statements proved above, the resulting monad on

(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})_{X_{\dR}}

is left t-exact. Thus it suffices to show that this monad is conservative on eventually coconnective objects. Observe that the monad is given by the action of the object

\Av^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G}_{*}\Av^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}_{!}\delta_{1,X}

in
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G,X})
. After étale localization on
X
we have
(\mathfrak{L}G)_{X}\cong(\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}\times X
for a fixed
x\in X(k)
, from which it follows that the object

\Av^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G}_{*}\Av^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}_{!}\delta_{1,X}

has the form
\mathcal{M}\boxtimes\omega_{X}
with respect to the decomposition

\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G,X})\cong\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G,x})\otimes\mathsf{D}(X).

The naïve Satake equivalence implies that for any
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have
H^{i}\mathcal{M}\cong\Sat_{G,x}^{\naive}V
for some
V
in
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})^{\heartsuit}
. Since the monad in question is left t-exact, we have
H^{i}\mathcal{M}=0
for
i<0
, and Theorem
6.5.1
implies that
H^{0}\mathcal{M}=\delta_{1,x}
. Thus it suffices show that the action of the symmetric monoidal category
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})
on
(D(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})_{x}
is left t-exact. This follows from the fact that the functor (
6.11.1
) is
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})
-linear and induces an equivalence on the hearts.

∎

Lemma 6.11.2
.

For any finite set
I
, the t-structure on
(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
satisfies the conditions of Proposition
3.9.2
.

Proof.

Most of the conditions can be easily deduced from the corresponding properties of the t-structure on
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{M})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
, with the exception of coherence.

For coherence, we consider the adjunction of
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
-module categories

\Av^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}_{!}:(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\rightleftarrows(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi,\acc}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}:\Av^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G}_{*}.

Using Theorem
6.8.1
, we can replace the right side by its spectral counterpart:

(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\rightleftarrows\Upsilon(\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{\check{P}})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{0}^{\fact}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M}))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}.

Proposition
6.11.1
says that the left adjoint is t-exact, and also conservative on eventually coconnective objects. By Lemma
2.4.1
, an object in
(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
is almost compact if and only if it is so after applying the left adjoint. Now coherence of the left side follows from coherence of the right side, which was proved in Proposition
5.3.1
.

∎

Thus we can define

\mathsf{Sph}_{G,P}:=(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})^{\ren},

which is
a priori
an object of
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}
. Note that the adjunction (
6.9.1
) renormalizes to a monadic adjunction

\mathsf{Sph}_{M}\rightleftarrows\mathsf{Sph}_{G,P},

and in particular
\mathsf{Sph}_{G,P}
belongs to
\mathsf{FactCat}
because
\mathsf{Sph}_{M}
does.

Proposition 6.11.3
.

The object
\mathsf{Sph}_{G,P}
admits a unique structure of
(\mathsf{Sph}_{G},\mathsf{Sph}_{M})
-bimodule in
\mathsf{FactCat}
compatible with the morphism

\mathsf{Sph}_{G,P}\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M}.

Proof.

By Proposition
3.9.3
, it suffices to show that the
(\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G}),\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{M}))
-bimodule structure on
\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M}
takes place in the pseudo-tensor category
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\otimes}_{\aULA}
. For
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{M})
, this follows from the existence of the right adjoint in (
6.9.1
), which is
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{M})
-linear as well as conservative and left t-exact over
X^{I}_{\dR}
for every finite set
I
. As for
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})
, by right completeness of the t-structure and the naïve Satake equivalence, it is enough to show that any object of
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}^{\heartsuit}
acts by a left t-exact functor. Using factorization, this immediately reduces to the corresponding claim at a point
x\in X(k)
, which was established in the proof of Lemma
6.11.1
.

∎

6.12.
Derived Satake transform for a parabolic

The first step is to construct a morphism

(6.12.1)

\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G}\times\check{M})

in
\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl}
. By Proposition
3.4.1
, this is the same datum as a morphism

\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\otimes\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M}).

By Theorem
6.5.1
, we can identify

\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\tilde{\longrightarrow}\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi},

so we will construct a morphism

\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}\otimes\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M}).

First, convolve in the middle to obtain a morphism

\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}\otimes\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M}\longrightarrow(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}.

Then compose with (
6.7.1
) to obtain the desired morphism (
6.12.1
).

Theorem 6.12.1
.

The image of the unit under the morphism (
6.12.1
) is canonically isomorphic to
\Upsilon(\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{\check{P}},\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}})
as a factorization algebra.

Proof.

We have already seen this in the case
P=G
, where the functor (
6.12.1
) is monoidal and the
\Upsilon(\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{\check{P}},\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}})
is the monoidal unit
\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}}
in
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G}\times\check{G})
.

For the general case, a parabolic variant of Theorem 7.9.1 in
[
Ras21a
]
says that the triangle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Upsilon(\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{\check{P}},-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\ref{semiinfres})}

in
\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl}
commutes. Here the left diagonal morphism is the composite

\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M}\xrightarrow{\Av_{!}^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}}(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi},

where the first functor is given by acting on the unit via
\Sat^{\naive}_{G}
. Since
\Upsilon(\check{\mathfrak{n}},-)
corresponds to
\Upsilon(\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{\check{P}},\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}})
under the equivalence

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl}}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G}),\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M}))\cong\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G}\times\check{M}),

the claim follows.

∎

Applying (
3.5.1
), we therefore obtain a morphism

(6.12.2)

\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M}\longrightarrow\Upsilon(\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{\check{P}},\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G}\times\check{M})).

in
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}
. By construction, it is a morphism of
(\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G}),\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{M}))
-bimodules, where the bimodule structure on the right side comes from the action of

(\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G}\times\check{G})),\mathcal{O}_{\check{M}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M}\times\check{M})))

via (
6.6.1
).

Proposition 6.12.2
.

The image of the morphism (
6.12.2
) is contained in

\Upsilon(\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{\check{P}},\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{0}^{\fact}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G}\times\check{M})).

For any finite set
I
, the resulting functor

(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\Upsilon(\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{\check{P}},\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{0}^{\fact}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G}\times\check{M}))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

is t-exact.

Proof.

The first claim follows from the fact that the image of the left adjoint in (
6.9.1
) generates the target under colimits, and hence the image of (
6.12.2
) is contained in the
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{M})
-submodule category generated by the unit.

Right t-exactness of the functor in question follows from the commutative triangle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Upsilon(\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{\check{P}},\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{0}^{\fact}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G}\times\check{M})),}

where the diagonal functors are given by acting on the unit objects via
\Sat_{M}^{\naive}
. Namely, over each
X^{I}_{\dR}
, the diagonal functors are t-exact and generate their targets under colimits.

Using factorization, left t-exactness reduces to the corresponding claim at a point
x\in X
. By right completeness of the t-structure, it suffices to show that (
6.12.2
) sends any object in

(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})_{x}^{\heartsuit}

to a coconnective object. As in the proof of Proposition
6.11.1
, we have an equivalence

(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})_{x}^{\heartsuit}\cong\mathsf{Rep}(\check{P})^{\heartsuit},

and
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{P})^{\heartsuit}
is generated under extensions and filtered colimits by the image of

\triv_{\check{N}_{\check{P}}}:\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M})^{\heartsuit}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(\check{P})^{\heartsuit}.

In view of the commutative triangle above, the claim follows from Lemma
5.5.1
.

∎

Thus (
6.12.2
) renormalizes to a morphism

\Sat_{G,P}:\mathsf{Sph}_{G,P}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Sph}_{\check{G},\check{P}}^{\spec}

in
\mathsf{FactCat}
. By construction, it is a morphism of
(\mathsf{Sph}_{G},\mathsf{Sph}_{M})
-bimodules, where the bimodule structure on the target comes from
\Sat_{G}
and
\Sat_{M}
.

The following is our second main theorem.

Theorem 6.12.3
.

The morphism
\Sat_{G,P}
is an isomorphism.

The proof of this theorem is contained in §
10
.

6.13.
Reduction to a point

Before proceeding, we show that in order to prove Theorem
6.12.3
(and hence its special case Theorem
6.6.1
), it is enough to show that

\Sat_{G,P,x}:\mathsf{Sph}_{G,P,x}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Sph}_{\check{G},\check{P},x}^{\spec}

is an equivalence for any
x\in X(k)
.

Consider the commutative triangle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Sph}_{G,P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Sph}_{\check{G},\check{P}}^{\spec}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sat_{G,P}}

in
\mathsf{FactCat}
, where the diagonal morphisms are uniquely characterized by
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M})
-linearity. For any finite set
I
, the diagonal functors preserve ULA objects over
X^{I}
and generate their targets, which implies that the functor
\Sat_{G,P}
preserves ULA objects. Thus, by Proposition B.8.1 in
[
Ras21a
]
, it suffices to show that this functor is an equivalence over each stratum in
X^{I}
. Using factorization, we can therefore assume that
I
is a singleton, i.e. we must show that
\Sat_{G,P,X_{\dR}}
is an equivalence.

Choosing an étale coordinate on
X
, we can moreover assume that we have
\mathsf{D}(X)
-linear equivalences

\mathsf{Sph}_{G,P,X_{\dR}}\cong\mathsf{Sph}_{G,P,x}\otimes\mathsf{D}(X)

and

\mathsf{Sph}_{\check{G},\check{P},X_{\dR}}^{\spec}\cong\mathsf{Sph}_{\check{G},\check{P},x}^{\spec}\otimes\mathsf{D}(X)

for a fixed
x\in X(k)
, and that the
\mathsf{D}(X)
-linear functor
\Sat_{G,P,X_{\dR}}
is induced from
\Sat_{G,P,x}
. Thus it suffices to show that

\Sat_{G,P,x}:\mathsf{Sph}_{G,P,x}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Sph}_{\check{G},\check{P},x}^{\spec}

is an equivalence.

7.
The case of a torus

The goal of this section is to formulate and prove Theorem
7.3.1
, a factorizable version of local geometric class field theory in the de Rham setting, then deduce Theorem
6.6.1
in the case
G=T
.

7.1.
Gauge forms

Let
H
denote an arbitrary algebraic group. For any finite set
I
, the space
(\mathfrak{h}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{D})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
is defined as follows: a point
S\to(\mathfrak{h}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{D})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
consists of
x_{I}:S\to X^{I}_{\dR}
together with a section of the vector bundle
(\mathfrak{h}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{X})|_{D_{x_{I}}}
. The space
(\mathfrak{h}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{D})^{x\ram}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
is defined similarly, but the section is allowed to have a pole along
\{x\}\times S
.

These assemble into factorization spaces
\mathfrak{h}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{D}
and
(\mathfrak{h}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{D})^{x\ram}
which are co-unital and corr-unital, respectively. The inclusion

\mathfrak{h}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{D}\longrightarrow(\mathfrak{h}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{D})^{x\ram}

is a morphism of corr-unital factorization spaces.

Fix a point
x\in X(k)
. We let
(\mathfrak{L}^{+}H)^{x\ram}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
be the group classifying maps
D_{x_{I}}\to H
which are possibly meromorphic along
\{x\}\times S
. It also forms a corr-unital factorization space, and receives a homomorphism

\mathfrak{L}^{+}H\longrightarrow(\mathfrak{L}^{+}H)^{x\ram}

compatible with this structure.

The group
(\mathfrak{L}^{+}H)^{x\ram}
acts on
(\mathfrak{h}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{D})^{x\ram}
, compatibly with the factorization structure, via the gauge action (cf. §1.12 of
[
Ras15c
]
). The subgroup
\mathfrak{L}^{+}H
preserves the subspace
\mathfrak{h}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{D}
. Define

\LS_{H}(D):=(\mathfrak{h}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{D})/\mathfrak{L}^{+}H

and

\LS_{H}(D)^{x\ram}:=(\mathfrak{h}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{D})^{x\ram}/(\mathfrak{L}^{+}H)^{x\ram},

a co-unital and corr-unital factorization space respectively. We have a canonical morphism of corr-unital factorization spaces

\iota:\LS_{H}(D)\longrightarrow\LS_{H}(D)^{x\ram}.

The co-unital factorization structure on
\LS_{H}(D)
makes
\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{H}(D))
into an object of
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}
. We recall from
[
Ras16
]
, Lemma 9.8.1 that we have a canonical equivalence

\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{H}(D))\tilde{\longrightarrow}\mathsf{Rep}(H)

in
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}
, and in particular
\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{H}(D))
actually belongs to
\mathsf{FactCat}
.

7.2.
Unital structures

We would like to upgrade
\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{H}(D)^{x\ram})
to a unital factorization category. Given an injection of finite sets
J\to I
, the corr-unital structure on
\LS_{H}(D)^{x\ram}
determines a correspondence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\LS_{H}(D)^{x\ram}_{J\to I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\LS_{H}(D)^{x\ram}_{X^{J}_{\dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\LS_{H}(D)^{x\ram}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta}

The morphism
\beta
is ind-schematic, but generally not schematic unless
H
is unipotent. In particular, the usual direct image of quasicoherent sheaves along this map is poorly behaved.

Proposition 7.2.1
.

If
H
is reductive, then for any injection of finite sets
J\to I
, the functor

\beta^{*}:\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{H}(D)^{x\ram}_{X^{I}_{\dR}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{H}(D)^{x\ram}_{J\to I})

admits a left adjoint.

Proof.

Writing
\LS_{H}(D)^{x\ram,\log}:=(\mathfrak{h}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{D})^{x\ram}/\mathfrak{L}^{+}H
, we can factorize
\beta
as

\LS_{H}(D)^{x\ram}_{J\to I}\xrightarrow{\beta_{1}}\LS_{H}(D)^{x\ram,\log}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\xrightarrow{\beta_{2}}\LS_{H}(D)^{x\ram}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}.

To see that
\beta_{1}^{*}
admits a left adjoint, it suffices to show the same for inverse image along the inclusion

(\mathfrak{h}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{D})^{x\ram}_{J\to I}\longrightarrow(\mathfrak{h}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{D})^{x\ram}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}.

For any
n\geq 0
, let

(\mathfrak{h}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{D})^{x\ram,\leq n}_{J\to I}\subset(\mathfrak{h}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{D})^{x\ram}_{J\to I}

be the closed subscheme where the
1
-form is allowed to have poles of order at most
1
at
x
. Then we have

(\mathfrak{h}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{D})^{x\ram}_{J\to I}=\underset{n}{\colim}\ (\mathfrak{h}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{D})^{x\ram,\leq n}_{J\to I},

and the transition maps are regular closed embeddings. Moreover, the map

(\mathfrak{h}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{D})^{x\ram,\leq n}_{J\to I}\longrightarrow(\mathfrak{h}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{D})^{x\ram,\leq n}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

is a regular closed embedding for any
n\geq 0
, from which the claim follows.

The existence of
(\beta_{2}^{*})^{\LL}
follows from the fact that
\LS_{H}(D)^{x\ram}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
is the quotient of
\LS_{H}(\mathring{D})^{x\ram,\log}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
by a certain Hecke groupoid, which is ind-proper because
H
is reductive.

∎

As a consequence of the proposition, we can endow the object
\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{H}(D)^{x\ram})
with a unital structure and hence view it as an object of
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}
. Explicitly, the structure morphism attached to an injection
J\to I
is given by

(\beta^{*})^{\LL}\alpha^{*}:\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{H}(D)^{x\ram}_{X^{J}_{\dR}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{H}(D)^{x\ram}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}).

As explained in §9.9 of
[
Ras16
]
, the
1
-affineness of
\LS_{H}(D)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
implies that
\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{H}(D)^{x\ram})
factorizes strictly, i.e. belongs to
\mathsf{FactCat}
.

It follows that

(\iota^{*})^{\LL}:\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{H}(D))\longrightarrow\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{H}(D)^{x\ram})

is a morphism in
\mathsf{FactCat}
. Moreover, the pointwise tensor product on
\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{H}(D)^{x\ram})
upgrades it to an object of
\mathsf{ComAlg}(\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl})
. The structure morphisms for this commutative algebra structure are generally only lax unital with respect to factorization, i.e. they do not take place in
\mathsf{FactCat}
. For instance, the morphism

\Vect\longrightarrow\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{H}(D)^{x\ram})

corresponding to the monoidal unit
\mathcal{O}_{\LS_{H}(D)^{x\ram}}
is not strictly unital with respect to factorization, since the map

(\iota^{*})^{\LL}\mathcal{O}_{\LS_{H}(D)}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\LS_{H}(D)^{x\ram}}

from the factorization unit to the monoidal unit is of course not an isomorphism.

7.3.
Local geometric class field theory in the de Rham setting

Let
\Gr_{T}^{\infty\cdot x}
denote the corr-unital factorization space classifying a point of
\Gr_{T}
together with a full level structure at
x
. In particular, the fiber of
\Gr_{T}^{\infty\cdot x}
at
x
identifies with
(\mathfrak{L}T)_{x}
, while its restriction to
X\setminus\{x\}
identifies with
\Gr_{T}
. We will write

p:\Gr_{T}^{\infty\cdot x}\longrightarrow\Gr_{T}

for the projection.

The commutative group structure on
\Gr_{T}^{\infty\cdot x}
endows
\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{T}^{\infty\cdot x})
with a convolution symmetric monoidal structure, which makes it an object of
\mathsf{ComAlg}(\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl})
. This commutative algebra structure takes place in the lax unital setting, corresponding to the fact that the natural morphism

\delta_{1}\longrightarrow\omega_{\mathfrak{L}^{+}T}

in
\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}T)_{x}
is not an isomorphism.

Theorem 7.3.1
.

There is a canonical equivalence

\mathbb{L}_{T}:\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{T}^{\infty\cdot x})\tilde{\longrightarrow}\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{\check{T}}(D)^{x\ram})

in
\mathsf{ComAlg}(\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl})
fitting into a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{T}^{\infty\cdot x})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{\check{T}}(D)^{x\ram})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{T})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{\check{T}}(D)).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{L}_{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\dR,*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

For any
n\geq 0
, let
\Gr_{T}^{n\cdot x}
be the quotient of
\Gr_{T}^{\infty\cdot x}
classifying a point of
\Gr_{T}
together with a structure of level
n
at
x
. To prove the theorem, we will construct a system of isomorphisms

\mathbb{L}_{T}^{(n)}:\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{T}^{n\cdot x})\tilde{\longrightarrow}\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{\check{T}}(D)^{x\ram,\leq n})

in
\mathsf{ComAlg}(\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl})
, and obtain
\mathbb{L}_{T}
by passing to the limit.

7.4.
Duality for quasicoherent sheaves on local systems

Fix a finite set
I
and
n\geq 0
. Write

\pi:\LS_{\check{T}}(D)^{x\ram,\leq n}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow X^{I}_{\dR}

for the projection. We define a
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
-linear functor

\pi_{*,\ren}:\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{\check{T}}(D)^{x\ram,\leq n}_{X^{I}_{\dR}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(X^{I})

as follows. Letting

\rho:\LS_{\check{T}}(D)^{x\ram,\log,\leq n}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow X^{I}_{\dR}

denote the projection, the usual direct image functor

\rho_{*}:\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{\check{T}}(D)^{x\ram,\log,\leq n}_{X^{I}_{\dR}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(X^{I})

is continuous and
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
-linear by Corollary 3.12.2 of
[
Ras15c
]
(the proof there is given at
x
rather than over
X^{I}_{\dR}
, but adapts to the latter setting). The map

\LS_{\check{T}}(D)^{x\ram,\log,\leq n}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\LS_{\check{T}}(D)^{x\ram,\leq n}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

realizes the latter as the quotient of the former by the formal group

\mathcal{G}:=((\mathfrak{L}^{+}\check{T})^{x\ram,\leq n}/\mathfrak{L}^{+}T)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}.

The
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
-module category
\mathsf{IndCoh}(\mathcal{G})
admits a natural symmetric monoidal structure with respect to convolution, which is in fact rigid because
\mathcal{G}
has finite-dimensional tangent space at the identity. Since
\rho
is equivariant for the action of
\mathcal{G}
, the functor
\rho_{*}
therefore factors through

\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{\check{T}}(D)^{x\ram,\log,\leq n}_{X^{I}_{\dR}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{\check{T}}(D)^{x\ram,\log,\leq n}_{X^{I}_{\dR}})_{\mathsf{IndCoh}(\mathcal{G})}\cong\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{\check{T}}(D)^{x\ram,\leq n}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}),

which yields the desired functor
\pi_{*,\ren}
.

Lemma 7.4.1
.

The
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
-linear pairing

\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{\check{T}}(D)^{x\ram,\leq n}_{X^{I}_{\dR}})\underset{\mathsf{D}(X^{I})}{\otimes}\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{\check{T}}(D)^{x\ram,\leq n}_{X^{I}_{\dR}})\xrightarrow{\Delta^{*}}\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{\check{T}}(D)^{x\ram,\leq n}_{X^{I}_{\dR}})\xrightarrow{\pi_{*,\ren}}\mathsf{D}(X^{I})

is perfect. This self-duality makes the following square commute:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{\check{T}}(D)^{x\ram,\leq n}_{X^{I}_{\dR}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{\check{T}}(D)^{x\ram,\leq n}_{X^{I}_{\dR}})^{\vee}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}(X^{I})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}(X^{I})^{\vee}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*,\ren}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\pi^{*})^{\vee}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

Proof.

Since
\rho_{*}
is continuous and

\Delta:\LS_{\check{T}}(D)^{x\ram,\log,\leq n}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\LS_{\check{T}}(D)^{x\ram,\log,\leq n}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\underset{X^{I}_{\dR}}{\times}\LS_{\check{T}}(D)^{x\ram,\log,\leq n}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

is affine, it follows that

\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{\check{T}}(D)^{x\ram,\log,\leq n}_{X^{I}_{\dR}})\underset{\mathsf{D}(X^{I})}{\otimes}\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{\check{T}}(D)^{x\ram,\log,\leq n}_{X^{I}_{\dR}})\xrightarrow{\Delta^{*}}\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{\check{T}}(D)^{x\ram,\log,\leq n}_{X^{I}_{\dR}})\xrightarrow{\rho_{*}}\mathsf{D}(X^{I})

is a perfect pairing. Now the fact that the pairing in lemma is perfect follows formally from the rigidity of
\mathsf{IndCoh}(\mathcal{G})
.

As for the commutativity of the square, note that
\rho_{*}
and
\rho^{*}
are dual by the construction of the self-duality on

\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{\check{T}}(D)^{x\ram,\log,\leq n}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}).

The claim then follows formally from the construction of
\pi_{*,\ren}
.

∎

7.5.
Proof of Theorem
7.3.1

For any
n\geq 0
and any finite set
I
, the Contou-Carrère pairing gives rise to a bimultiplicative line bundle

(7.5.1)

(\Gr_{T,X^{I}}^{n\cdot x})_{\dR}\underset{X^{I}_{\dR}}{\times}\LS_{\check{T}}(D)^{x\ram,\leq n}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\BB\mathbb{G}_{m}

(cf. §6.3 of
[
RH23
]
). Since
\Gr_{T,X^{I}}^{n\cdot x}
is an ind-scheme locally of finite type, Verdier duality defines a perfect pairing

\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{T,X^{I}}^{n\cdot x})\underset{\mathsf{D}(X^{I})}{\otimes}\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{T,X^{I}}^{n\cdot x})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(X^{I}).

Thus the line bundle (
7.5.1
) determines an object of

\mathsf{QCoh}((\Gr_{T,X^{I}}^{n\cdot x})_{\dR}\underset{X^{I}_{\dR}}{\times}\LS_{\check{T}}(D)^{x\ram,\leq n}_{X^{I}_{\dR}})\tilde{\longrightarrow}\Fun_{\mathsf{D}(X^{I})}(\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{T,X^{I}}^{n\cdot x}),\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{\check{T}}(D)^{x\ram,\leq n}_{X^{I}_{\dR}})),

which is the desired
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
-linear functor

\mathbb{L}_{T}^{(n)}:\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{T,X^{I}}^{n\cdot x})\longrightarrow\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{\check{T}}(D)^{x\ram,\leq n}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}).

The bimultiplicativity of (
7.5.1
) implies that
\mathbb{L}_{T}^{(n)}
is symmetric monoidal, and the compatibility of (
7.5.1
) with factorization implies that
\mathbb{L}_{T}^{(n)}
defines a morphism in
\mathsf{ComAlg}(\Fact_{\laxuntl})
as
I
varies.

On the other hand, by Lemma
7.4.1
, the line bundle (
7.5.1
) determines a
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
-linear functor

\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{\check{T}}(D)^{x\ram,\leq n}_{X^{I}_{\dR}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{T,X^{I}}^{n\cdot x}).

Define
{}^{\prime}\mathbb{L}_{T}^{(n)}
to be the composition of this functor with
\inv^{!}
, where

\inv:\Gr_{T,X^{I}}^{n\cdot x}\longrightarrow\Gr_{T,X^{I}}^{n\cdot x}

is inversion of the group law. We claim that
{}^{\prime}\mathbb{L}_{T}^{(n)}
is inverse to
\mathbb{L}_{T}^{(n)}
.

Denote by
\mathcal{L}
the line bundle (
7.5.1
), viewed as an object of

\mathsf{QCoh}((\Gr_{T,X^{I}}^{n\cdot x})_{\dR}\underset{X^{I}_{\dR}}{\times}\LS_{\check{T}}(D)^{x\ram,\leq n}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}).

Write

\upsilon:\Gr_{T,X^{I}}^{n\cdot x}\longrightarrow X^{I}

for the projection.

Lemma 7.5.1
.

There exist canonical isomorphisms

(\upsilon_{\dR,*}\otimes\id)(\mathcal{L})\cong\delta_{1,X^{I}}\quad\text{and}\quad(\id\otimes\pi_{*,\ren})(\mathcal{L})\cong(\iota^{*})^{\LL}\mathcal{O}_{\check{T},X^{I}}.

Here
\mathcal{O}_{\check{T},X^{I}}
denotes the image of
\omega_{X^{I}}
under the functor

\coind_{1}^{\check{T}}:\mathsf{D}(X^{I})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(\check{T})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\cong\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{\check{T}}(D))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}.

Before proving the lemma, let us explain how it implies that
\mathbb{L}_{T}^{(n)}
and
{}^{\prime}\mathbb{L}_{T}^{(n)}
are mutually inverse. Using the self-duality of
\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{T,X^{I}}^{n\cdot x})
, we see that the composition
