{}^{\prime}\mathbb{L}_{T}^{(n)}\circ\mathbb{L}_{T}^{(n)}:\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{T,X^{I}}^{n\cdot x})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{T,X^{I}}^{n\cdot x})

is given by a kernel
\mathcal{K}
in

\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{T,X^{I}}^{n\cdot x}\underset{X^{I}_{\dR}}{\times}\Gr_{T,X^{I}}^{n\cdot x}).

The bimultiplicativity of (
7.5.1
) implies that

\mathcal{K}\cong(\id\otimes\inv^{!})\mult^{!}(\upsilon_{\dR,*}\otimes\id)(\mathcal{L}),

where

\mult:\Gr_{T,X^{I}}^{n\cdot x}\underset{X^{I}_{\dR}}{\times}\Gr_{T,X^{I}}^{n\cdot x}\longrightarrow\Gr_{T,X^{I}}^{n\cdot x}

denotes the multiplication map. The lemma therefore implies that

\mathcal{K}\cong\Delta_{\dR,*}\omega_{\Gr_{T,X^{I}}^{n\cdot x}}

is isomorphic to the kernel corresponding to the identity functor, and hence
{}^{\prime}\mathbb{L}_{T}^{(n)}\circ\mathbb{L}_{T}^{(n)}
is isomorphic to the identity functor. A symmetric argumentshows that
\mathbb{L}_{T}^{(n)}\circ^{\prime}\mathbb{L}_{T}^{(n)}
is isomorphic to the identity.

Proof of Lemma
7.5.1
.

We begin by observing that
\mathbb{L}_{T}^{(n)}
and
{}^{\prime}\mathbb{L}_{T}^{(n)}
are mutually inverse over
X\setminus\{x\}
, where they agree with the standard equivalence

\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{T})\tilde{\longrightarrow}\mathsf{Rep}(\check{T})=\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{\check{T}}(D))

and its inverse respectively.

It follows that
(\upsilon_{\dR,*}\otimes\id)(\mathcal{L})
identifies with
\delta_{1}
away from
x
. The factorization structure on
(\upsilon_{\dR,*}\otimes\id)(\mathcal{L})
therefore upgrades it to an object of

\delta_{1}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{T}^{n\cdot x}))_{x}.

Since
\delta_{1}
is the unit object for the factorization structure on
\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{T})
, restriction along the inclusion

\Gr_{T,x}^{n\cdot x}\longrightarrow\Gr_{T,X^{I}}^{n\cdot x}

determines an equivalence

\delta_{1}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{T}^{n\cdot x}))_{x}\cong\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{T,x}^{n\cdot x}).

It therefore it suffices to show that

(\upsilon_{\dR,*}\otimes\id)(\mathcal{L})|^{!}_{\Gr_{T,x}^{n\cdot x}}\cong\delta_{1}.

Using the fact that
\mathcal{O}_{\check{T}}
is the unit for the factorization structure on
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{T})
, a symmetric argument reduces the construction of the other isomorphism to the claim that

(\id\otimes\pi_{*,\ren})(\mathcal{L})|_{\LS_{\check{T}}(\mathring{D}_{x})^{\leq n}}\cong(\iota^{*})^{\LL}\mathcal{O}_{\check{T}}.

Thus we have reduced to checking that
\mathbb{L}_{T}^{(n)}
and
{}^{\prime}\mathbb{L}_{T}^{(n)}
are mutually inverse over
x
. This is well-known (cf.
[
RH23
]
§6.3): a choice of coordinate at
x
allows one to decompose
\Gr_{T,x}^{n\cdot x}
and
\LS_{\check{T}}(\mathring{D}_{x})^{\leq n}
into direct factors which can be analyzed explicitly.

∎

7.6.
Proof of Theorem
6.6.1
for
G=T

As explained in §
6.13
, it suffices to prove that
\Sat_{T,x}
is an equivalence. Let us reinterpret the monoidal functor

\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{T,x})\xrightarrow{(\ref{presatake})}\mathcal{O}_{\check{T}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{T}\times\check{T}))_{x}.

Being the fiber of the factorization category
\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{T}^{\infty\cdot x})
at
x
, the category
\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}T)_{x}
admits commuting structures of
\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}T)_{x}
-module and factorization
\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{T})
-module at
x
. In particular, it defines a functor

\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}T)_{x}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{T})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x}.

Similarly, the category
\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{\check{T}}(\mathring{D}_{x}))
admits commuting structures of
\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{\check{T}}(\mathring{D}_{x}))
-module and factorization
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{T})
-module at
x
, hence it defines a functor

\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{\check{T}}(\mathring{D}_{x}))\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(\check{T})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x}.

Taking the fiber of
\mathbb{L}_{T}
at
x
, we obtain an equivalence

\mathbbm{L}_{T,x}:\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}T)_{x}\tilde{\longrightarrow}\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{\check{T}}(\mathring{D}_{x}))

of symmetric monoidal categories, which moreover respects the factorization module structures for
\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{T})\cong\mathsf{Rep}(\check{T})
. It therefore gives rise to a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}T)_{x}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{T})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{\check{T}}(\mathring{D}_{x}))\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Rep}(\check{T})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

in
\mathsf{DGCat}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}
.

We have a monoidal equivalence

\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{T,x})\tilde{\longrightarrow}\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}T)_{x}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}}(\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{T,x})),

where the right side means relative inner endomorphisms with respect to the action of
\mathsf{DGCat}
on
\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}T)_{x}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}
. On the other hand, there is a monoidal equivalence

\mathcal{O}_{\check{T}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{T}\times\check{T}))_{x}\tilde{\longrightarrow}\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{Rep}(\check{T})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x}}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{T}))

by
[
Ras16
]
§9.22.

Tracing through the constructions, we see that the previously constructed monoidal functor

\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{T,x})\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\check{T}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{T}\times\check{T}))_{x}

corresponds under the above identifications with the monoidal functor

\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}T)_{x}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}}(\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{T,x}))\longrightarrow\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{Rep}(\check{T})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x}}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{T}))

induced by the upper-right circuit of the above commutative square. Using the commutativity of the square (and hence Theorem
7.3.1
), it follows that this agrees with the monoidal functor induced by the lower-left circuit of the square. But this is an equivalence by Theorem 9.13.1 in
[
Ras16
]
(in fact, the theorem is proved after a series of reductions by establishing precisely this equivalence).

This completes the proof of Theorem
6.6.1
for
G=T
.

8.
Factorization modules at a point

In this section, we will give an explicit description of the category of factorization modules

\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P},\mathcal{O}_{G})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(G)\otimes\mathsf{Rep}(M))_{x}

at a fixed point
x\in X(k)
.

8.1.
Lie-
!
coalgebras

Recall that a
Lie-
!

coalgebra
in
\mathsf{Rep}(H)
is a Lie coalgebra in the symmetric monoidal category
\mathsf{Rep}(H)\otimes\mathsf{D}(X)
. One can give an alternative definition as follows. The category
\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{\Ran_{\dR}}
carries a non-unital symmetric monoidal structure denoted by
\otimes^{*}
(cf.
[
Ras15a
]
§7.17). Note that the restriction functor

\Delta^{!}:\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{\Ran_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X_{\dR}}\cong\mathsf{Rep}(H)\otimes\mathsf{D}(X)

is carries
\otimes^{*}
to
\otimes^{!}
, and in particular lifts to a functor

(\Delta^{!})^{\enh}:\mathsf{LieCoalg}^{\otimes^{*}}(\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{\Ran_{\dR}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{LieCoalg}^{\otimes^{!}}(\mathsf{Rep}(H)\otimes\mathsf{D}(X)).

Proposition 8.1.1
.

The functor
(\Delta^{!})^{\enh}
restricts to an equivalence on the full subcategory of
\mathsf{LieCoalg}^{\otimes^{*}}(\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{\Ran_{\dR}})
consisting of objects whose underlying D-module on
\Ran
is supported on the main diagonal.

Proof.

This follows from the observation that the left adjoint

\Delta_{\dR,*}:\mathsf{Rep}(H)\otimes\mathsf{D}(X)\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{\Ran_{\dR}}

of
\Delta^{!}
is fully faithful and left-lax symmetric monoidal.

∎

There is another non-unital symmetric monoidal structure
\oplus^{*}
on
\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{\Ran_{\dR}}
, which is defined in the same way as
\otimes^{*}
but with
\boxtimes
replaced by
\boxplus
. Note that unlike
\otimes^{*}
, the operation
\oplus^{*}
does not preserve colimits in each variable. The restriction functor

\Delta^{!}:\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{\Ran_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X_{\dR}}\cong\mathsf{Rep}(H)\otimes\mathsf{D}(X)

carries
\oplus^{*}
to
\oplus
, and hence lifts to a functor

(\Delta^{!})^{\enh,\oplus}:\mathsf{ComAlg}^{\oplus^{*}}(\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{\Ran_{\dR}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{ComAlg}^{\oplus}(\mathsf{Rep}(H)\otimes\mathsf{D}(X))\cong\mathsf{Rep}(H)\otimes\mathsf{D}(X).

The following result first appeared in Rozenblyum’s thesis.

Proposition 8.1.2
.

The functor
(\Delta^{!})^{\enh,\oplus}
admits a fully faithful left adjoint

\Fact^{\oplus}:\mathsf{Rep}(H)\otimes\mathsf{D}(X)\longrightarrow\mathsf{ComAlg}^{\oplus^{*}}(\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{\Ran_{\dR}}).

The essential image consists of those objects
\mathcal{M}
such that the structure map
\mathcal{M}\oplus^{*}\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{M}
induces an equivalence

(\mathcal{M}\boxplus\mathcal{M})|_{(\Ran_{\dR}\times\Ran_{\dR})_{\disj}}\tilde{\longrightarrow}(\add^{!}\mathcal{M})|_{(\Ran_{\dR}\times\Ran_{\dR})_{\disj}}.

The category
\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{\Ran_{\dR}}
is also equipped with the “pointwise” tensor product
\otimes^{!}
. Note that for any
\mathcal{M}
in
\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{\Ran_{\dR}}
, the functor
(-)\otimes^{!}\mathcal{M}
is lax symmetric monoidal with respect to
\oplus^{*}
. In particular
\otimes^{!}
naturally lifts to a symmetric monoidal structure on
\mathsf{ComAlg}^{\oplus^{*}}(\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{\Ran_{\dR}})
. The functor
(\Delta^{!})^{\enh,\oplus}
is symmetric monoidal with respect to
\otimes^{!}
, and hence
\Fact^{\oplus}
is left-lax symmetric monoidal. It therefore lifts to a functor

\mathsf{LieCoalg}^{\otimes^{!}}(\mathsf{Rep}(H)\otimes\mathsf{D}(X))\longrightarrow\mathsf{LieCoalg}^{\otimes^{!}}(\mathsf{ComAlg}^{\oplus^{*}}(\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{\Ran_{\dR}})).

8.2.

If
L
is a Lie-
!
coalgebra in
\mathsf{Rep}(H)
, we have seen that the object
\Delta_{\dR,*}L
in
\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{\Ran_{\dR}}
has the structure of Lie coalgebra with respect to
\otimes^{*}
. Given a finite set
I
, we can consider
\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{\Ran_{I,\dR}}
, where
\Ran_{I}
is the version of Ran space with marked points indexed by
I
. This is acted on by
\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{\Ran_{\dR}}
with its convolution product
\otimes^{*}
. In particular, we can define
Lie-
!

comodules
for
L
by

L\text{\textendash}\mathsf{comod}^{!}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}:=\Delta_{\dR,*}L\text{\textendash}\mathsf{comod}^{\otimes^{*}}(\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{\Ran_{I,\dR}})\times_{\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{\Ran_{I,\dR}}}\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}.

That is, an object of
\Delta_{\dR,*}L\text{\textendash}\mathsf{comod}^{\otimes^{*}}(\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{\Ran_{I,\dR}})
is a Lie-
!
comodule if its underlying object of
D(\Ran_{I})
is supported on the main diagonal
X^{I}\to\Ran_{I}
.

The assignment
I\mapsto L\text{\textendash}\mathsf{comod}^{!}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
can be promoted to an object
L\text{\textendash}\mathsf{comod}^{!}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))
in
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}
, similarly to the case of Lie-
*
modules.

On the other hand, we have the object
\Fact^{\oplus}(L)
in
\mathsf{LieCoalg}^{\otimes^{!}}(\mathsf{ComAlg}^{\oplus^{*}}(\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{\Ran_{\dR}}))
. For any finite set
I
, we obtain
\Fact^{\oplus}(L)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
, an object of
\mathsf{LieCoalg}^{\otimes^{!}}(\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}})
. Thus we can consider

\Fact^{\oplus}(L)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{comod}(\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}),

which is acted on by
\mathsf{D}(X^{I})
. Using the commutative
\oplus^{*}
-algebra structure on
\Fact^{\oplus}(L)
, one can assemble these into an object
\Fact^{\oplus}(L)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{comod}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))
of
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}
.

Proposition 8.2.1
.

There is a canonical equivalence

L\text{\textendash}\mathsf{comod}^{!}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))\tilde{\longrightarrow}\Fact^{\oplus}(L)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{comod}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))

in
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}
, which commutes with the forgetful functors to
\mathsf{Rep}(H)
.

Proof.

For any finite set
I
, we construct an equivalence

L\text{\textendash}\mathsf{comod}^{!}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\tilde{\longrightarrow}\Fact^{\oplus}(L)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{comod}(\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}})

which lifts the identity functor on
\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
. The compatibility with factorization structures will be manifest.

Since
\Fact^{\oplus}
is oplax symmetric monoidal with respect to
\otimes^{!}
, so is

\displaystyle\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X_{\dR}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}

\displaystyle\mapsto\Fact^{\oplus}(\mathcal{M})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}.

This defines an oplax action of
\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X_{\dR}}
on
\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
. On the other hand, we have the oplax symmetric monoidal functor

\Delta_{\dR,*}:(\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X_{\dR}},\otimes^{!})\longrightarrow(\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{\Ran_{\dR}},\otimes^{*}).

The action of
(\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{\Ran_{\dR}},\otimes^{*})
on
\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{\Ran_{I,\dR}}
therefore restricts to an oplax action of
\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X_{\dR}}
. Thus it suffices to show that

\Delta_{I,*}:\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{\Ran_{I,\dR}}

is a morphism of oplax
\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X_{\dR}}
-module categories with respect to the above actions. This follows from the observation that the
\mathcal{M}\mapsto\Fact^{\oplus}(\mathcal{M})_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
is isomorphic to the composition of

\displaystyle\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X_{\dR}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{\Ran_{I,\dR}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}

\displaystyle\mapsto\Delta_{\dR,*}(\mathcal{M})\otimes^{*}\Delta_{I,*}\omega_{X^{I}}

with

\Delta_{I}^{!}:\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{\Ran_{I,\dR}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}.

∎

8.3.
Commutative Hopf algebras

Let
\mathbb{O}
be a symmetric monoidal DG category. The functor

\triv_{\mathsf{ComAlg}}:\mathbb{O}\longrightarrow\mathsf{ComAlg}^{\aug}(\mathbb{O})

admits a left adjoint
\coPrim_{\mathsf{ComAlg}}
, and similarly

\triv_{\mathsf{LieCoalg}}:\mathbb{O}\longrightarrow\mathsf{LieCoalg}(\mathbb{O})

admits a right adjoint
\Prim_{\mathsf{LieCoalg}}
. We define

\coChev:=[1]\circ\coPrim_{\mathsf{ComAlg}}\quad\text{and}\quad\Chev:=[-1]\circ\Prim_{\mathsf{LieCoalg}}.

The Koszul duality between the commutative operad and the Lie co-operad means that these functor lift to adjoint functors

\coChev^{\enh}:\mathsf{ComAlg}^{\aug}(\mathbb{O})\rightleftarrows\mathsf{LieCoalg}(\mathbb{O}):\Chev^{\enh}.

Proposition 8.3.1
.

The adjoint functors

\BB_{\mathsf{LieCoalg}(\mathbb{O})^{\op}}:\mathsf{Grp}(\mathsf{LieCoalg}(\mathbb{O})^{\op})\rightleftarrows\mathsf{LieCoalg}(\mathbb{O})^{\op}:\Omega_{\mathsf{LieCoalg}(\mathbb{O})^{\op}}

are equivalences.

Proof.

This is proved in much the same way as Proposition 1.6.4 in Chapter 6 of
[
GR20
]
, the key difference being that the commutativity of the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Grp}(\mathsf{LieCoalg}(\mathbb{O})^{\op})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{LieCoalg}(\mathbb{O})^{\op}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{O}^{\op}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{O}^{\op}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\BB_{\mathsf{LieCoalg}(\mathbb{O})^{\op}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oblv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oblv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[1]_{\mathbb{O}^{\op}}}

is less obvious here. To prove this commutativity, we first rewrite

\mathsf{LieCoalg}(\mathbb{O})^{\op}\cong\mathsf{LieAlg}(\mathbb{O}^{\op}).

We remark that
\mathsf{LieAlg}(\mathbb{O}^{\op})
does not quite fit into the framework of
loc. cit.
, since the tensor product on
\mathbb{O}^{\op}
does not generally preserve colimits, but nonetheless we can consider Lie algebras in this category. Given
L
in
\mathsf{Grp}(\mathsf{LieAlg}(\mathbb{O}^{\op}))
, we note that the image of the simplicial object
(\BB_{\mathsf{LieAlg}(\mathbb{O}^{\op})}L)^{\bullet}
under the forgetful functor

\mathsf{Grp}(\mathsf{LieAlg}(\mathbb{O}^{\op}))\longrightarrow\mathsf{Grp}(\mathbb{O}^{\op})\cong\mathbb{O}^{\op}

(the equivalence here is due to the stability of
\mathbb{O}^{\op}
) is simply the Čech nerve of
L\to 0
. The tensor product on
\mathbb{O}^{\op}
certainly preserves this particular sifted colimit in each variable, since the geometric realization of the Čech nerve in
\mathbb{O}^{\op}
computes the shift
L[1]
. The claim now follows.

∎

The category of commutative Hopf algebras in
\mathbb{O}
is defined by

\mathsf{ComHopfAlg}(\mathbb{O}):=\mathsf{Grp}(\mathsf{ComAlg}^{\aug}(\mathbb{O})^{\op})^{\op}.

By Proposition (
8.3.1
) and its proof, the functor

(\BB_{\mathsf{LieCoalg}(\mathbb{O})^{\op}})^{\op}\circ\mathsf{Grp}(\coChev^{\enh,\op})^{\op}:\mathsf{ComHopfAlg}(\mathbb{O})\longrightarrow\mathsf{LieCoalg}(\mathbb{O})

fits into a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{ComHopfAlg}(\mathbb{O})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{LieCoalg}(\mathbb{O})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{ComAlg}(\mathbb{O})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{O},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oblv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oblv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coPrim_{\mathsf{ComAlg}}}

and hence we denote it by

\coPrim_{\mathsf{ComHopfAlg}}^{\enh}:=(\BB_{\mathsf{LieCoalg}(\mathbb{O})^{\op}})^{\op}\circ\mathsf{Grp}(\coChev^{\enh,\op})^{\op}.

Note that
\coPrim_{\mathsf{ComHopfAlg}}^{\enh}
admits the right adjoint

\UU^{\co}:\mathsf{Grp}(\Chev^{\enh,\op})^{\op}\circ(\Omega_{\mathsf{LieCoalg}(\mathbb{O})^{\op}})^{\op}:\mathsf{LieCoalg}(\mathbb{O})\longrightarrow\mathsf{ComHopfAlg}(\mathbb{O}).

This choice of notation is justified by the following observation.

Proposition 8.3.2
.

The composition

\mathsf{LieCoalg}(\mathbb{O})\xrightarrow{\UU^{\co}}\mathsf{ComHopfAlg}(\mathbb{O})\xrightarrow{\oblv}\mathsf{AssocCoalg}^{\aug}(\mathbb{O})

is right adjoint to the functor

\cores^{\mathsf{AssocCoalg}\to\mathsf{LieCoalg}}:\mathsf{AssocCoalg}^{\aug}(\mathbb{O})\longrightarrow\mathsf{LieCoalg}(\mathbb{O})

of corestriction along the morphism of co-operads
\emph{Assoc}^{*}\to\emph{Lie}^{*}
.

Proof.

The proof is analogous to that of Theorem 6.1.2 in Chapter 6 of
[
GR20
]
.

∎

Corollary 8.3.2.1
.

For any
A
in
\mathsf{ComHopfAlg}(\mathbb{O})
, there is a canonical morphism

\cores^{\mathsf{AssocCoalg}\to\mathsf{LieCoalg}}(A)\longrightarrow\coPrim_{\mathsf{ComHopfAlg}}^{\enh}(A)

in
\mathsf{LieCoalg}(\mathbb{O})
, with the same underlying morphism in
\mathbb{O}
as the unit map

A\longrightarrow\triv_{\mathsf{ComAlg}}\coPrim_{\mathsf{ComAlg}}(A).

In particular, the forgetful functor

A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{comod}(\mathbb{O})\longrightarrow\mathbb{O}

naturally lifts to a functor

A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{comod}(\mathbb{O})\longrightarrow\coPrim_{\mathsf{ComHopfAlg}}^{\enh}(A)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{comod}(\mathbb{O}).

Proof.

The morphism in question can be obtained as the composition

\displaystyle\cores^{\mathsf{AssocCoalg}\to\mathsf{LieCoalg}}(A)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\cores^{\mathsf{AssocCoalg}\to\mathsf{LieCoalg}}(\UU^{\co}(\coPrim_{\mathsf{ComHopfAlg}}^{\enh}(A)))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\coPrim_{\mathsf{ComHopfAlg}}^{\enh}(A).

∎

8.4.
Deformation theory

Let
\pi:Z\to Y
be a morphism of prestacks equipped with a section
\sigma:Y\to Z
which is affine.

Proposition 8.4.1
.

The cotangent complex
T^{*}(Y/Z)
relative to
\sigma
has a natural structure of Lie coalgebra in
\mathsf{QCoh}(Y)
, and
\sigma^{*}
naturally lifts to a functor

(s^{*})^{\enh}:\mathsf{QCoh}(Z)\longrightarrow T^{*}(Y/Z)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{comod}(\mathsf{QCoh}(Y)).

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{G}:=Y\times_{Z}Y
, a group in prestacks affine over
Y
. Without loss of generality, we can replace
Z
by the classifying prestack
\BB_{Y}\mathcal{G}
. Let
A\in\mathsf{ComHopfAlg}(\mathsf{QCoh}(Y)^{\leq 0})
be the direct image of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{G}}
under the projection
\mathcal{G}\to Y
, so we have a canonical equivalence

A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{comod}(\mathsf{QCoh}(Y))\tilde{\longrightarrow}\mathsf{QCoh}(\BB_{Y}\mathcal{G})

which fits into a commutative triangle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{comod}(\mathsf{QCoh}(Y))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{QCoh}(\BB_{Y}\mathcal{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{QCoh}(Y).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oblv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{*}}

Writing
e:Y\to\mathcal{G}
for the unit section, it follows from the definitions that we can identify

\coPrim_{\mathsf{ComAlg}}(A)\cong e^{*}T^{*}(\mathcal{G}/Y)\cong T^{*}(Y/B_{Y}\mathcal{G}).

Using the functor
\coPrim_{\mathsf{ComHopfAlg}}^{\enh}
constructed in the previous section, we obtain a Lie coalgebra structure on
T^{*}(Y/B_{Y}\mathcal{G})
. The second claim follows from Corollary
8.3.2.1
.

∎

8.5.

Now suppose that
\pi:Z\to Y
is a morphism of corr-unital commutative factorization spaces, again equipped with an affine section
\sigma:Y\to Z
. We assume that for any injection of finite sets
J\to I
, the structure map

Y_{I,J}\longrightarrow Y_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

is a regular closed embedding, and likewise for
Z
. This allows us to equip
\mathsf{QCoh}(Y)
and
\mathsf{QCoh}(Z)
with unital structures, and hence upgrade them to objects of
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}
. Namely, if

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{I,J}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{X^{J}_{\dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{X^{I}_{\dR}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta}

is the correspondence defining the corr-unital structure on
Y
, then the corresponding unital structure on
\mathsf{QCoh}(Y)
is given by

(\beta^{*})^{\LL}\alpha^{*}:\mathsf{QCoh}(Y)_{X^{J}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{QCoh}(Y)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}.

Lemma 8.5.1
.

There is a canonical isomorphism of Lie coalgebras

\Fact^{\oplus}(T^{*}(Y_{X_{\dR}}/Z_{X_{\dR}}))\tilde{\longrightarrow}T^{*}(Y_{\Ran_{\dR}}/Z_{\Ran_{\dR}})

in
\mathsf{QCoh}(Y_{\Ran_{\dR}})
.

Proof.

The commutative factorization structures on
Y
and
Z
induce a structure of commutative
\oplus^{*}
-algebra on
T^{*}(Y_{\Ran_{\dR}}/Z_{\Ran_{\dR}})
, which moreover lies in the essential image of
\Fact^{\oplus}
by Proposition
8.1.2
. It therefore suffices to produce an isomorphism of Lie-
!
coalgebras

T^{*}(Y_{X_{\dR}}/Z_{X_{\dR}})\tilde{\longrightarrow}(\Delta^{!})^{\enh}T^{*}(Y_{\Ran_{\dR}}/Z_{\Ran_{\dR}}),

which is immediate from the definitions.

∎

Proposition 8.5.2
.

The functor
\sigma^{*}
lifts to a morphism

\mathsf{QCoh}(Z)\longrightarrow T^{*}(Y_{X_{\dR}}/Z_{X_{\dR}})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{comod}^{!}(\mathsf{QCoh}(Y))

in
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}
.

Proof.

This follows from Propositions
8.4.1
and
8.2.1
in view of Lemma
8.5.1
.

∎

8.6.
Deformations of local systems

Let us specialize to the situation

\pi:\LS_{P}(D)^{x\ram}\underset{\LS_{M}(D)^{x\ram}}{\times}\LS_{M}(D)\rightleftarrows\LS_{M}(D):\sigma.

We begin with a geometric observation.

Proposition 8.6.1
.

For any injection of finite sets
J\to I
, the corr-unital structure map

\LS_{P}(D)^{x\ram}_{J\to I}\underset{\LS_{M}(D)^{x\ram}_{J\to I}}{\times}\LS_{M}(D)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\LS_{P}(D)^{x\ram}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\underset{\LS_{M}(D)^{x\ram}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}}{\times}\LS_{M}(D)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

is a regular closed embedding. Moreover, the section

\sigma:\LS_{M}(D)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\LS_{P}(D)^{x\ram}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\underset{\LS_{M}(D)^{x\ram}_{X^{I}_{\dR}}}{\times}\LS_{M}(D)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

is affine.

Proof.

Since
N_{P}
is unipotent, the canonical map

((\mathfrak{p}\otimes\Omega_{D}^{1})^{x\ram,\leq 1}_{J\to I}\underset{(\mathfrak{m}\otimes\Omega_{D}^{1})^{x\ram,\leq 1}_{J\to I}}{\times}(\mathfrak{m}\otimes\Omega_{D}^{1})_{X^{I}_{\dR}})/(\mathfrak{L}^{+}P)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\LS_{P}(D)^{x\ram}_{J\to I}\underset{\LS_{M}(D)^{x\ram}_{J\to I}}{\times}\LS_{M}(D)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

is an isomorphism. Both assertions follow readily from this presentation.

∎

The proposition allows us to endow

\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{P}(D)^{x\ram}\underset{\LS_{M}(D)^{x\ram}}{\times}\LS_{M}(D))

with a unital structure, and hence view it as an object of
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}
. As shown in §9.9 of
[
Ras16
]
, the
1
-affineness of
\LS_{P}(D)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
implies that the factorization structure is strict, i.e. this object belongs to
\mathsf{FactCat}
.

The object
j_{!}j^{!}(\mathfrak{n}_{P}^{*}\otimes\omega_{X})
inherits a structure of Lie-
!
coalgebra in
\mathsf{Rep}(M)
from
\mathfrak{n}_{P}^{*}\otimes\omega_{X}
. It is Verdier dual to the Lie-
*
algebra
j_{*}j^{*}(\mathfrak{n}_{P}\otimes k_{X})
which appears in §8 of
[
Ras16
]
.

Proposition 8.6.2
.

The relative cotangent complex of

\sigma:\LS_{M}(D)_{X_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\LS_{P}(D)^{x\ram}_{X_{\dR}}\underset{\LS_{M}(D)^{x\ram}_{X_{\dR}}}{\times}\LS_{M}(D)_{X_{\dR}}

is canonically isomorphic to
j_{!}j^{!}(\mathfrak{n}_{P}^{*}\otimes\omega_{X})
as a Lie-
!
coalgebra in
\mathsf{Rep}(M)
.

Proof.

Let
U:=X\setminus\{x\}
. Since

\LS_{H}(D)^{x\ram}_{X_{\dR}}\underset{X_{\dR}}{\times}U_{\dR}=\LS_{H}(D)_{U_{\dR}}=\mathbb{B}H\times U_{\dR},

we have

j^{!}T^{*}(\sigma)\cong T^{*}(\LS_{M}(D)_{U_{\dR}}/\LS_{P}(D)_{U_{\dR}})\cong\mathfrak{n}_{P}^{*}\otimes\omega_{U}\cong j^{!}(\mathfrak{n}_{P}^{*}\otimes\omega_{X}),

from which we obtain a morphism of Lie-
!
coalgebras

j_{!}j^{!}(\mathfrak{n}_{P}^{*}\otimes\omega_{X})\longrightarrow T^{*}(\sigma).

It is an isomorphism over
U
by construction, so it suffices to prove that

i^{!}j_{!}j^{!}(\mathfrak{n}_{P}^{*}\otimes\omega_{X})\longrightarrow i^{!}T^{*}(\sigma)

is an isomorphism, where
i:\{x\}\to X
is the inclusion.

Observe that

i^{!}T^{*}(\sigma)\cong T^{*}(\LS_{M}(D_{x})/\LS_{P}(\mathring{D}_{x})\underset{\LS_{M}(\mathring{D}_{x})}{\times}\LS_{M}(D_{x}))\cong\mathfrak{n}_{P}^{*}\otimes\CC^{\dR}(\mathring{D}_{x}).

By construction, the morphism in question is given by tensoring the canonical isomorphism

i^{!}j_{!}\omega_{U}\tilde{\longrightarrow}\CC^{\dR}(\mathring{D}_{x})

with
\mathfrak{n}_{P}^{*}
.

∎

Corollary 8.6.2.1
.

The functor
\sigma^{*}
lifts to a morphism

\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{P}(D)^{x\ram}\underset{\LS_{M}(D)^{x\ram}}{\times}\LS_{M}(D))\longrightarrow j_{*}j^{*}(\mathfrak{n}_{P}\otimes k_{X})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{*}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))

in
\mathsf{FactCat}
.

Proof.

Observe that

\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{H}(D)_{X_{\dR}})=\mathsf{QCoh}(\mathbb{B}H\times X_{\dR})=\mathsf{Rep}(H)\otimes\mathsf{D}(X)

is canonically self-dual via the self-duality on
\mathsf{Rep}(H)
and
\mathsf{D}(X)
. The resulting equivalence

\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X}^{\cc,\op}\tilde{\longrightarrow}\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X}^{\cc}

lifts to a contravariant equivalence
L\mapsto\mathbb{D}(L)
between Lie coalgebras in
\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X_{\dR}}
(a.k.a. Lie-
!
coalgebras in
\mathsf{Rep}(H)
) whose underlying object of
\mathsf{Rep}(H)_{X_{\dR}}
is compact and Lie-
*
algebras in
\mathsf{Rep}(H)
satisfying the same condition. If
L
is such a Lie-
*
algebra, then there is a tautological equivalence

L\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{*}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\tilde{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{D}(L)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{comod}^{!}(\mathsf{Rep}(H))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}

which upgrades to an isomorphism in
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}
.

The fact that
j_{*}j^{*}(\mathfrak{n}_{P}\otimes k_{X})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{*}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))
belongs to
\mathsf{FactCat}
(as opposed to merely
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}
) follows from the existence of the monadic adjunction

\ind:\mathfrak{n}_{P}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))\rightleftarrows j_{*}j^{*}(\mathfrak{n}_{P}\otimes k_{X})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{*}(\mathsf{Rep}(M)):\oblv

in
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}_{\laxuntl}
and the strictness of the factorization structure on
\mathfrak{n}_{P}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))
.

∎

8.7.

Consider the canonical morphism of factorization spaces

\iota:\LS_{P}(D)\longrightarrow\LS_{P}(D)^{x\ram}\underset{\LS_{M}(D)^{x\ram}}{\times}\LS_{M}(D).

Proposition
8.6.1
implies that
\iota^{*}
admits a left adjoint
(\iota^{*})^{\LL}
.

Proposition 8.7.1
.

The functor
(\iota^{*})^{\LL}
fits into a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Rep}(P)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{n}_{P}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{P}(D)^{x\ram}\underset{\LS_{M}(D)^{x\ram}}{\times}\LS_{M}(D))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{*}j^{*}(\mathfrak{n}_{P}\otimes k_{X})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{*}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\iota^{*})^{\LL}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\res^{N_{P}}_{\mathfrak{n}_{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s^{*})^{\enh}}

in
\mathsf{FactCat}
, where the right vertical functor is given by induction along the morphism of Lie-
*
algebras
\mathfrak{n}_{P}\otimes k_{X}\to j_{*}j^{*}(\mathfrak{n}_{P}\otimes k_{X})
.

Proof.

The square in question tautologically commutes if the vertical functors are replaced with their right adjoints. Thus we must show that the resulting natural transformation

\ind_{\mathfrak{n}_{P}\otimes k_{X}}^{j_{*}j^{*}(\mathfrak{n}_{P}\otimes k_{X})}\res_{\mathfrak{n}_{P}}^{N_{P}}\longrightarrow(s^{*})^{\enh}(\iota^{*})^{\LL}

is an isomorphism. For any finite set
I
, both composite functors are
\mathsf{Rep}(M)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
-linear, and since
\mathsf{Rep}(P)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
is generated as a
\mathsf{Rep}(M)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
-module by
\triv_{P}(\omega_{X^{I}})
, it suffices to check that this natural transformation is an isomorphism after evaluating on this object. Applying the conservative functor

\oblv:j_{*}j^{*}(\mathfrak{n}_{P}\otimes k_{X})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{*}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))_{X^{I}_{\dR}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(M)_{X^{I}_{\dR}},

we have reduced to proving that

\ind_{\mathfrak{n}_{P}\otimes k_{X}}^{j_{*}j^{*}(\mathfrak{n}_{P}\otimes k_{X})}\triv_{\mathfrak{n}_{P}}(\omega_{X^{I}})\longrightarrow s^{*}(\iota^{*})^{\LL}\triv_{P}(\omega_{X^{I}})

is an isomorphism in
\mathsf{Rep}(M)_{X^{I}_{\dR}}
. Using factorization, we we can assume that
I
is a singleton. Over
U=X\setminus\{x\}
, then this morphism is simply the identity map on
\triv_{M}(\omega_{U_{\dR}})
, so it suffices to check that the induced map on the fiber at
x
is an isomorphism.

In particular, we have reduced to proving the commutativity of the square in question at the point
x
. Then we are in the realm of finite-dimensional deformation theory, and it can be identified with the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{P}(D_{x}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{P}(D_{x})^{\wedge}_{\LS_{M}(D_{x})})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{P}(\mathring{D}_{x})\underset{\LS_{M}(\mathring{D}_{x})}{\times}\LS_{M}(D_{x}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{QCoh}((\LS_{P}(\mathring{D}_{x})\underset{\LS_{M}(\mathring{D}_{x})}{\times}\LS_{M}(D_{x}))^{\wedge}_{\LS_{M}(D_{x})}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\iota^{*})^{\LL}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\widehat{\iota}^{*})^{\LL}}

where the horizontal morphisms are given by inverse image and

\widehat{\iota}:\LS_{P}(D_{x})^{\wedge}_{\LS_{M}(D_{x})}\longrightarrow(\LS_{P}(\mathring{D}_{x})\underset{\LS_{M}(\mathring{D}_{x})}{\times}\LS_{M}(D_{x}))^{\wedge}_{\LS_{M}(D_{x})}

is obtained from
\iota
by formal completion along
\LS_{M}(D_{x})
. All of the stacks appearing here are formally smooth, and for such stacks we have the equivalence

\Upsilon_{Y}:\mathsf{QCoh}(Y)\tilde{\longrightarrow}\mathsf{IndCoh}(Y),

which intertwines inverse image for
\mathsf{QCoh}
with
!
-inverse image for
\mathsf{IndCoh}
. Since the maps
\iota
and
\widehat{\iota}
are proper, the above square can be identified with

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{IndCoh}(\LS_{P}(D_{x}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{IndCoh}(\LS_{P}(D_{x})^{\wedge}_{\LS_{M}(D_{x})})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{IndCoh}(\LS_{P}(\mathring{D}_{x})\underset{\LS_{M}(\mathring{D}_{x})}{\times}\LS_{M}(D_{x}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{IndCoh}((\LS_{P}(\mathring{D}_{x})\underset{\LS_{M}(\mathring{D}_{x})}{\times}\LS_{M}(D_{x}))^{\wedge}_{\LS_{M}(D_{x})}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\iota}_{*}}

which commutes by base change for the standard functors on
\mathsf{IndCoh}
.

∎

In particular, it follows that
(s^{*})^{\enh}
induces a morphism

(8.7.1)

\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{P}(\mathring{D}_{x})\underset{\LS_{M}(\mathring{D}_{x})}{\times}\LS_{M}(D_{x}))\longrightarrow j_{*}j^{*}(\mathfrak{n}_{P}\otimes k_{X})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{*}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))_{x}

of factorization
\mathsf{Rep}(P)
-module categories at
x
.

Proposition 8.7.2
.

The morphism (
8.7.1
) restricts to an equivalence on the full subcategory

\mathsf{QCoh}((\LS_{P}(\mathring{D}_{x})\underset{\LS_{M}(\mathring{D}_{x})}{\times}\LS_{M}(D_{x}))^{\wedge}_{\LS_{M}(D_{x})}),

embedded via the left adjoint of the restriction functor

\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{P}(\mathring{D}_{x})\underset{\LS_{M}(\mathring{D}_{x})}{\times}\LS_{M}(D_{x}))\longrightarrow\mathsf{QCoh}((\LS_{P}(\mathring{D}_{x})\underset{\LS_{M}(\mathring{D}_{x})}{\times}\LS_{M}(D_{x}))^{\wedge}_{\LS_{M}(D_{x})}).

Proof.

Identify (
8.7.1
) with the composition

\displaystyle\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{P}(\mathring{D}_{x})\underset{\LS_{M}(\mathring{D}_{x})}{\times}\LS_{M}(D_{x}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Upsilon}}{{\tilde{\longrightarrow}}}\mathsf{IndCoh}(\LS_{P}(\mathring{D}_{x})\underset{\LS_{M}(\mathring{D}_{x})}{\times}\LS_{M}(D_{x}))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathsf{IndCoh}((\LS_{P}(\mathring{D}_{x})\underset{\LS_{M}(\mathring{D}_{x})}{\times}\LS_{M}(D_{x}))^{\wedge}_{\LS_{M}(D_{x})})

as in the proof of Proposition
8.7.1
.

∎

8.8.
Factorization modules for
\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P})

The lax factorization category
\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{H}(D)^{x\ram})
makes
\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{H}(\mathring{D}_{x}))
a factorization
\mathsf{Rep}(H)
-module at
x
. The action of
\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{\Gamma}(\mathring{D}_{x}))
on itself by tensor product of quasicoherent sheaves commutes with the factorization
\mathsf{Rep}(H)
-module structure. This yields a functor

\displaystyle\Phi_{H}:\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{H}(\mathring{D}_{x}))\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(H)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x}

which commutes with the forgetful functors to
\mathsf{DGCat}
. On the level of objects, this means that there is a natural factorization
\mathsf{Rep}(H)
-module structure on any
\mathcal{C}
in
\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{H}(\mathring{D}_{x}))\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}
coming from the factorization module structure on the second factor of

\mathcal{C}\otimes_{\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{H}(\mathring{D}_{x}))}\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{H}(\mathring{D}_{x}))=\mathcal{C}.

The restriction functor

\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{H}(\mathring{D}_{x}))\longrightarrow\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{H}(\mathring{D}_{x})^{\wedge}_{\LS_{H}(D_{x})})

admits a fully faithful
\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{H}(\mathring{D}_{x}))
-linear left adjoint. It follows that the restriction of scalars functor

\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{H}(\mathring{D}_{x})^{\wedge}_{\LS_{H}(D_{x})})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}\longrightarrow\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{H}(\mathring{D}_{x}))\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}

is fully faithful.

Theorem 8.8.1
.

The restriction of
\Phi_{H}
to the full subcategory
\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{H}(\mathring{D}_{x})^{\wedge}_{\LS_{H}(D_{x})})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}
is fully faithful.

Proof.

This is Theorem 9.13.1 of
[
Ras16
]
.

∎

Proposition 8.8.2
.

There is a canonical isomorphism

\Phi_{P}(\mathsf{QCoh}((\LS_{P}(\mathring{D}_{x})\underset{\LS_{M}(\mathring{D}_{x})}{\times}\LS_{M}(D_{x}))^{\wedge}_{\LS_{M}(D_{x})}))\tilde{\longrightarrow}\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))_{x}

in
\mathsf{Rep}(P)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x}
, where
\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{P}(\mathring{D}_{x})^{\wedge}_{\LS_{P}(D_{x})})
acts on

\mathsf{QCoh}((\LS_{P}(\mathring{D}_{x})\underset{\LS_{M}(\mathring{D}_{x})}{\times}\LS_{M}(D_{x}))^{\wedge}_{\LS_{M}(D_{x})})

via restriction along the composition

\displaystyle(\LS_{P}(\mathring{D}_{x})\underset{\LS_{M}(\mathring{D}_{x})}{\times}\LS_{M}(D_{x}))^{\wedge}_{\LS_{M}(D_{x})}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow(\LS_{P}(\mathring{D}_{x})\underset{\LS_{M}(\mathring{D}_{x})}{\times}\LS_{M}(D_{x}))^{\wedge}_{\LS_{P}(D_{x})}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\LS_{P}(\mathring{D}_{x})^{\wedge}_{\LS_{P}(D_{x})},

and the factorization
\mathsf{Rep}(P)
-module structure on
\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))_{x}
arises from the morphism

\mathsf{Rep}(P)\longrightarrow\mathfrak{n}_{P}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))\xrightarrow{\ind^{*\to\fact}}\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))_{x}

in
\mathsf{FactCat}
.

Proof.

Lemma 8.16.1 of
[
Ras16
]
says that

\ind^{*\to\ch}:j_{*}j^{*}(\mathfrak{n}_{P}\otimes k_{X})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{*}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))_{x}\tilde{\longrightarrow}\UU^{\ch}(j_{*}j^{*}(\mathfrak{n}_{P}\otimes k_{X}))\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\ch}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))_{x}

is an equivalence. Identifying

j^{*}\UU^{\ch}(j_{*}j^{*}(\mathfrak{n}_{P}\otimes k_{X}))\tilde{\longrightarrow}j^{*}\UU^{\ch}(\mathfrak{n}_{P}\otimes k_{X})

as chiral algebras on
X\setminus\{x\}
and applying Proposition
5.1.1
, we obtain an equivalence

j_{*}j^{*}(\mathfrak{n}_{P}\otimes k_{X})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{*}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))_{x}\tilde{\longrightarrow}\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fact}(\mathsf{Rep}(M))_{x}.

By construction, this equivalence respects the factorization
\mathsf{Rep}(P)
-module structures.

We can apply
\Phi_{P}
to the
\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{P}(\mathring{D}_{x}))
-linear functor

(\iota_{x}^{*})^{\LL}:\mathsf{Rep}(P)=\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{P}(D_{x}))\longrightarrow\mathsf{QCoh}((\LS_{P}(\mathring{D}_{x})\underset{\LS_{M}(\mathring{D}_{x})}{\times}\LS_{M}(D_{x}))^{\wedge}_{\LS_{M}(D_{x})})

to obtain a morphism in
\mathsf{Rep}(P)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\fact}_{x}
. Inspecting the constructions, we see that taking the fiber at
x
of the morphism
(\iota^{*})^{\LL}
in
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}
from Proposition
8.7.1
yields the same morphism. Now apply Proposition
8.7.2
.

∎

8.9.
Spectral Hecke category at a point

Finally, we deduce the promised description of factorization
\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n},\mathcal{O}_{G})
-modules at
x
.

Theorem 8.9.1
.

There is a canonical equivalence

\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P},\mathcal{O}_{G})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times M))_{x}\tilde{\longrightarrow}\mathsf{QCoh}((\LS_{P}(\mathring{D}_{x})\underset{\LS_{G\times M}(\mathring{D}_{x})}{\times}\LS_{G\times M}(D_{x}))^{\wedge}_{\LS_{M}(D_{x})}).

Proof.

As explained in
[
Ras16
]
§§9.22-24, for any factorization
\mathsf{Rep}(G)
-module category
\mathcal{C}
at
x
, we can identify

\mathcal{O}_{G}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(G)\otimes\mathcal{C})_{x}\tilde{\longrightarrow}\Fun_{\mathsf{Rep}(G)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x}}(\mathsf{Rep}(G),\mathcal{C}).

Here we view the diagonal bimodule
\mathcal{O}_{G}
as a factorization algebra in
\mathsf{Rep}(G\times G)
, and
\mathsf{Rep}(G)\otimes\mathcal{C}
as a factorization
\mathsf{Rep}(G\times G)
-module at
x
. Moreover, if
\mathcal{C}
lies in the essential image of

\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{G}(\mathring{D}_{x})^{\wedge}_{\LS_{G}(D_{x})})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}

under
\Phi_{G}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\mathsf{Rep}(G)\underset{\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{G}(\mathring{D}_{x})^{\wedge}_{\LS_{G}(D_{x})})}{\otimes}\mathcal{C}

\displaystyle\tilde{\longrightarrow}\Fun_{\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{G}(\mathring{D}_{x})^{\wedge}_{\LS_{G}(D_{x})})}(\mathsf{Rep}(G),\mathcal{C})

\displaystyle\tilde{\longrightarrow}\Fun_{\mathsf{Rep}(G)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x}}(\mathsf{Rep}(G),\mathcal{C})

\displaystyle\tilde{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{O}_{G}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(G)\otimes\mathcal{C})_{x},

where we used Theorem
8.8.1
for the second equivalence. The first equivalence uses the observation that
\mathsf{Rep}(G)
is dualizable and self-dual as an object of
\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{G}(\mathring{D}_{x})^{\wedge}_{\LS_{G}(D_{x})})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}
, cf.
loc. cit.
§9.18.

We now apply the above in the case

\mathcal{C}=\mathsf{QCoh}((\LS_{P}(\mathring{D}_{x})\underset{\LS_{M}(\mathring{D}_{x})}{\times}\LS_{M}(D_{x}))^{\wedge}_{\LS_{M}(D_{x})}),

viewed as a
\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{G}(\mathring{D}_{x})^{\wedge}_{\LS_{G}(D_{x})})
-module via inverse image along the projection

\LS_{P}(\mathring{D}_{x})^{\wedge}_{\LS_{P}(D_{x})}\longrightarrow\LS_{G}(\mathring{D}_{x})^{\wedge}_{\LS_{G}(D_{x})}.

Note that by Lemma
3.6.1
, we have an equivalence

\mathcal{O}_{G}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(G)\otimes\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(M)))_{x}\tilde{\longrightarrow}\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P},\mathcal{O}_{G})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times M))_{x}.

Applying Proposition
8.8.2
and the equivalence in the previous paragraph identifies the right hand side with

\mathsf{Rep}(G)\underset{\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{G}(\mathring{D}_{x})^{\wedge}_{\LS_{G}(D_{x})})}{\otimes}\mathsf{QCoh}((\LS_{P}(\mathring{D}_{x})\underset{\LS_{M}(\mathring{D}_{x})}{\times}\LS_{M}(D_{x}))^{\wedge}_{\LS_{M}(D_{x})}).

The morphism

\LS_{G}(D_{x})\longrightarrow\LS_{G}(\mathring{D}_{x})^{\wedge}_{\LS_{G}(D_{x})},

being isomorphic to
\{0\}/G\to\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}_{0}/G
, is affine, which implies that the above tensor product identifies with

\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{G}(D_{x})\underset{\LS_{G}(\mathring{D}_{x})^{\wedge}_{\LS_{G}(D_{x})}}{\times}(\LS_{P}(\mathring{D}_{x})\underset{\LS_{M}(\mathring{D}_{x})}{\times}\LS_{M}(D_{x}))^{\wedge}_{\LS_{M}(D_{x})}).

Finally, using the identification

\LS_{H}(\mathring{D}_{x})^{\wedge}_{\LS_{H}(D_{x})}\tilde{\longrightarrow}\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}_{0}/H,

it is easy to see that the canonical map from the above fiber product to

\LS_{G}(D_{x})\underset{\LS_{G}(\mathring{D}_{x})}{\times}\LS_{P}(\mathring{D}_{x})\underset{\LS_{M}(\mathring{D}_{x})}{\times}\LS_{M}(D_{x})=\LS_{P}(\mathring{D}_{x})\underset{\LS_{G\times M}(\mathring{D}_{x})}{\times}\LS_{G\times M}(D_{x})

factors through an isomorphism with the formal completion of the latter along
\LS_{M}(D_{x})
.

∎

Corollary 8.9.1.1
.

The monad on
\mathsf{Rep}(P)
induced by the adjunction

\mathsf{Rep}(P)\rightleftarrows\mathsf{Sph}^{\spec}_{G,P,x},

where the left adjoint is (
5.6.2
), is isomorphic to the monad given by the associative algebra
\Sym((\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{n}_{P})[-2])
.

Proof.

Observe that the equivalence of Theorem
8.9.1
restricts to an equivalence

\Upsilon(\mathfrak{n}_{P},\mathcal{O}_{G})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{0}^{\fact}(\mathsf{Rep}(G\times M))_{x}\tilde{\longrightarrow}\mathsf{QCoh}((\LS_{P}(\mathring{D}_{x})\underset{\LS_{G\times M}(\mathring{D}_{x})}{\times}\LS_{G\times M}(D_{x}))^{\wedge}_{\LS_{P}(D_{x})}).

We claim that this equivalence is t-exact with respect to the usual t-structure on quasicoherent sheaves: recall that

(\LS_{P}(\mathring{D}_{x})\underset{\LS_{G\times M}(\mathring{D}_{x})}{\times}\LS_{G\times M}(D_{x}))^{\wedge}_{\LS_{P}(D_{x})}\cong(\mathfrak{n}_{P}\underset{\mathfrak{g}}{\times}\{0\})/P,

and in particular this is an algebraic stack of finite type. The t-structure on

\mathsf{QCoh}((\mathfrak{n}_{P}\underset{\mathfrak{g}}{\times}\{0\})/P)

is uniquely characterized by the fact that direct image along

\{0\}/P\longrightarrow(\mathfrak{n}_{P}\underset{\mathfrak{g}}{\times}\{0\})/P

is t-exact, whence the claim.

It follows that the above equivalence preserves coherence, and hence renormalizes to

\mathsf{Sph}_{G,P,x}^{\spec}\tilde{\longrightarrow}\mathsf{IndCoh}((\LS_{P}(\mathring{D}_{x})\underset{\LS_{G\times M}(\mathring{D}_{x})}{\times}\LS_{G\times M}(D_{x}))^{\wedge}_{\LS_{P}(D_{x})}).

The functor (
5.6.2
) corresponds to direct image of ind-coherent sheaves along

\iota:\LS_{P}(D_{x})\longrightarrow(\LS_{P}(\mathring{D}_{x})\underset{\LS_{G\times M}(\mathring{D}_{x})}{\times}\LS_{G\times M}(D_{x}))^{\wedge}_{\LS_{P}(D_{x})},

where we identify

\mathsf{IndCoh}(\LS_{P}(D_{x}))\cong\mathsf{QCoh}(\LS_{P}(D_{x}))\cong\mathsf{Rep}(P)

using the t-exact functor
\Psi
. After a choice of formal coordinate at
x
, this becomes the map

\{0\}/P\longrightarrow(\mathfrak{n}_{P}\underset{\mathfrak{g}}{\times}\{0\})/P.

If we identify

\Gamma(\mathfrak{n}_{P}\underset{\mathfrak{g}}{\times}\{0\},\mathcal{O})=\Sym((\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{n}_{P})^{*}[1]),

then we have

\iota^{!}\iota_{*}V=\Hom_{\Sym((\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{n}_{P})^{*}[1])}(k,V)=\Sym((\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{n}_{P})[-2])\otimes V

for any
V
in
\mathsf{Rep}(P)
, i.e. the monad
\iota^{!}\iota_{*}
is given by the algebra
\Sym((\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{n}_{P})[-2])
Koszul dual to
\Sym((\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{n}_{P})^{*}[1])
, as desired.

∎

9.
The equivalence for
P=G

In this section, we complete the proof of the factorizable derived Satake equivalence, as formulated in Theorem
6.6.1
.

9.1.
The derived Satake transform as a morphism of monads

By the construction of the morphism (
6.6.1
), we have a commutative triangle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G}\times\check{G}))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sat_{G}^{\naive}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oblv^{\operatorname{com}\to\operatorname{fact}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\ref{presatake})}

in
\mathsf{FactCat}^{\laxfact}
. After renormalization, we obtain a commutative triangle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Sph}_{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Sph}_{\check{G}}^{\spec}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sat_{G}}

in
\mathsf{FactCat}
. By Lemma
6.3.1
and Theorem
4.6.1
respectively, the diagonal functors preserve ULA objects over each
X^{I}
, hence admit factorizable right adjoints. Moreover, their right adjoints are conservative and hence monadic. The above triangle therefore corresponds to a morphism

(9.1.1)

\Psi_{G}\longrightarrow\Psi_{\check{G}}^{\spec}

between the corresponding monads on
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})
in
\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl}
, which we will prove is an isomorphism. As previously explained, Theorem
6.6.1
will follow if we prove that the morphism of monads

\Psi_{G,x}\longrightarrow\Psi_{\check{G},x}^{\spec}

is an isomorphism over each
x\in X(k)
.

9.2.
Explicit description of the monads at a point

We will need the following lemma.

Lemma 9.2.1
.

Let
H
be an affine group scheme and suppose that
\mathsf{Rep}(H)
acts on a DG category
\mathcal{C}
. For any objects
c,d
in
\mathcal{C}
, we have a canonical isomorphism

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathsf{Rep}(H)}(c,d)\tilde{\longrightarrow}\Hom_{\mathcal{C}}(c,\mathcal{O}_{H}\star d)

in
\mathsf{Rep}(H)
, where the
H
-action on the right side arises by functoriality from the
H
-action on
\mathcal{O}_{H}
by right translations.

Now we deduce an explicit description of the monad
\Psi_{G,x}
from a well-known equivariant cohomology calculation originally due to Ginzburg.

Proposition 9.2.2
.

The monad

\Psi_{G,x}:\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Sph}_{G,x}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})

is isomorphic to the monad given by the associative algebra
\Sym(\check{\mathfrak{g}}[-2])
.

Proof.

The functor

\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Sph}_{G,x}

obtained by renormalizing
\Sat_{G,x}^{\naive}
is monoidal, and in particular can be viewed as a
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})
-linear functor. It follows that
\Psi_{G,x}
is given by the associative algebra
\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})}(\delta_{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G})
. Lemma
9.2.1
yields a
\check{G}
-equivariant isomorphism

\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})}(\delta_{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G})\tilde{\longrightarrow}\Hom_{\mathsf{Sph}_{G,x}}(\delta_{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G},\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}}\star\delta_{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G}).

Note that this is an isomorphism of associative algebras in
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})
, where the algebra structure on the right side is induced by the algebra structure on
\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}}
and composition of morphisms in
\mathsf{Sph}_{G,x}
.

Then apply Theorem 7.6.1 from
[
ABG04
]
, which supplies an isomorphism of associative algebras

\Hom_{\mathsf{Sph}_{G,x}}(\delta_{1},\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}}\star\delta_{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G})\tilde{\longrightarrow}\Sym(\check{\mathfrak{g}}[-2]).

∎

Next, we show that the monad
\Psi_{\check{G},x}^{\spec}
is abstractly isomorphic to the same associative algebra as
\Psi_{G}
.

Proposition 9.2.3
.

The monad

\Psi_{\check{G},x}^{\spec}:\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Sph}_{\check{G},x}^{\spec}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})

is isomorphic to the monad given by the associative algebra
\Sym(\check{\mathfrak{g}}[-2])
.

Proof.

This is Corollary
8.9.1.1
, applied in the case
\check{P}=\check{G}
.

∎

9.3.
Another reduction step

We claim that if
\Sat_{G,x}
induces an isomorphism

\End_{\mathsf{Sph}_{G,x}}(\delta_{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G})\tilde{\longrightarrow}\End_{\mathsf{Sph}_{\check{G},x}^{\spec}}(\Vac_{\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}}})

on endomorphisms of the unit objects, then it is an equivalence.

We have previously shown that if the morphism of monads (
9.1.1
) is an isomorphism over
x
, then
\Sat_{G,x}
is an equivalence. Propositions
9.2.2
and
9.2.3
show that both of these monads are abstractly isomorphic to the associative algebra
\Sym(\check{\mathfrak{g}}[-2])
in
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})
, so we must prove that the endomorphism

\varphi_{G}:\Sym(\check{\mathfrak{g}}[-2])\longrightarrow\Sym(\check{\mathfrak{g}}[-2])

corresponding to (
9.1.1
) is an isomorphism.

It suffices to show that
\varphi_{G}
is an isomorphism at the level of cohomology. We view the cohomology of the associative DG algebra
\Sym(\check{\mathfrak{g}}[-2])
as a classical graded commutative algebra, so we need only prove that
\varphi_{G}
restricts to an isomorphism on the generators
\check{\mathfrak{g}}
. On the other hand, the subspace

H^{2}(\Sym(\check{\mathfrak{g}}[-2])^{\check{G}})\subset\check{\mathfrak{g}}

generates the adjoint representation, so it is enough to show that
\varphi_{G}
restricts to an isomorphism on the algebra of invariants
\Sym(\check{\mathfrak{g}}[-2])^{\check{G}}
.

From the proof of Proposition
9.2.2
we obtain a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sym(\check{\mathfrak{g}}[-2])^{\check{G}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sym(\check{\mathfrak{g}}[-2])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\End_{\mathsf{Sph}_{G,x}}(\delta_{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})}(\delta_{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

where the left vertical isomorphism is the composition

\Sym(\check{\mathfrak{g}}[-2])^{\check{G}}\tilde{\longrightarrow}\Sym(\mathfrak{g}[-2])^{G}\tilde{\longrightarrow}\CC_{\dR}(\mathbb{B}G)\tilde{\longrightarrow}\End_{\mathsf{Sph}_{G,x}}(\delta_{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G}).

Similarly, Proposition
9.2.3
yields a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sym(\check{\mathfrak{g}}[-2])^{\check{G}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sym(\check{\mathfrak{g}}[-2])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\End_{\mathsf{Sph}_{\check{G},x}^{\spec}}(\Vac_{\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})}(\Vac_{\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}}}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

The claim now follows.

9.4.
Conclusion of the proof

Finally, to prove that
\Sat_{G,x}
induces an isomorphism on endomorphisms of the unit objects, we reduce to the case
G=T
using
\Sat_{G,B}
as an intermediary. Consider the commutative diagram in
\mathsf{FactCat}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Sph}_{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Sph}_{\check{G}}^{\spec}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Sph}_{G,B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Sph}_{\check{G},\check{B}}^{\spec}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Sph}_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Sph}_{\check{T}}^{\spec},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sat_{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sat_{G,B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sat_{T}}

where
\mathsf{Sph}_{G}\to\mathsf{Sph}_{G,B}
is the unique morphism of
\mathsf{Sph}_{G}
-modules in
\mathsf{FactCat}
(uniqueness is a consequence of unitality), and similarly for the other vertical functors.

Taking the fiber at
x
and passing to endomorphisms of the unit objects, we obtain a commutative diagram of associative algebras

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\End_{\mathsf{Sph}_{G,x}}(\delta_{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\End_{\mathsf{Sph}_{\check{G},x}^{\spec}}(\Vac_{\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\End_{\mathsf{Sph}_{G,B,x}}(\Delta^{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\End_{\mathsf{Sph}_{\check{G},\check{B},x}^{\spec}}(\Vac_{\Upsilon(\check{\mathfrak{n}},\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}})})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\End_{\mathsf{Sph}_{T,x}}(\delta_{\mathfrak{L}^{+}T})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\End_{\mathsf{Sph}_{\check{T},x}^{\spec}}(\Vac_{\mathcal{O}_{\check{T}}}).}

Let us describe the algebras in the middle row: first, we have

\Sym(\check{\mathfrak{t}}[-2])\tilde{\longrightarrow}\Sym(\mathfrak{t}^{*}[-2])\tilde{\longrightarrow}\CC_{\dR}(\mathbb{B}T)\tilde{\longrightarrow}\End_{\mathsf{Sph}_{G,B,x}}(\Delta^{0}).

By Corollary
8.9.1.1
, we have an equivalence

\mathsf{Sph}_{\check{G},\check{B},x}^{\spec}\tilde{\longrightarrow}\Sym((\check{\mathfrak{g}}/\check{\mathfrak{n}})[-2])\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{B}))

which sends

\Vac_{\Upsilon(\check{\mathfrak{n}},\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}})}\mapsto\Sym((\check{\mathfrak{g}}/\check{\mathfrak{n}})[-2]).

In particular, we have isomorphisms

\displaystyle\End_{\mathsf{Sph}_{\check{G},\check{B},x}^{\spec}}(\Vac_{\Upsilon(\check{\mathfrak{n}},\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}})})

\displaystyle\tilde{\longrightarrow}\End_{\Sym((\check{\mathfrak{g}}/\check{\mathfrak{n}})[-2])\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{B}))}(\Sym((\check{\mathfrak{g}}/\check{\mathfrak{n}})[-2]))

\displaystyle\tilde{\longrightarrow}\Sym((\check{\mathfrak{g}}/\check{\mathfrak{n}})[-2])^{\check{B}}=\Sym(\check{\mathfrak{t}}[-2]).

To summarize, under these identifications the above commutative diagram of associative algebras becomes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sym(\check{\mathfrak{g}}[-2])^{\check{G}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sym(\check{\mathfrak{g}}[-2])^{\check{G}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sym(\check{\mathfrak{t}}[-2])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sym(\check{\mathfrak{t}}[-2])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sym(\check{\mathfrak{t}}[-2])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sym(\check{\mathfrak{t}}[-2]).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id}

Here the downward vertical arrows are given by the Chevalley homomorphism

\Sym(\check{\mathfrak{g}}[-2])^{\check{G}}\tilde{\longrightarrow}\Sym(\check{\mathfrak{t}}[-2])^{W}\longrightarrow\Sym(\check{\mathfrak{t}}[-2]).

We have previously proved that Theorem
6.6.1
holds in the case
G=T
, which implies that
\varphi_{T}
is an isomorphism. It follows that

\varphi_{G}:\Sym(\check{\mathfrak{g}}[-2])^{\check{G}}\longrightarrow\Sym(\check{\mathfrak{g}}[-2])^{\check{G}}

is injective at the level of cohomology, hence an isomorphism, since
\Sym(\check{\mathfrak{g}}[-2])^{\check{G}}
has finite-dimensional cohomologies.

10.
The equivalence for a proper parabolic

In this section, we deduce Theorem
6.12.3
from Theorem
6.6.1
and the main theorem of
[
Ras16
]
.

10.1.
Recovering spherical objects from Whittaker invariants

Consider the pairing in
\mathsf{FactCat}
defined as the composite

\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\otimes\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)\tilde{\longrightarrow}\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}\otimes\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi},

where the first functor is the equivalence of Theorem
6.5.1
and the second functor is given by convolution. By Proposition
3.4.1
, this corresponds to a morphism

(10.1.1)

\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\otimes\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}.

in
\mathsf{FactCat}_{\laxuntl}
. By construction, this morphism is equivariant for the right action of
\mathfrak{L}G
.

Proposition 10.1.1
.

The morphism (
10.1.1
) factors through the inclusion

\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\otimes\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi,\acc}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\otimes\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi},

and moreover sends the unit
\delta_{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G}
to the image of the regular bimodule
\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}}
under the functor

\displaystyle\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\otimes\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})

\displaystyle\tilde{\longrightarrow}\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\otimes\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\oblv_{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G}}\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\otimes\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi,\acc}.

Proof.

For first assertion, note that (
10.1.1
) can also be obtained from the composite

\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\otimes\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)\xrightarrow{\Av_{!}^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}}\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi},

where the first morphism is the action of
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})
coming from
\Sat_{G}^{\naive}
.

For the second claim, apply the functor of
\mathfrak{L}^{+}G
-invariants to (
10.1.1
) to obtain a morphism

\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\otimes\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}\tilde{\longrightarrow}\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\otimes\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G}).

By construction, this agrees with the morphism of associative algebras (
6.5.1
) and in particular preserves the monoidal unit. The claim follows.

∎

Observe that for a fixed
x\in X(k)
, the category

\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\otimes\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi,\acc}

has a canonical structure of factorization module at
x
for the factorization category

\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\otimes\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}\cong\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\otimes\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G}),

compatible with the functor

\oblv_{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G}:\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\otimes\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\otimes\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi,\acc}.

The previous proposition implies that (
10.1.1
) induces a morphism

\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\otimes\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi,\acc})_{x},

equivariant for the right action of
(\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}
.

If
\mathcal{C}
is a left
\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}
-module, we obtain a functor

\mathcal{C}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G}\tilde{\longrightarrow}\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}\underset{\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}}{\otimes}\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\otimes\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi,\acc})_{x}\underset{\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}}{\otimes}\mathcal{C}.

Note that since the
\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}
-module and factorization
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})
-module structures on the category

\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi,\acc}

commute, the category

\mathcal{C}^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi,\acc}\cong\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi,\acc}\underset{\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}}{\otimes}\mathcal{C}

automatically inherits a factorization
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})
-module structure, and hence we can identify

\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\otimes\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi,\acc})_{x}\underset{\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}}{\otimes}\mathcal{C}\tilde{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\otimes\mathcal{C}^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi,\acc})_{x}.

We will show that the resulting functor

(10.1.2)

\mathcal{C}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\otimes\mathcal{C}^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi,\acc})_{x}.

is “nearly” an equivalence.

10.2.
Tempered geometric Satake equivalence

Given a
\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}
-module category
\mathcal{C}
, we define the
anti-tempered
subcategory of
\mathcal{C}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G}
by

\mathcal{C}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G,\antitemp}:=\ker(\mathcal{C}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G}\xrightarrow{\Av^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}_{!}}\mathcal{C}^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}).

The
tempered
subcategory
\mathcal{C}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G,\temp}
is defined to be the right complement of
\mathcal{C}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G,\antitemp}
. The inclusion

\mathcal{C}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G,\temp}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G}

admits a right adjoint.

Proposition 10.2.1
.

The composite functor

\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}G,\temp}\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}G}=\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})_{x}\xrightarrow{(\ref{presatake})}\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\otimes\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G}))_{x}

is an equivalence.

Proof.

Recall that (
6.6.1
) is t-exact, and that by Theorem
6.6.1
it becomes an equivalence after renormalization. By Corollary 2.3.7 of
[
Ber21
]
, the subcategory

\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}G,\antitemp}\subset\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})_{x}

consists precisely of the infinitely connective objects, i.e. those with vanishing cohomology objects in all degrees. It follows that the functor

\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})_{x}\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}G,\temp}

right adjoint to the inclusion realizes the latter as the left completion of the t-structure on the former. On the other hand, the proof of Proposition
8.9.1.1
shows that we have a t-exact equivalence

\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\otimes\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G}))_{x}\cong\mathsf{QCoh}((\{0\}\underset{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}{\times}\{0\})/\check{G}),

and in particular the t-structure on the source is left complete. Thus

\mathsf{Sph}_{\check{G},x}^{\spec}=\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\otimes\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G}))_{x}^{\ren}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\otimes\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G}))_{x}

realizes the target as the left completion of the source. It follows that the functor in the proposition is obtained by applying left completion to the equivalence of Theorem
6.6.1
, hence is an equivalence.

∎

10.3.

We will need a couple of lemmas.

Lemma 10.3.1
.

The convolution functor

\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}_{x}\underset{\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})_{x}}{\otimes}\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}

is an equivalence onto

\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi,\acc}.

Proof.

This functor is fully faithful because
\mathcal{H}_{G,x}
is ind-proper (see Theorem 3.1.5 of
[
CD21
]
), so it suffices to show that the image generates the target under colimits. Tensor the functor

\Av^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}_{!}:\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})_{x}\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}_{x}

with
\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}
over
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})_{x}
to obtain a functor

\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}_{x}\underset{\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})_{x}}{\otimes}\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}.

Its composition with the functor in question identifies with

\Av^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}_{!}:\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi},

whose image generates the accessible subcategory by definition.

∎

Lemma 10.3.2
.

The functor

\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G,\temp}\underset{\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})_{x}}{\otimes}\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G}\underset{\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})_{x}}{\otimes}\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}=\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G}

is an equivalence onto
\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G,\temp}
.

Proof.

Recall that the inclusion

\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G,\temp}\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G}

admits a right adjoint, which is automatically
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})_{x}
-linear because
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})_{x}
is compactly generated by left and right dualizable objects. It follows that the functor in question is fully faithful.

To see that the image of this functor is
\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G,\temp}
, it suffices to show that that the kernel of the right adjoint

\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G}\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G,\temp}\underset{\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})_{x}}{\otimes}\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}

is equal to
\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G,\antitemp}
. This follows from the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G}\underset{\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})_{x}}{\otimes}\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}\underset{\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})_{x}}{\otimes}\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi,\acc}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Av_{!}^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}\otimes\id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Av_{!}^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

∎

Restrict (
10.1.2
) to the tempered subcategory to obtain a functor

(10.3.1)

\mathcal{C}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G,\temp}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\otimes\mathcal{C}^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi,\acc})_{x}.

Proposition 10.3.3
.

The functor (
10.3.1
) is an equivalence for any
\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}
-module category
\mathcal{C}
.

Proof.

First, we can immediately reduce to the universal case
\mathcal{C}=\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}
. Namely, we have

\mathcal{C}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G,\temp}\cong\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G,\temp}\underset{\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}}{\otimes}\mathcal{C},

and the functor in question is given by tensoring

(10.3.2)

\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G,\temp}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\otimes\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi,\acc})_{x}

with
\mathcal{C}
over
\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}
.

Next, we reduce to the case
\mathcal{C}=\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})_{x}
. First, observe that

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\otimes

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi})_{x}\underset{\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})_{x}}{\otimes}\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}

\displaystyle\tilde{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\otimes\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}\underset{\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})}{\otimes}\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G))_{x}

\displaystyle\tilde{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\otimes\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi,\acc})_{x},

where the first equivalence is because the right
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})_{x}
-module and factorization
\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})
-module structures on

\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}_{x}

commute, and the second equivalence uses Lemma
10.3.1
. On the other hand, we have

\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G,\temp}\underset{\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})_{x}}{\otimes}\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}\tilde{\longrightarrow}\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G,\temp}

by the previous lemma. Thus if we can establish that (
10.3.1
) is an equivalence for
\mathcal{C}=\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})_{x}
, then the case
\mathcal{C}=\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}
will follow by tensoring with
\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{x}
over
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{H}_{G})_{x}
.

To see that

\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G,\temp}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\otimes\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi,\acc})_{x}

is an equivalence, observe that

\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi,\acc}=\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi,\acc}\tilde{\longrightarrow}\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})

by Theorem
6.5.1
. The resulting functor

\mathsf{D}(\Gr_{G})_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G,\temp}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\otimes\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G}))_{x}

is an equivalence by Proposition
10.2.1
.

∎

10.4.
Proof of Theorem
6.12.3

As shown in §
6.13
, it is enough to prove that
\Sat_{G,P,x}
is an equivalence for a fixed
x\in X(k)
.

Apply Proposition
10.3.3
in the case
\mathcal{C}=(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})_{x}
, which yields the equivalence

(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G,\temp}\tilde{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\otimes(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi,\acc})_{x}.

Theorem
6.8.1
supplies an isomorphism

(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi,\acc}\tilde{\longrightarrow}\Upsilon(\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{\check{P}})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{0}^{\fact}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M}))

in
\mathsf{FactCat}
, and the commutative triangle in the proof of Theorem
6.12.1
yields a commutative triangle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi,\acc}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Upsilon(\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{\check{P}})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{0}^{\fact}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M})).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Upsilon(\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{\check{P}},-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

Here the left diagonal morphism is the composite

\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}^{+}G\backslash\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M}\xrightarrow{\Av_{!}^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}}(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi,\acc},

where the first morphism is given by the acting on the unit via
\Sat_{G}^{\naive}
. We therefore obtain an equivalence

(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G,\temp}\tilde{\longrightarrow}\Upsilon(\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{\check{P}},\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\otimes\Upsilon(\check{\mathfrak{n}})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{0}^{\fact}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M})))_{x},

and by definition the latter category is equivalent to

\Upsilon(\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{\check{P}},\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{0}^{\fact}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\otimes\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M}))_{x}.

Equip the category

(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G,\temp}

with the t-structure characterized by the requirement that the functor

(10.4.1)

(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G}\longrightarrow(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G,\temp}

right adjoint to the inclusion is left t-exact. In fact, this functor is t-exact: its kernel

(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G,\antitemp}

equals the kernel of
\Av^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}_{!}
by definition, and by Proposition
6.11.1
the latter consists of infinitely connective objects, i.e. those with vanishing cohomology objects in all degrees. In particular this kernel is stable under truncation functors, which implies that (
10.4.1
) is t-exact as claimed.

Combining the above equivalences, we obtain

(10.4.2)

(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G,\temp}\tilde{\longrightarrow}\Upsilon(\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{\check{P}},\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{0}^{\fact}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\otimes\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M}))_{x}.

Tracing through the constructions, we see that this is the restriction of (
6.12.2
) to the tempered subcategory. We claim that the triangle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G,\temp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Upsilon(\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{\check{P}},\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{0}^{\fact}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\otimes\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M}))_{x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\ref{tempquotps})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\ref{psfunc1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\ref{temppsequiv})}

commutes, which amounts to the assertion that (
6.12.2
) vanishes on

(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G,\antitemp}.

As noted above, this subcategory consists of infinite connective objects, and the t-structure on

\Upsilon(\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{\check{P}},\mathcal{O}_{\check{G}})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}_{0}^{\fact}(\mathsf{Rep}(\check{G})\otimes\mathsf{Rep}(\check{M}))_{x}\cong\mathsf{QCoh}((\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{\check{P}}\underset{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}{\times}\{0\})/\check{P})

is left complete, so the claim follows.

It therefore suffices to show that (
10.4.1
) restricts to an equivalence on eventually coconnective objects, or equivalently on coconnective objects. The restriction of this functor to coconnective objects admits the left adjoint

((\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G,\temp})^{\geq 0}\longrightarrow(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G}\xrightarrow{\tau^{\geq 0}}((\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G})^{\geq 0},

which is fully faithful because (
10.4.1
) is t-exact. So it is enough to show that (
10.4.1
) is conservative on eventually coconnective objects. But

\Av^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}_{!}:(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}^{+}G}\longrightarrow(\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{L}G)_{\mathfrak{L}N_{P}\mathfrak{L}^{+}M})_{x}^{\mathfrak{L}N^{-},\psi}

factors through (
10.4.1
), and the former is conservative on eventually coconnective objects by Proposition
6.11.1
.
